VOICE_CLONE_GUIDE.md

Voice Clone: What I Need From You, Step by Step

The course has 34 videos and about 44,000 spoken words (about 5.6 hours). Your cloned voice reads every script. Here is the whole process. Your part takes about 30 minutes today.

The Plan (Adi 13 November: record a clip, send it in the chat; compare open source with ElevenLabs)

  1. Adi records the consent line and the 3 minute script from this page: https://claude.ai/artifact/3boHpnnwT1fP9wF2Gehhrj and sends the clip in the chat.
  2. Open-source clone first (VOICE_OPTIONS.md): VoxCPM2 (Apache 2.0, free for commercial use) with Qwen3-TTS 1.7B as the fallback. A 10 to 20 second reference cut from the clip, one sentence or short paragraph at a time, the same reference and seed for every chunk. It needs a GPU: a rented RTX 4090 (about $1 to $3 for the whole course) or Adi's Mac overnight. This cloud session has no GPU.
  3. A/B test: V01 and V10 voiced by VoxCPM2 and by an ElevenLabs instant clone, sent to Adi. He picks by ear.
  4. All 34 videos voiced with the winner; tools/mix_audio.sh mixes each video's voice with an optional music bed (bed at -24 LUFS, ducked under the voice, final -16 LUFS). Music: licence-safe options in VOICE_OPTIONS.md section 5.

The ElevenLabs route below stays as the fallback and the A/B comparison.

Why You Upload It Yourself

The voice provider (ElevenLabs is my pick: it has the most natural long-form narration and an API I can run the scripts through) only lets the owner of a voice clone it, and it checks that by asking you to read a short line in the app. So the sample goes straight into your own account, not to me. That also keeps your voice files out of our repos and chats.

Step 1: Record the Sample (15 minutes)

  1. Use the same microphone you use for the webinar. Sit 15 to 20 cm from it, in a quiet room with soft furnishings (no echo, no fan, no music).
  2. Open VOICE_SAMPLE_SCRIPT.md (in this card). Read it once out loud to warm up, then record it in one take. It is about 3 minutes.
  3. Speak the way you speak on the webinar: same energy, same pace. Do not perform. Keep natural pauses.
  4. Do not clean it up: no noise removal, no music, no compression. Export as WAV, or MP3 at 192 kbps or higher.
  5. Name it adi-voice-sample-01.wav.

Step 2: Create the Clone (10 minutes)

  1. Sign in or sign up at https://elevenlabs.io with the Tantra Google account (use the Tantra Brave profile).
  2. Pick a paid plan. The Creator plan covers the instant clone test. All 34 videos need roughly 250,000 characters, so check the plan's monthly credits on the pricing page before you start. One month on a larger plan is usually cheaper than two months on a small one.
  3. Go to Voices, then Create a voice, then Instant Voice Clone.
  4. Name it "Adi (Tantra Academy)". Upload adi-voice-sample-01.wav. Tick the box confirming it is your voice and you consent to cloning it. Save.
  5. Copy the Voice ID from the voice's settings. It is not a secret: send it to me in chat.

Step 3: Give Me Access to Generate (2 minutes)

When I ask for it, a secure form appears in this session for an ElevenLabs API key (ElevenLabs: Profile, then API Keys, then Create, with Text to Speech access only). You paste the key into the form, never into the chat. I never see it: the session's network adds it to my requests.

Step 4: I Generate and You Listen (I do this)

  1. I generate V01 and V10 first (the welcome and the hardest setup video) and send them to you.
  2. You listen to both. If the voice is right, I generate all 34. If it sounds off (flat, wrong accent, odd pauses), we go to Step 5.

Step 5 (only if needed): The Professional Clone

The instant clone works from minutes of audio. The professional clone trains on at least 30 minutes and sounds closer to you over long videos. 1. Record yourself reading scripts V01 to V04 aloud, about 30 minutes, with the same set-up as Step 1. That gives the clone your real teaching voice, and it also gives us real-voice versions of those 4 videos. 2. ElevenLabs: Voices, Create a voice, Professional Voice Clone. Upload the files and complete the voice check it asks for. Training takes a few hours. 3. Send me the new Voice ID and I regenerate.

Your Consent, on Record

Reply in chat with this line (edit it if you want): "I, Adithya Murali, consent to Tantra using an AI clone of my voice to narrate the Tantra Academy course videos." I add it to card 74 with the date.

How I Generate (for the team)

  • python3 tools/voice_text.py writes the narration text per video, with the slide and screen cues removed, in parts of at most 2,400 characters (site/voice-text/).
  • python3 tools/voice_generate.py --voice-id <id> --only V01,V10 voices those videos into site/voice-mp3/. Leave out --only for all 34. --dry-run shows what would be sent and the character count.
  • The ElevenLabs key never sits in a file: in a cloud session the network adds it; on a Mac, put it in ~/.config/tantra/keys/elevenlabs.env as ELEVENLABS_API_KEY=....
VOICE_SAMPLE_SCRIPT.md

Voice Sample Script (read aloud, about 3 minutes)

Read it in one take, in your webinar voice. The lines mix short and long sentences, numbers, questions and product names, so the clone learns how you say all of them.

Hi, I'm Adi, and welcome to the Tantra Academy.

Most funnels don't fail because the idea is bad. They fail in the setup. Somebody spends three weeks picking tools, never sends a single invite, and calls it a failed experiment. I've watched that happen more times than I can count. So this course does it the other way around.

In the next thirty days, you're going to build the same system our expert team builds for clients. Day one, your offer, your buyer and your list. Day two, your pages, your payments and your follow-ups, all tested with two fake leads before a real person ever sees them. By day seven, you have traffic coming in from LinkedIn, calendar invites, WhatsApp and content.

Does that sound like a lot? It is. That's why every step is a screen recording. You watch me click, then you click. If something doesn't match your screen, stop the video and ask in the group.

Here's the one number I want you to remember. Customers equal sign-ups, times show-up rate, times booking rate, times close rate. Four numbers. When revenue is low, one of them is the reason, and our job every week is to find it and fix only that one.

Let me give you an example. Say a thousand people see your invite. A hundred and twenty click. Fifty-four sign up, thirty show up, three book a call, and two buy. Which step is broken? Not the traffic. Not the page. It's the booking rate. So we change the offer slide, or the booking page, and nothing else, and we measure again next week.

You'll use four tools. The Funnel OS, where you plan and build everything by chat. Tantra, for outreach on email, LinkedIn and WhatsApp. The Tantra CRM, where every lead, booking and payment lands. And the Content OS, which writes and publishes your posts.

One more thing. Some steps run on their own clock. New mailboxes need at least fourteen days to warm up. Payment accounts take a few business days to verify. So we start those today, on day zero, before anything else.

Right. Open video two, and let's start the clocks.

scripts/SCRIPT_GUIDE.md

How Every Course Script Is Written

Read this fully before writing a script. One file per video: scripts/V<NN>-<slug>.md.

Format (the slide builder parses it, keep it exact)

# V07: Pick Your Funnel and Write Your Event Line
- Module: M1 Decide
- Day: Day 1
- Length: about 8 minutes (about 1,000 to 1,150 spoken words)
- Systems on screen: Funnel OS
- Before you start: what must already be done
- Done when: one line, checkable

## Slides
### Slide 1: Short Heading in Title Case
- pointer, 3 to 8 words
- pointer
### Slide 2: ...

## Voice script
Plain paragraphs, read aloud by the cloned voice.
[SLIDE 1]
[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "..." > the reply shows ...]

## Screen recording shot list
1. Where to start, what to click, what must be visible, roughly how long

## Build Score checklist
- [ ] item (N points)

Rules for the format: - 4 to 8 slides per video. 2 to 4 pointers per slide. Pointers are fragments, not sentences. No full stops at the end of pointers. - Voice script length: about 130 spoken words per minute of the stated length. - Put [SLIDE n] on its own line where the slide appears, and [SCREEN: ...] on its own line where the screen recording takes over. Most of the video is screen: slides are only for the main points. - The Build Score points of all 34 videos add up to 100. Your batch's share is in the brief you were given.

Voice (this is Adi talking)

  • First person "I" (Adi) and "you". Plain, direct, warm. Like a founder showing a friend his screen.
  • Open on a specific pain the member recognises in the first two sentences, then say what this video gets them.
  • Short sentences. Contractions are fine ("you'll", "it's").
  • Written for a cloned voice: no markdown, no bullet points, no brackets other than the cue lines, no URLs read out letter by letter (say "usetantra dot com" once, otherwise "in the Tantra app"). Write numbers the way people say them only when a digit string would read oddly.
  • End every video with what to do next, in one or two sentences, and the next video's name.

Hard rules (a script that breaks one is rejected)

  1. Our team, never "my team". Who builds, coaches or supports is "our expert team" or "the Tantra team". Adi says "I" only about his own view or story.

  2. No em dashes or en dashes. Use a full stop, comma, colon or "to".

  3. Banned words: leverage, unlock, seamless, game-changer, supercharge, delve, robust, elevate, end to end, patchwork, engineered system, one dashboard, revolutionary, skyrocket, effortless, guaranteed results, exclusive, proven, secret, don't miss, "in today's fast-paced world", "let's dive in", "buckle up". No claim that there is no offer (the three phrases banned in deliverables/_shared/PROOF_PACK.md section 5).
  4. Funnel OS is the Funnel OS. It is a chat app. Never name the AI model, vendor or tool underneath it. Do not mention any AI coding tool by name.
  5. Approved proof only. Allowed: 400+ brands, 10+ years, 21+ countries, 47+ industries. Growth Idea (Shweta's UK coaching group): 11 webinars May 2025 to Feb 2026, 765,105 reached, 6,517 registered, 1,384 live, 80 sales calls, 4 clients; best webinar 28 Aug 2025: 99,859 reached (98,000 by calendar invite), 1,990 registered, 321 live, 25 calls. FintechCircle (Susanne, London): outreach grew 10,000 to 20,046 to 30,000 invites; episode 4: 38,138 invites, 187 Yes and 221 Maybe RSVPs. Each client named at most once per video. Nothing else: no other client, no invented result, no member result.
  6. Teaching-model numbers are labelled. The reference rates below are a teaching model, never a benchmark and never a result. Say "in our teaching model" every time you use one. Better: tell members to compare against their own last week.
  7. Never promise: inbox placement, a revenue figure, "instantly". Say "when a reply is detected", not "instantly".
  8. Public words: "calendar invites" (not "cold calendar"). The app is at usetantra.com (never app.usetantra.com).
  9. Never name these vendors: Instantly, HeyReach, Apollo, the old booking tool scan.py blocks, GoDaddy, Microsoft 365, SendGrid, Zoom, GoHighLevel, HubSpot, Metricool, Cal.com.
  10. Never show or read a key, password or real contact on screen. Shot lists must say "blur" wherever a key, email list or phone number would appear.

The stack (what is on screen)

System What it does in the course
Funnel OS (chat app) Where the member works. Holds the Funnel Brief (offer, method, buyer, list, funnel, channels, proof), writes every page and message, builds and deploys the pages to the member's Cloudflare, and runs the other systems from chat through their connections: it can draft Tantra campaigns, create CRM pipelines, booking pages, sequences and tracked links, create payment products and webhooks, and pull the numbers for the weekly review. It never sends, launches, buys or deletes without the member typing an approval in their own words.
Tantra app (usetantra.com) The outreach engine: cold email from warmed mailboxes, LinkedIn (connected by signing in inside Tantra), WhatsApp (scan a QR), calendar invite campaigns from Google Workspace senders, omnichannel sequences, the reply inbox. API key from Connectors > API Keys. Reply and RSVP events go to the CRM by webhook.
Tantra CRM (crm.usetantra.com) Pipelines and stages, deal and contact fields, booking pages with qualifying questions and round robin hosts, sequences with email, WhatsApp, SMS, AI call, task and Slack steps, tracked short links, the tracking snippet, payment connections, Revenue and Acquisition dashboards, dry run mode for testing.
Content OS (content.usetantra.com) The content engine: context bucket, formats and cadence, a batch written and imaged, approval on a phone, publishing to LinkedIn, X, Instagram, YouTube, TikTok and company pages through connected publishing accounts.
Payments Dodo Payments (merchant of record, adds local tax), Razorpay (India; KYC and international payments approval first), Stripe (restricted key, never the secret key). Test mode first. A sale counts only when the signed webhook arrives, never from a thank-you page visit.
Cloudflare Domains, DNS and hosting for the pages. Funnel OS works through an API token the member creates.

Facts to teach (from the playbooks; use exactly)

  • Six stages, one KPI each: Traffic (visit rate), Landing (sign-up rate), Nurture (show-up rate), Conversion (booking rate), Revenue (close rate), Long-term nurture (rebooking rate). Customers = sign-ups x show-up rate x booking rate x close rate.
  • Value score: dream outcome x likelihood, divided by time delay x effort. Fix the two weakest levers.
  • Offer sentence: "I help [who] go from [before] to [after] using [method], in [time], or [promise]."
  • List sizing: buyers needed = revenue target / price; then divide by close rate, booking rate, show-up rate and sign-up rate. Use your own rates once you have them.
  • Eight funnels: Webinar, Workshop, Reverse Squeeze Page (video), Book a Call, Challenge, Community, Social Following, Product Launch. Not sure: pick the webinar.
  • Event line: title under 70 characters, no $, % or "free", date at least 2 weeks out (for cold traffic), the buyer's time zone.
  • Domains and email: 2 or 3 outreach domains, never the main domain, 2 or 3 mailboxes each. Exactly one SPF record. DMARC p=none. DKIM 2048 bit from Google Admin. Warm up at least 14 days: 20 a day while warming, 50 a day per mailbox once warm. Grow by adding mailboxes, not by pushing one harder.
  • Mailboxes to Tantra: a Google Cloud project, Gmail and Calendar APIs on, a service account with a JSON key uploaded in Tantra (Connectors > Google Service Accounts), domain wide delegation in Google Admin using the numeric Client ID (not the service account email) and the scopes Tantra lists. Workspaces created after 3 May 2024 must allow service account keys first. Delegation can take up to an hour. Then verify each inbox and set it to Cold.
  • Calendar invites: a separate domain and Google Workspace, one user per sender, end the trial, 20 invites a day per sender until day 60, then 50, never above 200. Meeting style title, the time zone in line one, no merge fields, send 8 to 12 days before the event. Never build a second campaign because counters read zero.
  • LinkedIn: connect inside Tantra. 15 to 20 requests a day, at most 200 a week. Note under 300 characters. Messages on days 0, 3, 7, 12 and 25 after acceptance. Withdraw unanswered requests after 14 days. Never run a second automation tool on the same profile.
  • WhatsApp: opted-in contacts only, STOP in every message, one link per message. Templates need Meta approval. Marketing templates do not deliver to US numbers, so US contacts get a Utility message.
  • Cold email: 5 steps on days 0, 3, 6, 9, 13. Subject only on step 1. A/B pairs: A benefit led, B problem led. Pause a mailbox if bounces go above 5%.
  • Landing page, nine blocks: trust strip, headline, short muted video, 3 proof points, form (above the fold), method, testimonials, FAQ, sticky button. Test at 375, 768 and 1280 pixels wide. One unticked consent box per channel.
  • Thank-you page: confirm, add to calendar, WhatsApp or community, what happens next, a second door to book or buy now.
  • Reminders: 7 days, 24 hours (08:00 local), 1 hour, 15 minutes, live. One link per message.
  • Webinar clock (60 min): open 0 to 8, credibility 8 to 10, problem 10 to 16, old vs new 16 to 19, method 19 to 34, proof 34 to 39, offer 39 to 43, objections 43 to 44, call to action 44 to 46, Q and A 46 to 60. Four polls.
  • Sales clocks: enquiry called within 5 minutes; form filled with no booking reviewed within 12 hours; no-show messaged within 5 minutes; proposal followed up at 48 hours and 7 days; paid kickoff within 3 to 7 days; every lost deal gets a reason. Under about $1,000 send people to checkout; above it, book a call.
  • AI call step: consent per number, says it is automated, under 30 seconds, inside the send window, never retried.
  • Pipeline stages (default): Form Filled, Call Scheduled, No Show, Qualified, Unqualified, Call Held, Follow-Up, Proposal Sent, Proposal Accepted, Payment Completed, Onboarding. Event funnels add tags Registered, Attended, No-Show, Replay Watched. "Tools report events, events move deals. If you moved a deal by hand, that step is not wired."
  • UTMs: source = channel, medium = outreach, paid or organic, campaign = the event, content = the A or B version.
  • Test run: two test contacts, one attends and one misses, through every automation, a test booking, a test payment and a refund. Write the automation register: each automation is Live and proven, Failing, Built with one step left, or Not built. Nothing launches while any line says Failing.
  • Weekly loop: observe, find the worst step, one change, split the list 50/50 with separate utm_content, measure, keep or revert. Daily: answer every reply the same day with the booking link (15 minutes). Weekly: the channel check and one experiment. Monthly: the five line scale plan (cost per buyer vs lifetime value, cheapest channel, worst step, next offer up, first-win date for buyers).
  • Worst step: compute the six rates; compare each to your own last run; the lowest ratio wins, a tie goes to the earlier step. The fixes: sends but few visits, change the message or the list; visits but few sign-ups, change the headline; low show-up, change a reminder; few offer clicks, change the offer slide; clicks but few calls, change the booking page or the follow-up; one channel costs far more per buyer, move the budget.
  • Experiment size: you need a few hundred people per half at minimum; decide only when the gap between A and B is bigger than twice the square root of (A + B) conversions.
  • Channel check thresholds (starting points, not benchmarks): fix when bounces are over 5% or click through is under 1%; scale when click through is at least 3%, conversion at least 1.5% and bounces at most 2%.
  • Ads: target cost per sign-up = the most you would pay per buyer x show-up rate x close rate. Build ads paused. Pause at 3 times the target with no sign-ups.
  • Teaching model rates (label them every time): webinar sign-up to show-up 29.87%, booking rate 7.53%, close rate 17.22%; visit rate per person reached: cold email 1.06%, calendar invites 0.17%, LinkedIn 0.71%.
  • Long-term nurture: welcome lessons, a weekly letter with a WhatsApp version an hour later (under 400 characters), remove after 20 unopened emails, a nine word reactivation email after 90 quiet days, the next event invite to everyone who did not buy.

Deeper sources if a step needs more detail: deliverables/33-bonus-books/content/playbook/ (current playbook), context/from-tantra-content/04-workshop-curriculum.md (onboarding centre cards), context/from-tantra-content/05-funnel-systems.md (equations), context/from-tantra-content/sources/revops-os-knowledge/ (channels, integrations, operate.md). Those sources sometimes name older tools (Cal.com, GoHighLevel, Metricool, Zoom): always translate to the stack above.

scripts/V01-welcome-how-the-30-days-work.md

V01: Welcome: How the 30 Days Work

  • Module: M0 Start
  • Day: Day 0
  • Length: about 7 minutes (about 850 to 950 spoken words)
  • Systems on screen: Academy, Funnel OS
  • Before you start: you can sign in to the Tantra app and see the Academy
  • Done when: you are in the WhatsApp group, you have posted your intro, and your Day 1 and Day 2 build hours are in your calendar

Slides

Slide 1: Welcome to the 100K MRR Club

  • One funnel, built and tested in two days
  • Traffic from Day 3
  • Weeks 2 to 4: optimise to closed revenue

Slide 2: What You Get

  • Funnel OS and Content OS
  • Tantra Pro for outreach
  • A weekly group coaching call
  • Your own 15 minute slot each week
  • 6 months of access

Slide 3: The 30-Day Map

  • Day 0: start the clocks
  • Day 1 and 2: build and test
  • Days 3 to 7: traffic
  • Weeks 2 to 4: one fix a day

Slide 4: Two Scores

  • Build Score: out of 100, weeks 1 and 2
  • Funnel Score: six rates, weeks 2 to 4
  • Compared with your own last week only

Slide 5: How to Ask for Help

  • Video number and step
  • What you expected, what happened
  • A screenshot with keys blurred
  • Answered the same day

Voice script

[SLIDE 1] You've probably bought a course before, watched the first three videos, and then life happened. The tools sat half set up, and the funnel never got a single visitor. This club is built so that doesn't happen to you. In the next seven minutes I'll show you exactly how the thirty days work, what you'll have at the end, and how to get help the moment you're stuck.

I'm Adi. Together with our expert team, I've spent more than ten years building outreach and funnels for over four hundred brands in more than twenty one countries. What you're about to do is the same build we run for clients, in the same order, with the same checks.

Here's the promise in plain words. In two days you build one funnel and test every step of it. From Day 3 you send traffic to it. And for the rest of the month you improve it a little every day, until people are booking calls and paying you.

[SLIDE 2] Let me show you what you've joined.

[SCREEN: Tantra app > Academy > the 100K MRR Club course page with the 34 videos listed in modules]

This is the Academy, inside the Tantra app at usetantra dot com. Every video lives here, in order, grouped into seven modules. Under each video you'll see a short checklist called Done when. Tick the items as you finish them.

The club gives you four systems. First, Funnel OS. It's a chat app, and it's where you'll do most of the work. You tell it about your business once, and it writes your pages and messages, deploys your pages, and runs the other systems for you, but only when you approve.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS > a project open with the chat on the left and the Funnel Brief on the right]

Second, the Tantra app, which is your outreach engine. Cold email, LinkedIn, WhatsApp and calendar invites, all with one reply inbox. Your Tantra Pro plan covers that. Third, the Tantra CRM, where every lead, booking and payment lands. And fourth, the Content OS, which writes and publishes your posts on the channels you choose.

Around the software you get people. A weekly group coaching call, an hour long, where each member brings their numbers and their worst step. Inside that hour there are four fifteen minute slots, and you book one in the Tantra CRM whenever you want the call to focus on your build. The invite for the call and for your slot use the same Meet link, so you always know where to go. And you have six months of access, so you're not on your own after day thirty.

[SLIDE 3] Now the map. There are four parts.

Day 0 is today. Some things take time no software can shorten. New mailboxes need at least fourteen days to warm up. Payment accounts take a few days to verify. So today you start every one of those clocks. The next video shows you how.

Day 1 and Day 2 are build days. You make your decisions in Funnel OS, connect your mailboxes, set up the CRM and payments, build your pages and automations, and then run a full test with two fake leads. Nothing goes live until that test passes.

Days 3 to 7 are traffic. LinkedIn, calendar invites, WhatsApp to people who opted in, your content and your own warm list all start here. Cold email waits until your mailboxes have warmed for fourteen days. That's honest, and it protects your domains.

Weeks 2 to 4 are where the money is made. Every day you answer replies and make one small change. Every week you run one experiment on the step that's leaking most. By the end of the month you're working on closed revenue, not on setup.

[SCREEN: Academy > the Day 0 kickoff call card and the WhatsApp group link]

Before you go further, join the WhatsApp group from this card. It's where you post your progress, ask questions and see what others are building. Then put two blocks in your calendar, five to six hours each, for Day 1 and Day 2. Those are the build days, and they go much faster when they're protected.

[SLIDE 4] You'll see two scores. The first is your Build Score, out of one hundred. Every video ends with a short checklist, and each item you finish adds points. A member at one hundred has a funnel that's built, tested and live. At six in the evening on Day 1 and Day 2, a coach looks at everyone's Build Score, and if you're below sixty by the end of Day 2, expect a message from us. That's not a telling off. It's us finding where you're stuck.

The second is your Funnel Score, from week 2. It's the six rates from your dashboard: visits, sign-ups, show-ups, bookings, closes and rebookings. Each one is shown against your own previous week, never against anybody else's business. The weekly call starts with your lowest one.

[SCREEN: Academy > your member page showing Build Score and Funnel Score panels]

Both scores sit on your member page here, and the same numbers sit in the CRM, so you, your coach and your weekly report all read the same thing.

[SLIDE 5] Last thing: how to ask for help so you get a useful answer fast. Post in the WhatsApp group with four things. The video number and the step. What you expected to happen. What actually happened. And a screenshot. Before you send a screenshot, blur any key, password, email list or phone number. A coach answers the same day.

And if you're stuck on wording or a decision, ask Funnel OS first. It knows your brief, and it'll often get you moving in a minute.

So here's what to do now. Join the WhatsApp group, post a two line intro with what you sell and who you sell to, and block your build hours. Then open the next video, Start Every Clock Today.

Screen recording shot list

  1. Tantra app, signed in, Academy open on the 100K MRR Club course page. Scroll slowly through the seven modules so all 34 titles are visible. About 20 seconds.
  2. Open V01 and show the Done when checklist under the video. Tick one item to show how it works, then untick it. About 10 seconds.

scripts/V02-start-every-clock-today.md

V02: Start Every Clock Today

  • Module: M0 Start
  • Day: Day 0
  • Length: about 10 minutes (about 1,250 to 1,350 spoken words)
  • Systems on screen: Cloudflare, Google Workspace, Dodo, Twilio
  • Before you start: V01 watched, a card you can pay with, about 90 minutes free today
  • Done when: your domains are bought, both Google Workspaces exist, your payment account is applied for, and your cash list for the week is written

Slides

Slide 1: Four Clocks Software Cannot Speed Up

  • Mailbox warm-up: 14 days minimum
  • Calendar senders: slow ramp to day 60
  • Payment verification: 1 to 5 days
  • WhatsApp templates: hours to days

Slide 2: What Runs While You Wait

  • Days 3 to 13: LinkedIn and calendar invites
  • WhatsApp to opted-in contacts
  • Content and your own warm list
  • Cold email from day 14

Slide 3: Domains Today

  • 2 or 3 outreach domains
  • Never your main domain
  • One separate calendar invite domain
  • Click the registry email within 15 days

Slide 4: Accounts to Open Today

  • Google Workspace for outreach domains
  • Second Workspace for calendar senders
  • Payment provider, test mode first
  • Twilio, upgraded and funded

Slide 5: Your Cash This Week

  • Domains, paid yearly
  • Workspace mailboxes, paid monthly
  • Twilio credit, at least $20
  • Tantra Pro and extra sending accounts

Voice script

[SLIDE 1] Here's the mistake that costs people a month. They build a beautiful funnel in two days, press go on Day 3, and then find out their new mailboxes can't send cold email for two more weeks and their payment account is still under review. The funnel is ready and the plumbing isn't. This video stops that from happening to you. By the end of it, every slow clock in your business will be running, and you'll know exactly what you need to pay for this week.

There are four clocks no software can speed up, including ours.

The first is mailbox warm-up. A brand new mailbox is like a brand new phone number. Nobody trusts it yet. It needs at least fourteen days of gentle sending before it can do cold email at a real volume. While it warms, it sends about twenty a day. Once warm, about fifty a day per mailbox. The clock starts when your mailboxes are connected in Tantra, which you'll do on Day 1, but you can't connect mailboxes you haven't bought. So the domains and Workspace happen today.

The second is calendar invite senders. These are separate Google Workspace users that send meeting style invites. Each one sends twenty a day until it's sixty days old, then fifty. Starting today means the ramp starts today.

The third is payment verification. Dodo Payments usually takes one to three business days. Razorpay needs KYC, and if you sell abroad, international payments approval too, which takes longer. Stripe varies. You'll build and test everything in test mode meanwhile, so nothing waits on it, but the application goes in today.

The fourth is WhatsApp templates. Messages you send through the WhatsApp business system need Meta to approve the template first. That takes hours to days.

[SLIDE 2] Let me be straight about what this means. Cold email does not start on Day 3. It starts on day fourteen, once warm-up is done. Days 3 to 13 run on LinkedIn, calendar invites, WhatsApp to people who opted in, your content and your own warm list. That's plenty to fill a first event, and it means your sending domains get a fair start instead of being burned in week one.

[SLIDE 3] Let's buy the domains.

[SCREEN: Cloudflare dashboard > Domain Registration > Register Domains > search box]

I'm in Cloudflare, under Domain Registration. You need two or three outreach domains. Never send cold email from your main domain. If something goes wrong with an outreach domain, you lose that domain, not your company's email. Pick names that look like yours. If your business is called Northfield, try get northfield, or northfield hq. Short, easy to say, no hyphens.

[SCREEN: Cloudflare > search results > add two outreach domains and one calendar domain to cart > checkout]

Then buy one more domain just for calendar invites. Keep it separate from the outreach domains, so each kind of sending builds its own reputation.

Before you check out, Cloudflare asks for your registrant contact. Use your real details. That's a registry rule. A day or so later you'll get an email from the registry asking you to confirm. Click it within fifteen days, or the domains get suspended. Put a reminder in your calendar now.

One more thing while you're in Cloudflare. Don't touch the DNS records yet. You'll set up every record for these domains in one go in module two, with Funnel OS doing the typing. Today is only about owning the names.

[SCREEN: Google Workspace sign-up > enter the first outreach domain > choose plan]

Next, Google Workspace. Sign up with your first outreach domain. Then add the other outreach domains to the same Workspace as secondary domains, and create two or three mailboxes on each. Use real looking names, like your first name, or a colleague's who'll answer the replies. You'll set up the DNS records and connect these to Tantra in module two, so don't worry about any of that today.

[SCREEN: a second Google Workspace sign-up using the calendar domain > create sender users > Billing > end trial]

Now a second, separate Workspace on your calendar domain. Create one user for each calendar sender you plan to run. Then go to billing and end the free trial. Trial accounts are limited in how much they can send, so ending the trial today is part of starting the clock.

Why two separate Workspaces? Because calendar invites and cold email behave differently, and you don't want a problem with one to hurt the other. If an outreach domain gets a bad week, your calendar senders carry on. If a calendar sender gets flagged, your outreach mailboxes don't notice. Separate domains, separate Workspaces, separate reputations.

[SLIDE 4] Next, payments.

[SCREEN: Dodo Payments sign-up > business details > verification form]

If you sell worldwide and want tax handled for you, Dodo Payments is the simple choice. It's the merchant of record, so it adds local tax for you. Fill in the business details and submit verification now. If you're in India and selling mostly in India, Razorpay works well, but start the KYC today, and if you take payment from abroad, apply for international payments in the same sitting. If you already use Stripe, keep it. In every case, your first sales will be test sales, and you'll connect the provider to Funnel OS in a later video.

[SCREEN: Twilio console > Upgrade > add funds > Phone Numbers]

Then Twilio, for text messages. A trial Twilio account can only text numbers you've verified yourself, so upgrade it and add at least twenty dollars of credit. If your buyers are in the US or Canada, you'll need a toll-free number with verification, and that takes three to five business days, so request it today. Elsewhere, Twilio may ask for a regulatory bundle first.

Text messages are for reminders close to the event, like the one hour and fifteen minute reminders, to people who ticked the SMS consent box. They're not for cold outreach. If your buyers are in India, skip SMS. You'll use WhatsApp and email instead.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "List every sign-up and every waiting clock for my business, and draft my WhatsApp confirmation and reminder templates for Meta review" > the reply shows a checklist and two draft templates]

Now open Funnel OS and ask it for your sign-up list. Tell it where your buyers are and which payment provider you chose. It'll list every account, which ones start a wait, and what you've already done. While you're there, ask it to draft your WhatsApp confirmation and reminder templates, so you can submit them for Meta's review today. One link per message, and a line telling people to reply STOP to opt out.

[SLIDE 5] Finally, money. Here's what you'll pay this week, so nothing surprises you on Day 3.

Domains, paid yearly, usually around ten to fifteen dollars each. Google Workspace, a monthly fee per mailbox, for every outreach mailbox and every calendar sender. Twilio credit, at least twenty dollars. And Tantra Pro, plus any extra sending accounts beyond what your plan includes. Your plan page in the Tantra app shows what your membership covers, so check it today, not on launch day.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS > Funnel Brief > a "Cash this week" note with each line and its amount]

Ask Funnel OS to write that into a short cash list in your brief, with each line and its amount. If any line is a stretch, say so in the WhatsApp group now. We'd rather plan around it with you today.

Let me give you the common mistakes from Day 0, so you can skip them. Buying one outreach domain instead of two or three, so one bad week stops everything. Sending from the main company domain because it's already set up. Leaving the calendar Workspace on trial. Forgetting the registry email. And waiting until the funnel is built to apply for payments, which puts a three day gap right in the middle of launch week. If you've done the steps in this video, you've avoided all five.

That's every clock running. Your domains are bought, both Workspaces exist, your payment account is in review, and your templates are with Meta. Post your Build Score in the group, then open the next video, Funnel OS: One Chat That Runs Your Whole Funnel.

Screen recording shot list

  1. Cloudflare dashboard, Domain Registration, search for a demo name. Show available results, add two outreach domains and one calendar domain to the cart. Blur the account email and billing details. About 60 seconds.
  2. Cloudflare checkout, registrant contact form. Blur every field: name, address, phone, email. About 20 seconds.
  3. Google Workspace sign-up for the first outreach domain, then Admin console > Domains > add secondary domains, then Users > add two mailboxes. Blur any real names and the admin email. About 90 seconds.
  4. Second Workspace on the calendar domain: add sender users, then Billing > end trial. Blur user emails and card details. About 45 seconds.
  5. Dodo Payments sign-up and the verification form. Blur all business and identity details. About 40 seconds.
  6. Twilio console: Upgrade, add funds, Phone Numbers > toll-free verification request. Blur the account SID, any key and every phone number. About 50 seconds.
  7. Funnel OS chat with the sign-up list prompt and the reply, then the two draft WhatsApp templates. About 45 seconds.
  8. Funnel OS Funnel Brief with the "Cash this week" note. Use round demo amounts only. About 15 seconds.

  • [ ] Outreach domains and calendar domain bought, registry email reminder set (1 point)
  • [ ] Both Google Workspaces created with mailboxes and senders, calendar trial ended (1 point)
  • [ ] Payment account applied for, Twilio funded or skipped with a reason, cash list written in your brief (1 point)
scripts/V03-funnel-os-one-chat-that-runs-your-whole-funnel.md

V03: Funnel OS: One Chat That Runs Your Whole Funnel

  • Module: M1 Decide
  • Day: Day 1
  • Length: about 10 minutes (about 1,250 to 1,350 spoken words)
  • Systems on screen: Funnel OS
  • Before you start: V02 done, so your Cloudflare account and payment account exist
  • Done when: your project is open, your Funnel Brief has its eight sections, and Tantra, the CRM, the Content OS, Cloudflare and your payment provider all show Connected

Slides

Slide 1: One Chat, Every System

  • You type, Funnel OS does the work
  • Pages, messages, campaigns, numbers
  • Built from one Funnel Brief

Slide 2: The Funnel Brief

  • Eight sections, filled on Day 1
  • Offer, method, buyer, list
  • Funnel, event line, channels
  • Every page is written from it

Slide 3: Five Connections

  • Tantra: API key
  • Tantra CRM and Content OS
  • Cloudflare: API token
  • Payment provider: test key first

Slide 4: The Approval Rule

  • Never sends, launches, buys or deletes
  • Until you approve in your own words
  • A thumbs up is not approval

Slide 5: How to Ask

  • Say the outcome, not the clicks
  • Name the system if it matters
  • Ask it to show before it does

Voice script

[SLIDE 1] Most people building a funnel have eleven tabs open: the page builder, the email tool, the CRM, the payment dashboard, a spreadsheet of numbers, and a doc where they keep rewriting the same offer. Every tab has its own copy of the truth, and they drift apart. In this video I'll show you how to do almost all of it from one chat, Funnel OS, and how to stay in control while it works.

Funnel OS is a chat app. You tell it what you want in plain words, and it does the work across your systems. It writes your pages, emails and messages. It builds your pages and puts them live on your own Cloudflare. It drafts your Tantra campaigns, sets up your CRM pipeline, booking pages and sequences, creates your payment products, and pulls your numbers for the weekly review. You'll use it in almost every video from here.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS > Projects > New project > name it after your business > Create]

Let's start a project. Click New project and name it after your business and the funnel, for example Northfield webinar. One project per funnel. If you run a second funnel later, it gets its own project, so the two never mix.

A project holds three things. The chat, where you talk to it. The Funnel Brief, which is the memory of your business. And your files: the list you'll upload later, your logo, your testimonials, your call notes. Close the tab tonight, come back tomorrow, and it picks up exactly where you left off, because everything it needs is in the project, not in your head.

[SLIDE 2] [SCREEN: Funnel OS > the new project > Funnel Brief panel on the right showing eight empty sections]

On the right is your Funnel Brief. It has eight sections: your offer, your method and proof, your value score, your buyer, your list, your funnel, your event line, and your channels. Today, in the next five videos, you'll fill every one of them, mostly by answering Funnel OS's questions.

Here's why the brief matters. Every page, email, invite and reminder Funnel OS writes is written from this brief. So if your offer changes, you change it here once, and everything written after that uses the new version. No more five versions of your price floating around.

The sections also build on each other. Your buyer depends on your offer. Your list depends on your buyer. Your event line depends on your funnel. That's why we fill them in order over the next five videos, and why it's worth getting each one right before moving on.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Here is my website and my last three sales emails. Start my Funnel Brief from them and mark anything you guessed" > paste a demo website address > the reply fills parts of sections 1, 2 and 4 with check marks next to guesses]

A good first message is to give it what you already have. Your website, a few sales emails, notes from a sales call. It starts the brief from that and marks anything it guessed, so you know what to check. It never invents proof. If you don't have a result to show yet, it leaves that line empty.

[SLIDE 3] Now the connections. These let Funnel OS work inside your other systems.

[SCREEN: Tantra app > Connectors > API Keys > Create key > copy]

First, Tantra. In the Tantra app, open Connectors, then API Keys, and create a key. It's shown once, so copy it straight across.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS > Connections > Tantra > paste key > Save > status shows Connected]

In Funnel OS, open Connections, choose Tantra, paste, and save. You'll see Connected. Campaigns it creates in Tantra are always drafts until you approve them.

[SCREEN: Tantra CRM > Settings > API > create key > Funnel OS > Connections > Tantra CRM > paste > Connected]

Second, the Tantra CRM. Same pattern: create a key in the CRM's settings, paste it into Funnel OS. Now it can build your pipeline, your booking page and your sequences, and read your deals for the weekly numbers.

[SCREEN: Content OS > Settings > API > create key > Funnel OS > Connections > Content OS > paste > Connected]

Third, the Content OS. Same again. This lets Funnel OS hand your offer and event line straight to your content, so your posts promote the same event your pages do.

[SCREEN: Cloudflare > My Profile > API Tokens > Create Token > custom token with the permissions Funnel OS lists > choose your account > Create > copy]

Fourth, Cloudflare. In Cloudflare, go to your profile, then API Tokens, and create a custom token. Funnel OS shows you exactly which permissions to tick: zone and DNS editing so it can set up your domains, and the hosting permissions so it can deploy your pages. Set it to your own account. Copy the token, paste it into Funnel OS, and save.

[SCREEN: Dodo Payments > Developer > API Keys > test mode > create > Funnel OS > Connections > Payments > Dodo > paste > Connected (test mode)]

Fifth, your payment provider. Always start with the test mode key. For Dodo, it's under the developer settings in test mode. For Razorpay, use the test keys. For Stripe, create a restricted key with only the permissions Funnel OS lists, never the full access key. You'll swap to the live key only after your test run passes, much later.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS > Connections > all five rows showing Connected, one row showing an error message "permission missing"]

If a connection shows an error instead of Connected, it's almost always one of three things. The key was copied with a space at the end. The token is missing a permission. Or the key was made in the wrong account, for example a live key when you meant test. Funnel OS tells you which one in plain words. Fix it in the system the key came from, make a fresh key, and paste again.

Every key you paste is stored in the connection, not in the chat. If you ever paste a key into the chat by mistake, delete that key in the system it came from and make a new one.

[SLIDE 4] Now the most important rule in this course. Funnel OS never sends, launches, buys or deletes anything until you approve it, in your own words.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Create a test draft campaign in Tantra called Approval test" > the reply shows the draft and asks for approval > type "Yes, create the draft campaign called Approval test" > the reply confirms it is created as a draft]

Watch. I ask it to create a test campaign. It shows me exactly what it will do and stops. It's waiting for me. A thumbs up or OK isn't enough. I write it out: yes, create the draft campaign called Approval test. Only then does it act, and even then the campaign is a draft. Sending is a separate approval.

That rule protects you. Nothing goes to your list, no domain is bought, no payment product goes live and nothing gets deleted because of a typo or a guess. You're always the one who presses go.

It also means you can be relaxed about asking. Ask it to plan a whole campaign, draft fifty messages or rebuild a page. None of that touches the outside world until you say so. If you change your mind halfway, just say stop, or ask it to show you what it has so far. Drafts cost nothing. Sends, launches, purchases and deletions are the four things that wait for you, every single time, in every system it's connected to.

[SLIDE 5] Last, how to ask. Tell it the outcome you want, not the clicks. Say, set up a pipeline for my webinar funnel with the default stages, not, go to the CRM and click new pipeline. If it matters which system, name it. And when you're unsure, ask it to show you first. Show me the reminder emails before you build them. Show me last week's numbers by stage.

Here are a few things members ask on a normal day, so you can see how wide it goes. Draft the second follow up email for people who didn't book. Add a qualifying question to my booking page asking for company size. Create a test product at one dollar so I can try a payment. Which channel brought the most sign-ups this week? Write five LinkedIn posts that invite people to my event. Each of those touches a different system, and you never leave the chat. When the answer means changing something, it shows you first and waits for your approval.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "What is still missing before I can build my pages?" > the reply lists empty brief sections and any connection not yet Connected]

And here's the question I ask most: what's still missing? It reads your brief and your connections and tells you what's left, in order.

So, your project is open, your brief is waiting, and your five connections are green. If one won't connect, post the error in the WhatsApp group with the key blurred. Next, fill section one: Your Offer, Your Method and the Value Score.

Screen recording shot list

  1. Funnel OS, Projects list, click New project, type a demo name, Create. About 15 seconds.
  2. The empty project with the Funnel Brief panel showing all eight section headings. About 10 seconds.
  3. Type the first message, paste a demo website address, show the reply filling parts of sections 1, 2 and 4 with check marks. About 40 seconds.
  4. Tantra app, Connectors > API Keys > Create key. Blur the key the moment it appears. About 20 seconds.
  5. Funnel OS, Connections > Tantra, paste (blurred), Save, show Connected. About 15 seconds.
  6. Tantra CRM settings, create key (blur), paste into Funnel OS, Connected. About 25 seconds.
  7. Content OS settings, create key (blur), paste into Funnel OS, Connected. About 20 seconds.
  8. Cloudflare, My Profile > API Tokens > Create Token, custom token, tick the listed permissions, choose the account, Create. Blur the token and the account email. About 45 seconds.
  9. Dodo test mode, Developer > API Keys, create key (blur), paste into Funnel OS Payments, show Connected (test mode). About 30 seconds.
  10. Funnel OS Connections list with all five rows Connected, then a demo row showing a "permission missing" error. About 15 seconds.
  11. Funnel OS chat: the Approval test request, the pause, the typed approval, the draft confirmation. About 40 seconds.
  12. Funnel OS chat: "What is still missing" and the reply. About 20 seconds.

Build Score checklist

  • [ ] Funnel OS project created and the Funnel Brief started from your own material (1 point)
  • [ ] Tantra and the Tantra CRM show Connected (1 point)
  • [ ] Content OS, Cloudflare and your payment provider (test mode) show Connected, and one approval typed in your own words (1 point)
scripts/V04-your-offer-your-method-and-the-value-score.md

V04: Your Offer, Your Method and the Value Score

  • Module: M1 Decide
  • Day: Day 1
  • Length: about 10 minutes (about 1,200 to 1,300 spoken words)
  • Systems on screen: Funnel OS
  • Before you start: V03 done, your Funnel OS project open
  • Done when: sections 1, 2 and 3 of your Funnel Brief are filled, and your offer sentence carries the fixes for your two weakest levers

Slides

Slide 1: One Sentence Every Page Repeats

  • I help who go from before to after
  • Using your method, in a time
  • Or your promise

Slide 2: Fill the Six Slots

  • Who: size, industry, role
  • After: a number, a unit, a period
  • Method holds the tool, never the outcome
  • Promise: who carries the risk

Slide 3: Name Your Method

  • Two to four words
  • Three to five steps, each with a because
  • Why the obvious alternative fails

Slide 4: Proof, Never Invented

  • Number, who, when
  • None yet: borrowed, and labelled
  • Empty is better than made up

Slide 5: The Value Score

  • Dream outcome x likelihood
  • Divided by time delay x effort
  • Fix the two weakest levers

Voice script

[SLIDE 1] If someone asks what you do and you need three sentences and a "basically" to answer, every page and email you write will have the same problem. People read it, nod, and don't act. In this video you'll write one sentence that says what you sell so clearly a stranger can repeat it back, give your method a name, and score your offer the way a sceptical buyer would, then fix its two weakest points.

Here's the sentence. I help who, go from before, to after, using your method, in this much time, or your promise. Every page, email, invite and post in your funnel repeats it. So we get it right once, here.

[SLIDE 2] There are six slots. Let's go through them.

Who is a size, an industry and a role together. Not founders. Not small businesses. Something like accounting firms with ten to fifty staff, the managing partner. A stranger should be able to picture one company.

Before is where they're stuck today, in their words, not yours. After is the result, with a number, a unit and a period. Ten booked calls a month. Not more growth.

The method is how you get them there. Your tool or software goes here, never in the after. Nobody wants software. They want the result.

Time is how long until they see it. And the promise is who carries the risk if it doesn't work. If you can't honestly offer one, say no guarantee. Never write a promise you won't keep.

Here's what a finished one sounds like. We help B2B software companies with eleven to fifty staff go from ad hoc demos to a steady weekly demo calendar, using a three channel invite system, in sixty days, or we keep working at no charge until you get there. You can picture the company. You know the before and the after. The method is named. And the risk sits with the seller, not the buyer.

The two mistakes I see most often are a buyer named as a market, like SMBs or founders, and an after with no number in it, like more growth or better leads. If yours has either, fix it before you go on.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Interview me for section 1 of my Funnel Brief, the offer sentence. Ask one question at a time." > the reply asks the first question about who you sell to]

You don't need to write this from scratch. Ask Funnel OS to interview you for section one. It asks one question at a time. Answer like you're talking to a friend. Paste in real customer words where you have them.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > the reply shows three versions of the offer sentence, guesses marked check, and a note on which one a stranger could repeat]

At the end it gives you three versions, marks anything it guessed, and tells you which one a stranger is most likely to repeat after hearing it once. Now do the real test. Read your favourite out loud to someone outside your business. If they ask a follow-up question, it's not done. Tell Funnel OS what they asked, and it'll tighten it.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS > Funnel Brief > section 1 showing the sentence, price, what is included, what is not included, and the ladder]

Under the sentence, write the price, what's included, and what's not included. That last line matters more than people think. Anything you leave out comes back as an objection on the sales call. Then place the offer on your ladder. Is it your entry offer, your core offer or your top offer? Name and price the rung above it, even if you haven't built it yet. You'll need it in week four.

[SLIDE 3] Next, your method. An unnamed method sounds like every competitor, and then you compete on price. A named one gives people a reason to pick you.

Two to four words that describe what happens. Then three to five steps, each with one line starting with because. The because is what makes it believable. And one line on why the obvious alternative fails, whether that's hiring an agency, buying a tool on its own, or doing nothing.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Draft section 2: a method name, three to five steps with a because for each, why the alternative fails, and my voice as opposites. Use only what I give you." > paste delivery notes and three pieces of your writing > the reply shows the draft with check marks]

Give Funnel OS how you actually deliver the work, and three pieces of writing that sound like you. Posts, emails, a page. It drafts the method and writes your voice as opposites: three words you sound like, three you never sound like. That voice is what keeps every later page sounding like you and not like a template.

Here's an example of a method that works. The Demo Engine: list, invite, remind, follow up. Four steps, each one obvious once it's named, and each one with a reason. Lists, because you can't invite someone you haven't named. Reminders, because people who sign up forget. The one mistake to avoid is naming your method after the tool you use. Any competitor with the same tool can copy that name tomorrow. They can't copy how you do the work.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS > Funnel Brief > section 2 > brand rules: speaker name, title, two line bio, main call to action, colours as hex codes, font, logo file]

Also fill the brand rules once: your name and title, a two line bio, your main call to action, your colours as hex codes, your font and your logo. Write each one exactly once, so nothing drifts.

Colours as hex codes, not words like navy, because navy means a different blue to everyone. The bio is the one people will read on your landing page and hear at the start of your webinar, so keep it to what you've actually done.

[SLIDE 4] Now proof. One line per result: the number, who got it, and when. Here's what that looks like from our own work. Growth Idea, a UK coaching group, ran eleven webinars between May 2025 and February 2026 and got six thousand five hundred and seventeen registrations. Number, who, when.

If you don't have results yet, that's fine. Use borrowed proof, like public research, and label it borrowed. Or leave the line empty. Funnel OS will never fill it for you, and you should never make one up. An empty line costs you a little. An invented one can cost you the business.

[SLIDE 5] Last, the value score. It's four levers. Dream outcome times likelihood, divided by time delay times effort. The top two make the offer more valuable when they go up. The bottom two make it more valuable when they go down.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Score my offer on the value score as a sceptical managing partner would. Out of 10 each, one reason per score, one change per lever, then rewrite my offer sentence with the two weakest fixed." > the reply shows four scores, four changes and a rewritten sentence]

Ask Funnel OS to score you as a sceptical buyer would. Not as your mum would. If every lever comes back an eight, push back and ask it to be harsher.

For each lever, it also suggests one change. For dream outcome, that's usually a number and a deadline. For likelihood, proof a sceptic can see in ten seconds. For time delay, a first win in the first week. And for effort, one step you do for the buyer instead of asking them to do it.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS > the reply highlighting time delay 4 and effort 3 > the rewritten sentence including "first campaign live in week one, and we do the setup"]

Here, time delay and effort scored lowest. That's normal. Most people try to fix an offer by promising a bigger outcome, but the easier wins are at the bottom: a first result in week one, or one step you do for them. So the change is, first campaign live in week one, and we do the setup. Both fixes go inside the sentence.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Yes, update section 1 with the new sentence and save section 3" > the brief shows sections 1, 2 and 3 filled]

Approve the update in your own words, and sections one, two and three are done.

If you're wondering whether this is worth the time on a build day, here's the honest reason. Copy can't save a weak offer. If the offer is weak, people sign up, nobody buys, and three weeks from now you'll blame the page, rewrite the headline twice and change nothing that matters. Twenty minutes here saves that month.

Read your new sentence out loud once more. If it sounds like something you'd say on a call, you're there. Post it in the WhatsApp group if you'd like a second opinion, then open the next video, Your Buyer and the One Equation.

Screen recording shot list

  1. Funnel OS project open, type the interview prompt for section 1, answer three of its questions with a demo business. About 60 seconds.
  2. The reply with three offer versions and the stranger test note. About 20 seconds.
  3. Funnel Brief section 1: sentence, price, included, not included, ladder with the next rung named and priced. About 20 seconds.
  4. Section 2 prompt with pasted demo delivery notes and writing samples, then the draft method and voice. About 50 seconds.
  5. Section 2 brand rules filled with demo values (hex codes, font, logo link). Blur any real personal email. About 20 seconds.
  6. Section 2 proof list showing one borrowed line labelled borrowed and one empty line. About 10 seconds.
  7. The value score prompt and the reply with four scores and changes. Enlarge the two lowest scores. About 40 seconds.
  8. The typed approval and the brief showing sections 1 to 3 filled. About 15 seconds.

Build Score checklist

  • [ ] Offer sentence passes the stranger test, with price, included, not included and the next rung named (1 point)
  • [ ] Method named with steps and reasons, voice and brand rules written once, proof listed or left honestly empty (1 point)
  • [ ] Value score done, two weakest levers fixed inside the offer sentence (1 point)
scripts/V05-your-buyer-and-the-one-equation.md

V05: Your Buyer and the One Equation

  • Module: M1 Decide
  • Day: Day 1
  • Length: about 9 minutes (about 1,050 to 1,170 spoken words)
  • Systems on screen: Funnel OS
  • Before you start: V04 done, sections 1 to 3 of your Funnel Brief filled
  • Done when: section 4 of your Funnel Brief traces every value to a real customer, quotes the deal-killing objection, and lists at least three exclusions

Slides

Slide 1: Describe the Buyers You Have

  • Your 20 best customers
  • None yet: your 10 best conversations
  • Shared by more than half: it goes in

Slide 2: What the Persona Holds

  • Bands, not labels
  • The role that can say yes alone
  • The objection, word for word
  • The trigger that makes it urgent

Slide 3: Exclusions

  • What your worst five share
  • At least three written
  • They become negative filters

Slide 4: Six Stages, One Number Each

  • Traffic: visit rate
  • Landing: sign-up rate
  • Nurture: show-up rate
  • Conversion, revenue, long-term nurture

Slide 5: The One Equation

  • Customers = sign-ups x show-up x booking x close
  • Every rate multiplies
  • Fix the weakest, not the favourite

Voice script

[SLIDE 1] Here's a painful way to lose a month. Your emails go out, your page works, people even sign up, and none of them ever buy. Nothing was broken. You were just talking to the wrong people, very efficiently. This video fixes that before you build anything. You'll describe your buyer from real customers, not from a wish list, and then I'll show you the one equation that ties your whole funnel together.

Start with the customers you already have. List your twenty best, by revenue, by how long they stayed and by how easy they were to work with. Fewer than twenty? Use them all. None yet? Use your ten best sales conversations, and mark every value as assumed until a real customer confirms it.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Build section 4 of my Funnel Brief from these customers. A value goes in only if more than half share it. Quote objections exactly. Mark guesses as check." > paste a demo table of customers with size, industry, country, buyer title and why they bought]

Paste them into Funnel OS as a simple table: company, size, industry, country, the title of the person who bought, and why they bought. If you have sales call notes, paste those too. The rule it follows is simple. A value only goes into your persona if more than half of your best customers share it. That stops one big client from bending the whole picture.

[SLIDE 2] [SCREEN: Funnel OS > Funnel Brief > section 4 filling in: headcount band, revenue band, industries, geography, roles with title variants]

Here's what comes back. Bands, not labels. Ten to fifty staff, accounting, UK and Ireland. Not small businesses. Then the role that can say yes on their own, with the title variants people actually use on LinkedIn. Managing partner, practice owner, director. You'll use those exact titles to build your list in a few videos.

Then the number they're judged on, in their words. What blocks it. Where they get their information: the publications, communities and people they follow. And who else needs to sign off.

Then two reference clients. A ceiling client, the biggest company you'd realistically win, and a floor client, the smallest one worth serving. Anything outside that range goes into your exclusions. It's a quick way to stop your list drifting toward companies that look impressive but would never buy from you.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS > section 4 > Objection field showing a quoted line from a demo sales call]

Now the most valuable line in the whole brief. The objection that kills your deals, word for word. Not a summary. The exact sentence a buyer said. Something like, we tried outreach before and it made us look desperate. That sentence will become the opening of your best cold email and a slide in your webinar, because it shows you understand them better than anyone pitching them.

And the trigger. Something visible from the outside that makes this urgent this quarter. A new hire, a funding round, a rule change, a busy season ending. Triggers tell you when to reach out, not just who.

One thing I'd ask you to do today, outside Funnel OS. Send a short message to five of your customers: what number are you personally judged on this quarter, and what's in the way? Five sent, not five drafted. Their answers, in their own words, are the best material you'll ever give Funnel OS, and they often change the after in your offer sentence.

[SLIDE 3] Next, exclusions. Write down what your worst five customers had in common. Too small to pay on time. The wrong industry. A role that couldn't decide. Write at least three. When you build your list, these become negative filters, so those people never get an invite in the first place.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Check section 4. Which values trace to fewer than half my customers? What did you guess?" > the reply lists two check marks to confirm]

Ask Funnel OS to check its own work. Which values are guesses? Which trace to fewer than half your customers? Fix those now.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS > section 4 > Exclusions field showing three lines and a note "used as negative filters in section 5"]

Then look at the whole section as a stranger would. Could you hand it to a new salesperson on their first day, and would they know who to call and who to ignore? If yes, it's done. If they'd still have questions, those questions are your next edits. A guessed persona builds a list of the wrong people, and every step after that works perfectly on buyers who will never buy.

[SLIDE 4] Now the equation. Every funnel in this course has six stages, and each stage has one number.

Traffic is your visit rate: of the people you reach, how many visit your page. Landing is your sign-up rate: of the visitors, how many sign up. Nurture is your show-up rate: of the sign-ups, how many turn up. Conversion is your booking rate: of the people who showed up, how many book a call or click buy. Revenue is your close rate: of the calls, how many pay. And long-term nurture is your rebooking rate: of everyone who didn't buy, how many come back later.

Six stages, six numbers. That's your dashboard in week two, and it's your Funnel Score.

[SLIDE 5] And they connect in one line. Customers equal sign-ups, times show-up rate, times booking rate, times close rate.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Show me the one equation with the teaching model rates, labelled, for 1,000 sign-ups, then show what happens if show-up rate goes up by a third" > the reply shows the calculation stage by stage]

Let me show you with numbers. These are from our teaching model. They're not a benchmark and not anyone's result, just a way to see the maths. In our teaching model, show-up rate is about thirty percent, booking rate about seven and a half percent, and close rate about seventeen percent. So a thousand sign-ups gives you about three hundred people in the room, about twenty two booked calls, and about four customers.

Now here's the point. Every rate multiplies. If you raise show-up by a third, from the same thousand sign-ups you get about five customers instead of four, and you didn't send a single extra email. That's why, from week two, you don't fix the step you like working on. You fix the step that's weakest compared with your own last week. One change, on one step.

It also tells you something about where to spend your effort this week. Every stage you build in the next two days exists to move one of those six numbers. The landing page moves sign-up rate. The reminders move show-up rate. The offer and the booking page move booking rate. Your sales follow ups move close rate. When you're tired on Day 2 and tempted to skip a reminder, remember it's a multiplier on everything that comes after it.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS > Funnel Brief > section 4 complete with no check marks left]

For now you don't have your own rates yet, and that's fine. You'll have them after your first week of traffic. What you do have is a buyer you can describe in one breath and an equation you'll come back to every single week.

Your next step is to clear every check mark in section four. Then open the next video, Size Your List Backwards From Your Revenue Goal, where this equation tells you how many people you need to reach.

Screen recording shot list

  1. Funnel OS project, type the section 4 prompt, paste a demo customer table (made-up company names only). Blur nothing real because nothing real is shown. About 40 seconds.
  2. Section 4 filling in: bands, roles and title variants, goal, challenge, information sources. Scroll slowly. About 40 seconds.
  3. The objection field with a quoted demo line. Enlarge it. About 15 seconds.
  4. Trigger and exclusions fields filled. About 20 seconds.
  5. The self-check prompt and the reply listing guesses, then clearing them. About 30 seconds.
  6. The equation prompt and the stage by stage reply, with the "teaching model" label visible on screen. About 45 seconds.
  7. Section 4 complete with no check marks. About 10 seconds.

Build Score checklist

  • [ ] Section 4 filled from real customers or conversations, with title variants, the trigger and the objection quoted word for word (1 point)
  • [ ] At least three exclusions written and no check marks left in section 4 (1 point)
scripts/V06-size-your-list-backwards-from-your-revenue-goal.md

V06: Size Your List Backwards From Your Revenue Goal

  • Module: M1 Decide
  • Day: Day 1
  • Length: about 8 minutes (about 1,000 to 1,100 spoken words)
  • Systems on screen: Funnel OS
  • Before you start: V05 done, your price written in section 1
  • Done when: section 5 of your Funnel Brief shows the maths from your revenue target back to people reached, every rate is labelled, and the target is either realistic or changed

Slides

Slide 1: Start From the Money

  • Revenue target for this funnel
  • Divided by price
  • Equals buyers needed

Slide 2: Walk Back Through the Rates

  • Buyers divided by close rate
  • Then booking rate
  • Then show-up rate
  • Then sign-up and visit rate

Slide 3: Label Every Rate

  • Your own: from your records
  • Teaching model: labelled, replaced in week 2
  • Unknown: will measure

Slide 4: When the Number Is Absurd

  • Raise the price or the rung
  • Change the offer
  • Change the funnel
  • Not just buy more data

Voice script

[SLIDE 1] Most people decide how big their list should be by looking at their budget. A thousand contacts sounds like a lot, so they buy a thousand. Three weeks later they work out that a thousand contacts could never have produced the sales they needed. In this video you'll do it the other way round. You'll start from the money and work backwards to how many people you need to reach, so you know on Day 1 whether your plan can work.

Step one. Pick a monthly revenue target for this one funnel. Not your whole business, and not the hundred K yet. The first month from this funnel. Then divide it by your price. That's how many buyers you need.

Why a monthly number? Because MRR is monthly. If your offer is a retainer, each buyer adds to next month too, and that's how the hundred K builds up: month on month, not in one launch. If your offer is a one off project, divide its price by the months it covers, or just use the one off price and be honest that next month starts from zero.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Work my funnel backwards for section 5. Target: 20,000 dollars a month from this funnel. Price: 2,000 dollars. I have no rates of my own yet." > the reply starts with "Buyers needed: 10"]

Here's a demo. Twenty thousand dollars a month, at two thousand dollars a sale. That's ten buyers.

[SLIDE 2] Now walk backwards through the equation from the last video, one rate at a time. Buyers divided by close rate gives you how many sales calls you need. Divide that by booking rate, and you get how many people need to see your offer. Divide that by show-up rate, and you get how many sign-ups. Divide by sign-up rate, and you get visits. Divide by visit rate, and you get how many people you have to reach.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS > the reply showing each stage with the rate used, its label, and the result rounded up]

Funnel OS shows every stage, and it always rounds up, because you can't book half a call.

Read it from the bottom up and it's your whole funnel in one column. People reached at the top, buyers at the bottom, and a rate on every line in between. You'll see this same column again in week two, with your real numbers next to it.

[SLIDE 3] Here's the rule that matters most. Every rate gets a label. If you have your own number, from your own records, use it. Close rate is buyers divided by people who heard the offer. If you've run calls before, you probably know it.

If you don't, you've got two honest choices. You can use our teaching model, labelled as that. Or you can write unknown, will measure. What you never do is take a benchmark from a blog post and treat it as yours.

For the demo I'll use the teaching model, and I'll say so each time. In our teaching model, close rate is about seventeen percent. Ten buyers divided by that is fifty nine calls. In our teaching model, booking rate is about seven and a half percent. So about seven hundred and seventy people need to see the offer. In our teaching model, show-up rate is about thirty percent. So about two thousand six hundred sign-ups.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS > the sign-up rate line reading "unknown, will measure" and a note that the reach figure is shown as a range]

And sign-up rate? I don't have one for this page yet, so it says unknown, will measure. Funnel OS lists it as the first thing to measure in week one and shows the range of reach it would imply. That's honest. A range you'll narrow beats a single number you made up.

[SLIDE 4] Now look at that number. Two thousand six hundred sign-ups for one event is a lot. For a reality check, here's real data from a client. Growth Idea, a coaching group in the UK, ran eleven webinars and reached seven hundred and sixty five thousand people to get six and a half thousand registrations. That's not your rate and it's not a promise. It's just a picture of how big reach has to be when the price is modest and the traffic is cold.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "This looks too big for one month. Show me three ways to make it realistic." > the reply shows options: raise the price, sell the next rung, switch to a book a call funnel, spread over several events]

So when the number looks absurd, don't buy a bigger list. Change something upstream. Ask Funnel OS for options. You could raise the price, or sell the rung above it. At five thousand dollars, the same target needs four buyers, and every number behind it shrinks by more than half. You could improve the offer, which lifts the rates. You could switch funnels, for example to a book a call funnel if your buyers already know the category. Or you could spread the target over several events across the month.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Use 5,000 dollars, two events this month. Update section 5." > the reply shows the new maths > type "Yes, save this as section 5" > the brief updates]

Pick the change you'd actually make, run the maths again, and approve it into section five in your own words.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "How many mailboxes, LinkedIn profiles and calendar senders do I need to reach this many people before my event date?" > the reply shows sends per day per channel against the date]

Then ask one more question: can my sending reach this many people before the event date? Remember the clocks from Day 0. Mailboxes send about twenty a day while they warm and about fifty once warm, and cold email only starts on day fourteen. Calendar senders send twenty a day each until day sixty. LinkedIn is fifteen to twenty requests a day per profile. If the reply says you can't reach enough people in time, you have two honest options. Add mailboxes or senders now, knowing each new one warms from zero. Or move the date. Don't push one mailbox harder. That's how domains get burned. A slower, honest plan you can keep beats a fast one that breaks in week two.

One more thing about the hundred K. You won't get there from one funnel in month one, and you don't have to. What you're building is a machine that tells you, every week, which rate to fix. When your own rates replace the teaching model in week two, this maths gets sharper, and the jump from twenty thousand to a hundred thousand becomes a list of rates to move, not a wish.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS > Funnel Brief > section 5 showing target, price, buyers, each stage with a labelled rate, and the reach range]

So section five now shows your target, your price, your buyers, every stage, every rate with its label, and the reach you need. Write down which rate you'll measure first, and post your reach number in the WhatsApp group if you want a coach to sanity check it. Next video: Pick Your Funnel and Write Your Event Line.

Screen recording shot list

  1. Funnel OS project, type the backwards maths prompt with demo numbers. About 20 seconds.
  2. The reply, stage by stage, with each rate labelled own, teaching model, or unknown. Scroll slowly and enlarge the labels. About 50 seconds.
  3. The sign-up rate line reading "unknown, will measure" and the reach range. About 15 seconds.
  4. The "too big" prompt and the reply listing options. About 30 seconds.
  5. The sending capacity prompt and the reply showing sends per day per channel against the event date. About 25 seconds.
  6. The update prompt, the new maths, the typed approval and section 5 saved. About 40 seconds.

Build Score checklist

  • [ ] Section 5 shows the maths from your target and price back to people reached, rounded up (1 point)
  • [ ] Every rate labelled as your own, teaching model or unknown, and the target judged realistic or changed (1 point)
scripts/V07-pick-your-funnel-and-write-your-event-line.md

V07: Pick Your Funnel and Write Your Event Line

  • Module: M1 Decide
  • Day: Day 1
  • Length: about 8 minutes (about 1,000 to 1,100 spoken words)
  • Systems on screen: Funnel OS
  • Before you start: V06 done, sections 1 to 5 of your Funnel Brief filled
  • Done when: section 6 names your funnel and the answer that decided it, and section 7 holds one event line that nothing else in your brief contradicts

Slides

Slide 1: Eight Funnels

  • Webinar, Workshop, Reverse Squeeze Page
  • Book a Call, Challenge, Community
  • Social Following, Product Launch
  • Not sure: pick the webinar

Slide 2: Five Questions

  • Do you know which message wins
  • Must they learn or do first
  • How much do they already believe
  • Who closes: a page or a person

Slide 3: The Event Line

  • Title, date, time zone
  • Length, platform, replay close
  • Written once, repeated everywhere

Slide 4: Title Rules

  • Under 70 characters
  • No $, % or the word free
  • Buyer's outcome, then your method

Slide 5: Date Rules

  • At least 2 weeks out for cold traffic
  • The buyer's time zone, not yours
  • Check holidays in their countries

Voice script
