[SLIDE 1] I've watched people switch funnels three times in a month. A webinar, then a challenge because someone on social media said challenges work, then a book a call page. Each one half built, none of them measured. In this video you'll pick one funnel on purpose, write down why, and then write the single line that every invite, reminder and page will repeat.

There are eight funnels you can build in this course. Webinar. Workshop. Reverse squeeze page, which is a video page that gives before it asks. Book a call. Challenge. Community. Social following. And product launch.

You pick on two things: what your buyers already believe, and who closes the sale, a page or a person. Never on taste.

[SLIDE 2] Five questions decide it.

One. Do you already know which message wins buyers, with case studies to show it? If yes, a reverse squeeze page can sell from one video. If not, start with a webinar. It's the fastest way to learn which message lands.

Two. Do people have to learn or do something before they feel the value? If yes, a workshop or a challenge.

Three. How much do your buyers already believe? If they've never heard of you, give value first. If they know the category but not your method, teach first, with a webinar, a workshop or a launch. If they're already convinced and the deal is big, book a call.

Four. Who closes the sale? If a person closes on a call, every stage exists to book that call. If a page takes payment, that page has to answer every objection on its own. As a rough line, under about a thousand dollars, send people to checkout. Above it, book a call.

Five. How many hours a week can you really give it? A funnel you can run every week beats a better one you can't.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Ask me the five questions that pick my funnel, one at a time, using my brief" > the reply asks question one, already noting what section 2 says about proof]

Ask Funnel OS to run you through them. It reads your brief first, so it already knows your price, your proof and your buyer, and it'll challenge an answer that doesn't match.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS > the reply recommending Webinar with the reason "buyers know the category but not your method, and a person closes" > type "Yes, save Webinar to section 6 with that reason" > section 6 updates]

If you're still not sure at the end, pick the webinar. It suits any B2B business, and it tells you which message lands before you put time into anything slower. Our client Growth Idea, a UK coaching group, ran eleven of them between May 2025 and February 2026.

Write one sentence: the funnel and the answer that decided it. Webinar, because our buyers know the category but not our method, and a person closes. Then you don't switch. You finish this one, measure it, and only then decide.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "What does a webinar funnel ask of me each week once it is live?" > the reply lists the weekly tasks and hours]

Before you lock it in, ask Funnel OS what this funnel will ask of you every week once it's live. For a webinar it's the event itself, the reminders, answering replies and calls, and one experiment. If that doesn't fit your week, it's better to know now. A book a call funnel needs fewer live hours but more sales calls. A challenge needs an hour every day it runs. Then block your build hours in the calendar, if you haven't yet.

[SLIDE 3] Now the event line. It's one line with six parts: the title, the date, the time with the time zone, the length, the platform, and when the replay closes. You write it once, in section seven, and everything else copies it. A second version of the date anywhere, even one typo in one reminder, costs you attendees.

[SLIDE 4] The title first. It names the outcome your buyer wants, and hints at your method. Not the name of your offer. Keep it under seventy characters, because it has to fit in a calendar notification. And no dollar signs, no percent signs, and not the word free. Those read like an ad and get filtered.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Write 10 event titles from my brief. Under 70 characters. No $, % or free. Then pick two for an A/B test, one benefit led and one problem led." > the reply shows 10 titles with character counts and two picks]

Ask for ten. It counts the characters for you. Then it picks two for your first test: one benefit led, one problem led. You'll use both later, split across your list.

Here's the difference. A benefit led title says what they get. How accounting firms win new retainers without waiting for referrals. A problem led title names what hurts. Why referrals stopped filling your diary, and what to do instead. You don't know yet which one your buyers respond to, and neither do I. The test tells you, with your own list, in your first week.

[SLIDE 5] Now the date. For cold traffic, at least two weeks out. That gives invites time to land and gives your new calendar senders time to ramp. Check public holidays in your buyers' countries. Then the time. A working hour in your buyer's time zone, not yours. If your buyers are in London and you're in Bangalore, you set it for London.

Length: long enough to teach, make one offer and take questions. For a webinar, an hour. And set a real replay close date, and keep it. A replay that never closes gives nobody a reason to watch today.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Write my event line for section 7 with title A, Thursday 3 December, 14:00 London, 60 minutes, on my webinar platform, replay closes Sunday 6 December" > the reply shows one line > type "Yes, save it to section 7 and check the brief for any other date" > the reply confirms no conflicts]

Here's what that looks like as one line. Title, Thursday, two o'clock London time, sixty minutes, on your webinar platform, replay closes Sunday. Approve it into section seven, and ask Funnel OS to check the whole brief for any other version of the date or time. It should come back clean.

Why so strict about one line? Because in a moment it gets copied into your landing page, your thank-you page, your confirmation email, five reminders, your calendar invites, your LinkedIn messages and your posts. If one of those says two o'clock and another says three, people turn up at the wrong time, or not at all. One line, written once, means every copy is right.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS > Funnel Brief > sections 6 and 7 filled]

If your funnel has no event date, like book a call or community, you write the evergreen line instead: what the call or community gives people, in one sentence, and where it happens. You still write it once, and everything copies it.

Your next step: put your event in your own calendar now, with the time zone set. Then open the next video, Build, Clean and Load Your List.

Screen recording shot list

  1. Funnel OS project, type the five questions prompt, answer each with a demo business. About 60 seconds.
  2. The weekly load prompt and the reply listing weekly tasks and hours. About 20 seconds.
  3. The recommendation and reason, the typed approval, section 6 updating. About 20 seconds.
  4. The title prompt and the reply with 10 titles and character counts. Enlarge the counts and the two picks. About 30 seconds.
  5. The event line prompt, the one line reply, the typed approval and the conflict check reply. About 40 seconds.
  6. Funnel Brief with sections 6 and 7 filled. About 10 seconds.

Build Score checklist

  • [ ] Section 6 names your funnel and the answer that decided it (1 point)
  • [ ] Section 7 holds one event line with two A/B titles, a date at least 2 weeks out in the buyer's time zone, and a replay close date, with no conflicts in the brief (1 point)
scripts/V08-build-clean-and-load-your-list.md

V08: Build, Clean and Load Your List

  • Module: M1 Decide
  • Day: Day 1
  • Length: about 9 minutes (about 1,050 to 1,170 spoken words)
  • Systems on screen: Funnel OS, Tantra app
  • Before you start: V07 done, sections 4 and 5 of your Funnel Brief filled, Tantra connected in Funnel OS
  • Done when: a clean list, with every removed row kept and its reason, is approved on a 25 row sample and loaded into Tantra with matching counts

Slides

Slide 1: Pick One Source

  • Our data partners: no account needed
  • A contact database: title and company size
  • Clay: when a trigger defines the buyer
  • Your own CRM and customer list

Slide 2: Filters Come From the Brief

  • Titles with every LinkedIn variant
  • Size, industry, countries
  • Exclusions as negative filters
  • Work email and LinkedIn URL on every row

Slide 3: Clean in This Order

  • Lower case, then remove duplicates
  • Customer domains and exclusion words
  • Excluded countries and do not contact
  • Email format and domain match

Slide 4: Read the First 25 Rows

  • Would you invite each one
  • Wrong titles: fix the role field
  • Wrong companies: fix size or industry
  • Fix the filter, never the rows

Slide 5: Load It Into Tantra

  • Eight columns, fixed order
  • Counts match before and after
  • Never paste the list into a chat

Voice script

[SLIDE 1] Here's a mistake I see every month. Someone buys a list, loads it, and sends to it, and the first reply is from their own biggest client asking why they got a cold email. Or a hundred emails bounce on day one and the new domain takes a hit it never recovers from. In this video you'll build your list from your brief, clean it in a fixed order, check it with your own eyes, and load it into Tantra ready for your campaigns.

Start with one source. If you don't have a database account of your own, use our data partners. They pull the rows for you, quote the price before anything is bought, and send you a file. If your buyers are defined by title and company size and you already pay for a contact database, use that. If a trigger defines your buyer, like a recent funding round or a new hire, Clay is the better fit. And don't forget the list you already own: past enquiries, past registrants and lapsed buyers from your CRM. Those people already know you.

[SLIDE 2] [SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Turn my persona and exclusions into list filters for my data source, and tell me how many rows to ask for from section 5" > the reply shows titles with variants, headcount, industry, countries, negative filters and a row count]

You don't write the filters by hand. Ask Funnel OS to turn your persona into filters. It takes the titles with every variant people use on LinkedIn, your company size band, your industries, your countries, and your exclusions as negative filters. It also tells you how many rows to order, based on the reach you worked out in section five. More rows than that is money spent on people you'll never contact this month.

Every row needs a work email and a LinkedIn URL, so one list feeds both your email and LinkedIn campaigns in Tantra.

A word on size before you order. Your first list should be a few hundred to about a thousand people. That's enough to fill a first event and to learn your own rates, and small enough that a bad filter costs you very little. You can always order more next week, once you know which titles reply. You can't take back an email that went to the wrong thousand people.

And a word on price. Data prices change often, so check the current price on the source's own pricing page, or ask our data partners for a quote, before anyone buys anything. Funnel OS will never buy a list for you. It drafts the order and you place it.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS > the reply's country check listing each buyer country and its cold outreach rule status]

Before you order anything, check the cold outreach rules for each country you're selling into, for example GDPR in the UK and the EU. Funnel OS lists your countries and asks you to confirm each one. If you're not sure about a country, leave it out until you've checked. Never send into a country first and check later.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS > Files > upload the CSV from your data source > the file shows as uploaded with a row count, contents blurred]

When your file arrives, upload it to your Funnel OS project. Upload it as a file. Never paste a list of real people into the chat, and never post it in the WhatsApp group.

[SLIDE 3] Now the clean. It always runs in the same order, whoever made the file.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Clean my list in the standard order. Keep every removed row in a separate file with its reason. Here are my customer domains and my do not contact list." > upload a do not contact file > the reply shows each step with how many rows it removed]

One, every email goes to lower case with spaces trimmed. Two, duplicates out. Three, anyone at your customers' email domains, out. This is the step that stops you cold emailing your own clients, so give Funnel OS your customer domains. Four, anyone whose title or company contains one of your exclusion words. Five, anyone in a country you've excluded. Six, your do not contact list: past bounces, unsubscribes, people who already replied, past registrants and anyone you've already contacted. Funnel OS can pull Tantra's own bounce and unsubscribe records through the connection too. Seven, every email has to be well formed, and its domain has to match the company's website.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS > the clean summary table: rows in, removed per step, rows out, and a link to the removed rows file]

Each step shows how many rows it removed, and every removed row goes into a separate file with its reason. So if a coach asks why the list shrank, you can show them in ten seconds.

[SLIDE 4] Now the most important two minutes in this video. Read the first twenty five rows yourself.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS > the first 25 rows shown as a table with names, emails and LinkedIn URLs blurred, titles and company sizes visible]

For each one, ask: would I invite this person? If most of them look like your best customers, good. If you see a pattern of misses, it points at one field. Wrong titles mean your role field is too loose. Wrong companies mean your size or industry fields are off. Fix the filter in your brief, or add an exclusion word, and run it again. Never delete bad rows one by one. If you fix the rows, the next file has the same problem. If you fix the filter, it's gone for good.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Rows 4, 9 and 17 are recruiters. Add recruiter as an exclusion word and clean again." > the reply shows the new counts and a new 25 row sample]

Here three of my twenty five are recruiters. I add recruiter as an exclusion word, clean again, and read the new sample. Better. If the sample still looks wrong after two rounds, stop and post in the WhatsApp group with the titles blurred down to the pattern you see. Often the fix is a persona question, not a list question, and a coach can spot it quickly.

Notice what I'm not doing here. I'm not paying for an email checker. A good source verifies the list before it sends it, and the free checks you just ran catch the rest. What protects your domains in week three is not a tool, it's this order: clean, sample, fix the filter, then load.

[SLIDE 5] [SCREEN: Funnel OS > the clean CSV preview showing the eight column headers: first_name, last_name, title, company, website, email, linkedin_url, country, with the rows blurred]

The clean list comes out with eight columns in a fixed order: first name, last name, title, company, website, email, LinkedIn URL and country. If a first name is missing, the cell stays empty. Your messages will be written to read well either way.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Load the approved list into Tantra as a new list called Webinar December list" > the reply shows what it will do and the row count > type "Yes, load the 1,214 approved rows into Tantra as Webinar December list" > the reply confirms the import]

Now ask Funnel OS to load it into Tantra. It shows you the list name and the row count and waits. Approve it in your own words. Loading a list doesn't send anything. Your campaigns stay as drafts until much later.

[SCREEN: Tantra app > Contacts > lists > Webinar December list > count shows 1,214, contact details blurred]

Then open Tantra and check the count matches. You'll see the same eight fields mapped to the contact record: first name, last name, position, company, website, email, LinkedIn and location. Same number in Funnel OS and in Tantra means nothing was dropped on the way. If they don't match, ask Funnel OS which rows failed and why.

One last rule for later. One person, one sender. Never put the same person on two senders' LinkedIn lists. Funnel OS handles that when it builds your campaigns, but it's good to know why. Two of your people sending to the same prospect looks careless, and LinkedIn notices it too.

That's Module 1 done. Your brief has every decision in it, and your list is clean and sitting in Tantra. Post your Build Score in the group. Next, the infrastructure starts with Sending Domains and DNS in 20 Minutes.

Screen recording shot list

  1. Funnel OS project, the filters prompt and the reply with titles, size, industry, countries, negative filters and row count. About 40 seconds.
  2. The country check reply, ticking each country. About 20 seconds.
  3. Files > upload a demo CSV. Show only the file name and row count. Blur any preview of contents. About 15 seconds.
  4. The clean prompt, upload a demo do not contact file (blurred), and the reply with counts per step. About 50 seconds.
  5. The clean summary table and the removed rows file link. Blur the file contents if opened. About 20 seconds.
  6. The 25 row sample. Blur every name, email and LinkedIn URL; leave titles and company sizes readable. About 40 seconds.
  7. The exclusion word prompt, new counts and new sample (blurred the same way). About 25 seconds.
  8. The clean CSV preview with headers visible and rows blurred. About 10 seconds.
  9. The load prompt, the pause, the typed approval, the import confirmation. About 30 seconds.
  10. Tantra app, Contacts, the new list with the count visible and every contact detail blurred. About 15 seconds.

Build Score checklist

  • [ ] One source picked, filters built from your brief, and cold outreach rules checked for every buyer country (1 point)
  • [ ] List cleaned in the standard order with removed rows kept, and the first 25 rows approved after fixing the filter, not the rows (1 point)
  • [ ] Clean list loaded into Tantra with the count in Tantra matching Funnel OS (1 point)
scripts/V09-sending-domains-and-dns.md

V09: Sending Domains and DNS in 20 Minutes

  • Module: M2 Infrastructure
  • Day: Day 1
  • Length: about 10 minutes (about 1,250 to 1,400 spoken words)
  • Systems on screen: Funnel OS, Cloudflare, Google Admin
  • Before you start: Cloudflare account with billing, a default registrant contact and the registration agreement accepted; the Cloudflare API token connected in Funnel OS (V02 and V03); a paid Google Workspace you are super admin of
  • Done when: every outreach domain shows one MX, exactly one SPF, a DKIM key and DMARC in public DNS, and redirects to your main site

Slides

Slide 1: Why Your Main Domain Stays Out

  • Outreach domains carry the risk
  • Your normal email keeps arriving
  • 2 or 3 domains, never the main one

Slide 2: Pick Names a Buyer Recognises

  • Brand plus try, get, hq, meet
  • .com, no hyphens
  • Funnel OS checks price and availability
  • You approve the buy in your own words

Slide 3: Four Records Per Domain

  • MX pointing at Google
  • Exactly one SPF record
  • DKIM 2048 bit from Google Admin
  • DMARC with p=none

Slide 4: The One SPF Rule

  • Two SPF records fail silently
  • Edit the first, never add a second
  • Merge any new include into it

Slide 5: Mailboxes on the New Domains

  • Secondary domain in Google Workspace
  • 2 or 3 mailboxes per domain
  • First names as usernames
  • Every mailbox is a paid seat

Slide 6: Check What the World Sees

  • One SPF, one DMARC, a DKIM key
  • MX at Google, nothing twice
  • Typing the domain lands on your site

Voice script

[SLIDE 1] You've got a list ready and you want to start sending. But if you send cold email from your main domain and one bad week gets it flagged, your invoices, your client threads and your password resets stop arriving too. That is the mistake this video stops. In the next ten minutes you'll have two or three outreach domains, each with the four records that tell inbox providers your mail is real, and mailboxes on them ready for the next video.

Here's the rule. Outreach goes out from separate domains, never your main one. Two or three of them, and two or three mailboxes on each. If one domain has a bad week, the others keep going and your normal email is untouched.

How many do you need? Do the sum once. Three domains with three mailboxes each is nine mailboxes. Once they're warm, each one sends fifty a day, so that's four hundred and fifty cold emails a day. If your list size from video six needs more than that, add a domain and its mailboxes now, because every new mailbox starts its own warm-up from zero.

[SLIDE 2] Start with names. A prospect should connect the domain to your brand at a glance, so use your brand with a short word in front or behind it: try, get, hq, meet, hello, join. Stick to dot com and no hyphens. If you bought your domains on Day 0 in video two, skip ahead to the records. If not, we do it now.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Suggest six outreach domains for my brand and check price and availability in my Cloudflare account" > the reply shows a table of names, available yes or no, and the price for one year]

I ask Funnel OS for six names and it checks them against my Cloudflare account. It shows me which are free and what each costs. Domains are charged at cost to the card on the Cloudflare account. I pick three.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > the reply says "I will register these three domains for the total shown. Type your approval in your own words to go ahead." > type "Yes, register these three domains for this total"]

Notice what happened. Funnel OS never buys anything on its own. It shows the exact names and the total and waits until I type my approval in my own words. Then it registers each one and watches the status until Cloudflare says it succeeded. A registration can't be refunded once it goes through, so read the names twice before you approve.

If you already own a domain somewhere else and want to use it, that works too. Funnel OS adds it to Cloudflare and gives you two nameservers. You change them at your old registrar, and the domain shows active in Cloudflare once the change has spread. Just never use the domain your business already emails from.

[SCREEN: Email inbox > the Cloudflare registrant verification email > click the verify link > the confirmation page loads]

Now one click only you can do. Cloudflare sends a verification email to your registrant contact. Open it and click the link today. Check spam if it's not there. If you miss the fifteen day window, the registry suspends the domains. One click covers every domain bought with that contact.

[SLIDE 3] Next, the records. Every outreach domain needs four, and each one exactly once. An MX record so mail for the domain goes to Google. An SPF record that says Google may send for this domain. A DKIM key, which is a signature on every email, made in Google Admin at 2048 bit. And a DMARC record set to p equals none, which asks for reports without blocking anything while you start.

The DMARC reports go to an address on a domain you control, not a Gmail address, because a free mailbox can't accept reports for another domain. You won't read these reports every day. They're there so that if something goes wrong in a month, you can see which server sent what.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Write MX, SPF and DMARC for all three outreach domains, and redirect each one to my main site" > the reply lists each record per domain and a note that it read the existing records first]

I ask Funnel OS to write the MX, SPF and DMARC records and the redirect. It reads what's already in each zone first. If a record already plays the same role, it edits that one instead of adding a second.

[SLIDE 4] That matters most for SPF. A domain must have exactly one SPF record. Two of them break authentication and nothing tells you. There is no error message, your mail just lands worse. So the rule is: edit the first, never add a second. If a tool you add later asks for its own SPF line, merge its include into the record you have.

[SCREEN: Cloudflare dashboard > the first outreach domain > DNS > Records > the MX, the TXT record starting v=spf1, the TXT record named _dmarc]

Here they are in Cloudflare. One MX pointing at Google. One TXT record starting v equals spf1. One DMARC record. Funnel OS also added a redirect, so anyone who types the outreach domain into a browser lands on your main site instead of an empty page. A real looking domain with a real site behind it reads better to a prospect who gets curious.

[SLIDE 5] Now the mailboxes. If a partner built your inboxes on Day 0, they set this part up and you can jump to DKIM. If you're building them yourself, you add each outreach domain to your own Google Workspace.

[SCREEN: Google Admin > Account > Domains > Manage domains > Add a domain > type the outreach domain > choose Secondary domain > Add domain and start verification > copy the TXT value]

In Google Admin, open Account, Domains, Manage domains, and click Add a domain. Type the outreach domain and choose Secondary domain. Google gives you a verification value. I paste it to Funnel OS and it adds the TXT record in Cloudflare. Back in Google, click Verify, then Activate Gmail.

[SCREEN: Google Admin > Directory > Users > Add new user > first name, last name, the username on the outreach domain > Add new user]

Then add two or three users on each domain, under Directory, Users, Add new user. Use first names as usernames, a real person's name, because this is who your prospect will see. Every mailbox is a paid seat, so this is where your sending capacity is bought. Once warm, each mailbox sends fifty a day. To grow later, you add mailboxes. You never push one mailbox harder.

[SCREEN: Google Admin > Apps > Google Workspace > Gmail > Authenticate email > Selected domain: the outreach domain > Generate new record > DKIM key bit length 2048 > Prefix selector google > Generate]

Now DKIM. In Google Admin, go to Apps, Google Workspace, Gmail, Authenticate email. Pick the outreach domain, click Generate new record, keep 2048 bit and the selector google, and generate. Copy the value and paste it to Funnel OS. It writes the DKIM record named google underscore domainkey in Cloudflare.

[SCREEN: Google Admin > Authenticate email > Start authentication > status reads Authenticating email with DKIM]

Wait a few minutes, then click Start authentication. If Google shows an error, the record simply hasn't spread yet. DNS usually spreads in minutes and can take up to forty eight hours. Wait and click again. Repeat DKIM for each outreach domain.

[SLIDE 6] Last, check what the world sees, not what you think you typed.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Check public DNS for all three outreach domains" > the reply shows a pass or fail line for MX, SPF, DKIM and DMARC per domain]

Funnel OS reads public DNS for each domain and gives me one line per record. A pass looks like this: one SPF record, a DKIM key, a DMARC record, MX pointing at Google, and nothing listed twice. If any domain shows two SPF lines, fix it now, before a single email goes out. Then type each outreach domain in a browser and check it lands on your main site.

If a line fails, here's what it usually means. SPF missing the Google include: the record was typed by hand with a gap or a typo, so ask Funnel OS to rewrite it. DKIM not found: either it hasn't spread yet, or it went in under the wrong name, and it must be google underscore domainkey. MX looks odd: someone mixed Google's single new record with the old set of five. Keep one style, never both. Fix the record, wait ten minutes, and run the check again until every line passes.

One more thing to know before tomorrow. New mailboxes need at least fourteen days of warm-up before cold email starts. That clock starts when they connect to Tantra, which is the next video. So the sooner you finish these records, the sooner your cold email date arrives. Meanwhile the course runs LinkedIn, calendar invites and your warm list, so nothing waits on email.

Your next step: open Funnel OS, run the public DNS check and make sure every line passes. Then watch Connect Your Mailboxes to Tantra.

Screen recording shot list

  1. Funnel OS chat: ask for six outreach domain names with price and availability, show the table. About 40 seconds
  2. Funnel OS approval prompt, type the approval in your own words, show the registration status reading succeeded. About 40 seconds
  3. Email inbox: open the Cloudflare verification email and click the link. Blur the registrant name, address and phone number. About 20 seconds
  4. Funnel OS chat: ask for MX, SPF, DMARC and the redirect; show the reply noting it read existing records first. About 30 seconds
  5. Cloudflare dashboard, DNS, Records for one outreach domain: MX, one v=spf1 TXT, the _dmarc TXT, the redirect rule. Blur the account ID in the address bar. About 40 seconds
  6. Google Admin, Account, Domains, Manage domains, Add a domain, Secondary domain, the TXT value, Verify, Activate Gmail. Blur the verification value. About 60 seconds
  7. Google Admin, Directory, Users, Add new user on the outreach domain. Blur any password field. About 40 seconds
  8. Google Admin, Apps, Google Workspace, Gmail, Authenticate email, Generate new record at 2048 bit, selector google. Blur the generated key. Then Start authentication. About 60 seconds
  9. Funnel OS chat: public DNS check with a pass line per record per domain. About 30 seconds
  10. Browser: type an outreach domain, land on the main site. About 15 seconds

Build Score checklist

  • [ ] 2 or 3 outreach domains registered, none of them your main domain (1 point)
  • [ ] Registry verification email clicked (1 point)
  • [ ] Each domain has one MX, exactly one SPF, a 2048 bit DKIM key and DMARC p=none, and 2 or 3 mailboxes (1 point)
  • [ ] Public DNS check passes for every domain and each one redirects to your main site (1 point)
scripts/V10-connect-your-mailboxes-to-tantra.md

V10: Connect Your Mailboxes to Tantra

  • Module: M2 Infrastructure
  • Day: Day 1
  • Length: about 14 minutes (about 1,750 to 1,900 spoken words)
  • Systems on screen: Google Cloud, Google Admin, Tantra app
  • Before you start: V09 done (outreach domains with records and 2 or 3 mailboxes each); you are a super admin of the Google Workspace that holds the mailboxes; signed in to Tantra on the Pro plan
  • Done when: every outreach mailbox shows Connected and Gmail capable in Tantra, is set to Cold, and your cold email start date is written in the Funnel Brief

Slides

Slide 1: One Service Account, Every Mailbox

  • Tantra acts for your mailboxes
  • No signing in to each inbox
  • Four screens, about 20 minutes

Slide 2: The Five Steps

  • Cloud project, Gmail and Calendar APIs
  • Service account, JSON key into Tantra
  • Delegation in Google Admin
  • Verify each inbox, set to Cold

Slide 3: The Key Is a Password

  • Downloads once, keep it offline
  • Never in a chat or an email
  • Only place it goes: Tantra

Slide 4: Delegation, Two Fields

  • Client ID: the long number
  • Never the service account email
  • Scopes: the full line Tantra lists

Slide 5: When It Does Not Connect

  • unauthorized_client: email pasted, use the number
  • Calendar only: Gmail scopes missing
  • Create key greyed out: key policy
  • Still failing: wait up to an hour

Slide 6: Your Cold Email Date

  • Connected date plus 14 days
  • 20 a day while warming
  • 50 a day per mailbox once warm

Voice script

[SLIDE 1] This is the step where most people stall. You've got mailboxes, you've got Tantra, and between them sits a Google screen full of words like service account and domain wide delegation. People get one field wrong, the inbox says disconnected, and they lose a day guessing. So I'm going to go slowly, screen by screen, field by field, and show you the four errors people hit and what each one means. By the end, every mailbox is connected and warming.

Before you start, open three browser tabs: the Google Cloud console, Google Admin and Tantra. Open a blank note too. You'll copy one long number into it, and it's easier to have it sitting there than to hunt for it later. Set aside twenty minutes without interruptions. Most people who get stuck here got interrupted halfway through and came back to the wrong tab.

Here's what we're building. Instead of signing in to every mailbox one by one, you create one Google service account. Think of it as a robot user in your Google organisation. You give it permission, in Google Admin, to send and read on behalf of the mailboxes in your Workspace. Then you hand its key to Tantra. One key, every mailbox.

[SLIDE 2] Five steps. A Google Cloud project with the Gmail and Calendar APIs switched on. A service account with a JSON key. The key uploaded in Tantra. The delegation in Google Admin. And then each inbox verified in Tantra and set to Cold so it warms up.

Sign in to Google as a super admin of the Workspace that holds your outreach mailboxes. Not a personal Gmail account. A personal account has no organisation to hold the project, and nothing below will work.

[SCREEN: Google Cloud console > project picker at the top > New project > Project name "tantra-mailboxes" > Organisation: your company > Location: your company > Create]

Open the Google Cloud console. Click the project picker at the top, then New project. Name it something you'll recognise, like tantra mailboxes. Under organisation, pick your company. If it says No organisation, you're signed in with the wrong account. Click Create, and make sure the new project is selected at the top before you go on.

That last bit catches people. Google Cloud remembers the last project you used, so if you've ever opened the console before, you might be about to switch on APIs in some old project. Look at the name next to the Google Cloud logo. It should say the project you just made.

[SCREEN: Google Cloud console > APIs and Services > Library > search "Gmail API" > Enable > back to Library > search "Google Calendar API" > Enable]

Now the APIs. Open APIs and Services, then Library. Search Gmail API, open it, click Enable. Go back to the Library, search Google Calendar API, and click Enable. Both must be on. Gmail lets Tantra send and read replies. Calendar is what calendar invites use later.

[SCREEN: Google Cloud console > IAM and Admin > Service Accounts > Create service account > Service account name "tantra-mailboxes" > Create and continue > skip the optional roles > Done]

Next, the service account. Open IAM and Admin, then Service Accounts, then Create service account. Give it a name. Click Create and continue. Google offers you roles. Skip them, they're optional and you don't need any. Click Done.

[SCREEN: Service Accounts list > click the new account > Details tab > the Unique ID field, a long number > copy it into a note]

Click the account you just made. On the Details tab you'll see two things that look important. There's an email address ending in gserviceaccount dot com. And there's a Unique ID, a long number, about twenty one digits. That number is your Client ID. Copy the number into a note. Hold on to this, because the most common mistake in this whole video is pasting the email where the number should go.

[SLIDE 3] Now the key. This file is a password. Whoever holds it can act as your mailboxes. It downloads once, and the only place it ever goes is Tantra. Never into a chat, never into an email, never into a shared folder.

[SCREEN: Service account > Keys tab > Add key > Create new key > Key type JSON > Create > the browser downloads the file]

Open the Keys tab, click Add key, Create new key, choose JSON, and click Create. The file downloads.

If Create new key is greyed out, or Google says key creation is blocked by an organisation policy, that's the newer key policy. Workspaces created after the third of May 2024 block service account keys by default. Here's the fix.

[SCREEN: Google Cloud console > select the organisation in the picker > IAM and Admin > IAM > grant yourself the role Organization Policy Administrator > Save]

Switch the project picker to your organisation itself. Open IAM, find your own account and give yourself the role Organization Policy Administrator. Being super admin is not enough on its own, you need this role to change the policy.

[SCREEN: IAM and Admin > Organization Policies > search "disableServiceAccountKeyCreation" > open it > Manage policy > Override parent's policy > Add a rule > Enforcement Off > Set policy > repeat for the managed version of the same policy]

Then open Organization Policies and search for disable service account key creation. You'll see two: one plain and one marked managed. Open each one, click Manage policy, choose Override parent's policy, add a rule with Enforcement set to Off, and click Set policy. Do both. Go back to your project and create the key again. It works now.

[SCREEN: Tantra app > Connectors > Google Service Accounts > Upload Service Accounts > drop the JSON file > Upload > the list shows the service account]

Now into Tantra. Open Connectors, then Google Service Accounts. Drop the JSON file into Upload Service Accounts and click Upload. The service account appears in the list. That's the only place the key lives. Delete the downloaded copy from your Downloads folder once it shows here.

And if that file ever ends up somewhere it shouldn't, in an email or a shared drive, don't panic and don't ignore it. Go back to the service account's Keys tab in Google Cloud, delete that key, create a new one, and upload the new one in Tantra. The old file stops working the moment you delete it.

[SLIDE 4] Now the step that decides whether any of this works: the delegation. You're telling your Workspace, this service account may act for my users, with these permissions. It has two fields, and both have a trap.

[SCREEN: Google Admin > Security > Access and data control > API controls > Manage domain wide delegation > Add new]

Open Google Admin. Go to Security, then Access and data control, then API controls. Scroll down and click Manage domain wide delegation. Then click Add new.

[SCREEN: Add a new client ID dialog > Client ID field: paste the long number > OAuth scopes field: paste the full scope line copied from the Tantra screen > Authorize]

The first field is Client ID. Paste the long number from your note. Not the email. The dialog accepts the email without complaint, and then nothing connects. Use the number.

The second field is OAuth scopes. Tantra lists the exact scopes it needs. Copy the whole line from Tantra and paste it here, all on one line, separated by commas with no spaces. There are six: two for Calendar, two for Gmail, which are send and modify, and two that read the user's email address and profile. Then click Authorize.

Why six and not two? Gmail send lets Tantra send from the mailbox. Gmail modify lets it read replies and mark them, so replies land in your Tantra inbox and warm-up can do its job. The two calendar scopes are for invites. And the two that read the email address and profile let Tantra check it's talking to the right person. Take any of them out and something breaks later, usually on the day you need it.

Here's the trap in this field. If the Gmail scopes are missing, Tantra can still reach the calendar, so the mailbox looks half connected. Tantra shows it as Calendar only. A Calendar only mailbox cannot send a single email. So paste the full line, don't type it.

One more thing on where you do this. The delegation goes in the Workspace that holds the mailboxes. If a partner built your inboxes in their own Workspace, you don't click this. You send them two things: the long number and the scope line. Never the email of the service account, and never the key file.

[SCREEN: Tantra app > Accounts > Verify and Add Accounts > paste every outreach mailbox address, one per line > Verify & Add]

Now let's prove it. In Tantra, open Accounts, then Verify and Add Accounts. Paste every outreach mailbox address, one per line, and click Verify and Add.

[SCREEN: Tantra app > Accounts list > each mailbox shows Connected, with no Calendar only label]

This is what working looks like. Every mailbox says Connected, and none of them says Calendar only. That's your proof. Not the Google screen saying Authorized, this one.

Read each row carefully. The address should be exactly the mailbox you created, with no typo. The status should say Connected. And there should be no Calendar only label anywhere in the row. If you have nine mailboxes, count nine Connected rows. One missing row usually means one address was pasted with a space in it.

[SLIDE 5] If you don't see that, here's how to read what you see instead.

Disconnected with unauthorized client means the delegation doesn't match the key. Almost always, the email went into the Client ID field. Go back to Manage domain wide delegation, delete that entry, and add it again with the long number.

Calendar only means the Gmail scopes are missing. Open the delegation entry, click Edit, paste the full scope line again, and Authorize.

No service accounts uploaded means the key never reached Tantra. Upload it again under Connectors.

And if everything looks right and it still says Disconnected, wait. Delegation can take a few minutes, and sometimes up to an hour, to reach every Google server. Make a coffee, then click Verify again. Don't rebuild everything in a panic at minute five. That just creates a second service account and confuses the picture.

Two more you might see. Create new key greyed out is the key policy we fixed earlier, so go back to Organization Policies. And if the project shows No organisation, you created it with a personal Gmail account. Sign out, sign in as your Workspace super admin and start the project again.

[SCREEN: Tantra app > Accounts > open the first mailbox > set warm-up status to Cold > Save > repeat for each mailbox]

Once every mailbox is Connected, open each one and set it to Cold. Cold tells Tantra to start warming the mailbox: it sends and receives a small number of real looking emails each day, so the inbox providers learn that this mailbox is a normal sender. Over the fourteen days you'll see the warm-up numbers climb in Tantra. Leave the mailboxes alone while that happens. Don't send a hundred emails from one by hand to test it. That undoes the very thing warm-up is building. You can also ask Funnel OS to set every connected mailbox to Cold from the chat. It will show you the list first and wait for your yes.

[SLIDE 6] Now write down the date. Your cold email start date is today's connected date plus at least fourteen days. While warming, a mailbox sends twenty a day. Once warm, fifty a day per mailbox. If you want more volume later, you add mailboxes. You never push one mailbox harder. And leave warm-up running even after your campaigns go live.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "All outreach mailboxes are connected and set to Cold today. Record the warm-up start and my earliest cold email date." > the reply shows the date fourteen days out, saved in the Funnel Brief]

I tell Funnel OS the mailboxes connected today and it saves the earliest cold email date in my Funnel Brief. When we build the cold email campaign later, it won't let it start before that date. Between now and then, the course runs LinkedIn, calendar invites, WhatsApp to people who opted in, and your warm list. So nothing waits on email.

If you got stuck anywhere in this video, post a screenshot in the WhatsApp group with the key and IDs covered, and say which screen you're on. A coach will tell you which field to fix.

Your next step: get every mailbox to Connected, set each one to Cold, and save the date. Then watch Calendar Invite Senders.

Screen recording shot list

  1. Google Cloud console, project picker, New project, organisation set to the company (show the No organisation warning case briefly). About 45 seconds
  2. APIs and Services, Library, enable Gmail API and Google Calendar API. About 45 seconds
  3. IAM and Admin, Service Accounts, Create service account, skip roles, Done. About 40 seconds
  4. Service account Details tab: highlight the Unique ID and the email side by side. Blur both values except a few digits of the number. About 30 seconds
  5. Keys tab, Add key, Create new key, JSON, Create. Never open the file. Blur the file name and key ID. About 30 seconds
  6. Organisation level: IAM, grant Organization Policy Administrator; Organization Policies, both key creation policies set to Override parent's policy with Enforcement Off. Blur the organisation ID and admin email. About 90 seconds
  7. Tantra app, Connectors, Google Service Accounts, upload the key, the account in the list. Blur the service account email. About 40 seconds
  8. Google Admin, Security, Access and data control, API controls, Manage domain wide delegation, Add new, paste the Client ID and the scope line, Authorize. Blur the Client ID. About 75 seconds
  9. Tantra app, Accounts, Verify and Add Accounts, paste addresses, Verify & Add, the list showing Connected. Blur every mailbox address. About 60 seconds
  10. Error states for the slide 5 section: a Disconnected row with unauthorized_client and a Calendar only row (record on a test account). Blur addresses. About 45 seconds
  11. Tantra app, set each mailbox to Cold. Blur addresses. About 30 seconds
  12. Funnel OS chat: record the warm-up start and the cold email date. About 30 seconds

Build Score checklist

  • [ ] Google Cloud project under your organisation with Gmail API and Google Calendar API enabled (1 point)
  • [ ] Service account created and its JSON key uploaded in Tantra, with no copy left anywhere else (1 point)
  • [ ] Domain wide delegation added with the numeric Client ID and the full scope line (1 point)
  • [ ] Every outreach mailbox shows Connected in Tantra and none shows Calendar only (1 point)
  • [ ] Every mailbox set to Cold and the cold email start date (connected date plus 14 days) saved in the Funnel Brief (1 point)
scripts/V11-calendar-invite-senders.md

V11: Calendar Invite Senders

  • Module: M2 Infrastructure
  • Day: Day 1
  • Length: about 11 minutes (about 1,350 to 1,500 spoken words)
  • Systems on screen: Google Admin, Google Cloud, Tantra app, Funnel OS
  • Before you start: V10 done (you know the service account and delegation screens); the calendar Workspace started on Day 0 in V02, or a card ready to start it now
  • Done when: every calendar sender shows Connected in Tantra with a daily limit of 20, and the Workspace creation date is saved in the Funnel Brief

Slides

Slide 1: Why Calendar Invites Get Seen

  • The invite sits in their calendar
  • A real meeting style title
  • Growth Idea: 98,000 reached by invite

Slide 2: Its Own Domain, Its Own Workspace

  • Never your main domain
  • Never a cold email domain
  • One user per sender
  • If it is flagged, nothing else stops

Slide 3: Raise Google's Limits

  • End the trial
  • Business Starter, Flexible plan
  • Prepay $100

Slide 4: Connect Each Sender

  • One service account per sender
  • Delegation in this Workspace
  • Verify in Tantra: Connected

Slide 5: The Ramp

  • 20 a day per sender until day 60
  • Then 50 a day
  • Never above 200
  • Day 0 is the Workspace creation date

Voice script

[SLIDE 1] Your cold email can sit unread in a promotions tab for days. A calendar invite doesn't. It lands in the place your buyer checks every morning, with your event's date and time already in it. That's why we build calendar invite senders on Day 1, and it's why this channel carries most of your traffic in the first two weeks while your mailboxes warm up. In this video you'll set up the senders that send those invites, and connect them to Tantra the right way.

This channel works when it's built carefully. Growth Idea, Shweta's coaching group in the UK, ran their best webinar on the twenty eighth of August 2025. It reached 99,859 people, and 98,000 of them were reached by calendar invite. That's the channel we're setting up today.

Do the sum before you build. Each new sender sends twenty invites a day. Three senders is sixty a day, three hundred over a working week. Your list size from video six tells you how many people you need to reach before your event. If sixty a day won't get there in time, add senders now, because every sender's ramp starts on the day its Workspace is created.

[SLIDE 2] First rule: calendar invites go out from their own domain and their own Google Workspace. Not your main domain, and not one of your cold email domains. If this Workspace gets flagged, your cold email and your normal email keep working. Keeping things apart is cheap insurance.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Suggest a domain for my calendar invite senders and check the price" > the reply shows two names with price > type "Yes, register the first one for this price"]

If you didn't buy the calendar domain on Day 0, ask Funnel OS now. It checks names in Cloudflare and waits for your approval in your own words before it buys. Then it writes the same records you set up in the last video: one MX, exactly one SPF and DMARC. You'll add DKIM from Google Admin in a minute, same as before.

[SCREEN: Google Workspace sign up > business name > number of employees > "Yes, I have one I can use" > type the calendar domain > username for the first sender > Business Starter]

Now the Workspace. Sign up for Google Workspace Business Starter on the new domain. When it asks whether you have a domain, choose yes, you already have one, so Google doesn't sell you another. Create the first user as one of your senders. Google gives you a verification value. Paste it to Funnel OS and it adds the TXT record in Cloudflare. Then click Verify.

[SCREEN: Google Admin > Directory > Users > Add new user > a second and third sender]

Add one user per sender. Two or three is a good start. Use real first and last names. These people appear as the organiser of every invite, so pick the people who actually run your event, or the people whose names your buyers should see.

Then make each sender look like a real person, because that's what they are. Sign in as each one, add a profile photo and the full name in their Google account, and give them a short signature. An invite from a blank grey circle with no name looks like spam to the person reading it, and to Google.

[SLIDE 3] Next, two clicks that most people skip, and they matter. A new Workspace on its free trial gets Google's lowest sending limits, whatever else you do. So end the trial, and prepay.

[SCREEN: Google Admin > Billing > Subscriptions > the Business Starter subscription > End trial > Flexible Plan > confirm]

In Google Admin, open Billing, then Subscriptions. Click your subscription, then End trial. Choose Business Starter on the Flexible plan and confirm.

[SCREEN: Google Admin > Billing > Payment accounts > the payment account > Make a payment > Amount 100 > Make a payment]

Then open Billing, Payment accounts, your account, and click Make a payment. Pay one hundred dollars, or one hundred euros if you're billed in euros. A paid history is part of how Google decides how much you're allowed to send. Write down the date. That's day zero of your ramp.

[SCREEN: Google Admin > Apps > Google Workspace > Gmail > Authenticate email > the calendar domain > Generate new record > 2048 > Start authentication]

While you're in Google Admin, set up DKIM for this domain, exactly like the last video. Authenticate email, Generate new record at 2048 bit, give the record to Funnel OS, then Start authentication.

So this domain ends up with the same four records as your outreach domains: one MX, exactly one SPF, DKIM and DMARC. Ask Funnel OS to run the public DNS check on it before you go on. Calendar invites need these records in place before the first send, just like email.

[SLIDE 4] Now connect each sender to Tantra. You've done this once already, so it'll go faster. The difference here: each sender gets its own service account, and the delegation goes into this new Workspace, signed in as its super admin.

[SCREEN: Google Cloud console > project picker > New project under the calendar Workspace's organisation > enable Gmail API and Google Calendar API > IAM and Admin > Service Accounts > Create service account for the first sender > Keys > Add key > JSON]

In Google Cloud, signed in as the admin of the calendar Workspace, create a project, switch on the Gmail API and the Google Calendar API, and create one service account per sender. Copy each one's Unique ID, that long number, and create a JSON key for each. If key creation is blocked, it's the same policy from the last video. Fix it the same way.

[SCREEN: Tantra app > Connectors > Google Service Accounts > Upload Service Accounts > drop every sender's JSON file > Upload]

In Tantra, under Connectors, Google Service Accounts, upload every key. You can drop several files at once.

[SCREEN: Google Admin of the calendar Workspace > Security > Access and data control > API controls > Manage domain wide delegation > Add new > Client ID: the long number > OAuth scopes: the full line from Tantra > Authorize > repeat per sender]

Then in this Workspace's Google Admin, under Security, Access and data control, API controls, Manage domain wide delegation, add one entry per sender. The long number in Client ID, never the email. The full scope line from Tantra in OAuth scopes. Authorize. A calendar sender can still send invites with only the calendar scopes, but paste the full line anyway. It keeps every sender the same and saves you a puzzle later.

[SCREEN: Tantra app > Accounts > Verify and Add Accounts > paste the sender addresses > Verify & Add > each shows Connected]

Back in Tantra, Accounts, Verify and Add Accounts. Paste your sender addresses and click Verify and Add. Check each row the same way as last time: the right address, Connected, and nothing else in the status. Three senders, three Connected rows. If one is missing, it's almost always a typo in the address or a key that didn't upload. Each one should show Connected. If one says unauthorized client, the email went in where the number should be. If it's still Disconnected and everything looks right, give it up to an hour.

[SLIDE 5] Now the ramp. This is the part people break by being impatient. Each sender sends twenty invites a day until day sixty, counted from the day the Workspace was created and paid. After day sixty, fifty a day. Never above two hundred, ever. More reach comes from more senders, not from one sender going faster.

The first weeks also help if each sender behaves like a normal employee. Have each one accept two or three internal calendar events a day from a teammate in the same Workspace, and join a short Google Meet now and then. It's a few minutes of work and it gives Google a normal history for every sender before the cold invites start.

[SCREEN: Tantra app > Accounts > open each sender > daily invite limit 20 > warm-up status Cold > Save]

Open each sender in Tantra and set its daily invite limit to twenty and its status to Cold. Or ask Funnel OS to do it for every sender. It shows the list and waits for your yes.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "My calendar Workspace was created and paid on this date. Save it and remind me on day 60 to raise every sender to 50 a day." > the reply shows the day 60 date saved in the Funnel Brief]

Then tell Funnel OS the date the Workspace was created and paid. Not today's date if you set it up on Day 0. The real creation date. It saves it in the Funnel Brief and works out your day sixty, so it can remind you to raise each sender to fifty. Get this date wrong and the whole ramp is off.

One thing to know about what happens next. When someone says yes or maybe to your invite, Tantra sends that RSVP to your CRM through the webhook you'll set up in the next video, and the person is registered for your event. And Tantra's calendar campaign doesn't set reminders on the invite. Each guest gets their own Google Calendar default reminder, and your own reminders come from the CRM.

Two habits for later, so you hear them now. When you build the campaign, the counters can read zero for a while after it starts. Never build a second campaign because a counter reads zero. That's how people double send to the same list. And send your invites eight to twelve days before the event, with a meeting style title and the time zone in the first line. We write those in the Calendar Invite Campaigns video.

Your next step: get every calendar sender to Connected at twenty a day, and save the Workspace date. Then watch LinkedIn and WhatsApp in Tantra.

Screen recording shot list

  1. Funnel OS chat: suggest and approve the calendar domain. About 30 seconds
  2. Google Workspace sign up on the calendar domain, "I have one I can use", Business Starter. Blur the business address and phone number. About 60 seconds
  3. Google Admin, Directory, Users, add two more senders. Blur passwords. About 40 seconds
  4. Google Admin, Billing, Subscriptions, End trial, Flexible plan. Blur the payment card. About 30 seconds
  5. Google Admin, Billing, Payment accounts, Make a payment of 100. Blur the card and account number. About 30 seconds
  6. Google Admin, Authenticate email, DKIM at 2048, Start authentication. Blur the key. About 30 seconds
  7. Google Cloud: project, APIs, one service account per sender, JSON keys. Blur every Unique ID and email. About 75 seconds
  8. Tantra app, Connectors, Google Service Accounts, upload several keys. Blur the service account emails. About 30 seconds
  9. Google Admin, Manage domain wide delegation, one entry per sender. Blur Client IDs. About 60 seconds
  10. Tantra app, Accounts, Verify and Add Accounts, every sender Connected. Blur sender addresses. About 40 seconds
  11. Tantra app, each sender at 20 a day and Cold. Blur addresses. About 30 seconds
  12. Funnel OS chat: save the Workspace creation date and the day 60 reminder. About 30 seconds

Build Score checklist

  • [ ] Calendar invite domain and Workspace separate from your main and cold email domains, trial ended, $100 prepaid (1 point)
  • [ ] Every calendar sender shows Connected in Tantra (1 point)
  • [ ] Every sender set to 20 a day and Cold, and the Workspace creation date and day 60 saved in the Funnel Brief (1 point)
scripts/V12-linkedin-and-whatsapp-in-tantra.md

V12: LinkedIn and WhatsApp in Tantra

  • Module: M2 Infrastructure
  • Day: Day 1
  • Length: about 9 minutes (about 1,100 to 1,250 spoken words)
  • Systems on screen: Tantra app, Funnel OS
  • Before you start: signed in to Tantra on the Pro plan; your LinkedIn login and phone for the sign-in code; the phone whose WhatsApp number your buyers should hear from; WhatsApp templates submitted on Day 0 (V02)
  • Done when: LinkedIn and WhatsApp both show running in Tantra, the Tantra API key is connected in Funnel OS, and reply events reach your CRM

Slides

Slide 1: The Two Channels That Need No Warm-Up

  • LinkedIn runs from day 3
  • WhatsApp for people who opted in
  • Both connected inside Tantra

Slide 2: LinkedIn Pace

  • 15 to 20 requests a day
  • At most 200 a week
  • Note under 300 characters
  • No second automation tool, ever

Slide 3: WhatsApp Rules

  • Opted-in contacts only
  • STOP in every message
  • One link per message
  • US numbers get a Utility message

Slide 4: Connect Funnel OS to Tantra

  • Connectors, API Keys, Create API Key
  • Expiry empty, shown once
  • Into Funnel OS connections, never the chat

Slide 5: Replies Reach Your CRM

  • One webhook for every event
  • Turn LinkedIn and WhatsApp on
  • A reply becomes a task, same day

Voice script

[SLIDE 1] Your mailboxes need fourteen days before they can send cold email. If that's your only channel, you sit and wait for two weeks with nothing to show. You don't have to. LinkedIn needs no warm-up, and WhatsApp reaches people who already said yes to hearing from you. In this video you'll connect both inside Tantra, connect Funnel OS to Tantra so it can build your campaigns, and make sure every reply lands in your CRM.

[SCREEN: Tantra app > Channels > LinkedIn > Connect > sign in with your LinkedIn email and password > enter the code LinkedIn sends to your phone > the account shows syncing]

Start with LinkedIn. In Tantra, open Channels, choose LinkedIn and click Connect. You sign in with your own LinkedIn login, inside Tantra, and enter the code LinkedIn sends to your phone. Only you can do this step. Nobody else should hold your LinkedIn password, and Funnel OS can't connect it for you.

[SCREEN: Tantra app > Channels > the LinkedIn account card > status changes from syncing to running]

Tantra then syncs your account. Wait until the status changes from syncing to running. That can take a few minutes. Don't start anything while it still says syncing.

[SLIDE 2] Now the pace. LinkedIn restricts accounts that move too fast, and losing your account means losing your profile, so this matters more than any message you write. Start at fifteen to twenty connection requests a day. Never more than two hundred a week from one account. Raise a little each week, only while people keep accepting and LinkedIn shows no warning. If you see a warning, drop back to the last pace that was fine.

Your connection note stays under three hundred characters. After someone accepts, they get messages on days zero, three, seven, twelve and twenty five. Requests nobody answers get withdrawn after fourteen days. We write all of that in the campaigns video. Today it's just the connection.

While you're here, open your own LinkedIn profile and look at it the way a prospect will. Every request sends people to it. A clear photo, a headline that says who you help and how, and a recent post or two. If your headline still says what you did three jobs ago, change it today. It costs nothing and it's the first thing people check before they accept.

[SCREEN: Tantra app > Channels > the LinkedIn account > the daily limits shown on the account]

Open the account and look at its limits. Tantra holds the pace per account, across every campaign. Two campaigns from one account share the same pace, they don't add up.

And one hard rule. Never run a second LinkedIn automation tool on the same profile. If you have an old one connected, disconnect it today. Two tools pushing one profile is the fastest way to a restricted account.

[SLIDE 3] Now WhatsApp. The rule here is simple and it's not negotiable. You only message people who opted in. That means they ticked the WhatsApp box on your form, or they're existing contacts who gave you their number to hear from you. A number messaged without opt-in gets reported and blocked, and then your reminders stop reaching the people who did want them.

[SCREEN: Tantra app > Channels > WhatsApp > Connect > a QR code appears > on the phone: WhatsApp > Settings > Linked devices > Link a device > scan the code > the account shows syncing, then running]

In Tantra, open Channels, choose WhatsApp and click Connect. A QR code appears. On the phone whose number your buyers should hear from, open WhatsApp, go to Settings, Linked devices, Link a device, and scan the code. Wait for the sync to finish and the status to read running. Keep that phone switched on and connected, at least for the first day. Replies come to that WhatsApp on that phone, and you answer them as yourself, like any other message.

Three rules for every WhatsApp message you'll ever send from this. Every message says how to stop, with the word STOP in it. One link per message, because people read WhatsApp on a phone between other things. And plain statements of fact: the session starts tomorrow at eleven, here's your link. No pressure words.

[SCREEN: the WhatsApp template list where you submitted them on Day 0 > each template shows its review status]

Messages that start a conversation from your CRM sequences, like reminders, use the templates you submitted to Meta for review on Day 0. Check their status now. Approved means ready. Rejected means rewrite and resubmit today, because review can take hours or days. One more thing to know: marketing templates don't deliver to US numbers. So your US contacts get a Utility template, a plain reminder of something they signed up for.

[SLIDE 4] Now connect Funnel OS to Tantra, so it can draft your campaigns, read your accounts and check your numbers.

[SCREEN: Tantra app > left sidebar > Connectors > API Keys > Create API Key > name "Funnel OS" > leave expiry empty > Create > the copy icon]

In Tantra's left sidebar open Connectors, then API Keys, and click Create API Key. Name it Funnel OS so you'll recognise it. Leave the expiry empty. If you set one, everything quietly stops working on that day. Click Create, then copy the key with the copy icon. Tantra shows it once. If you lose it, delete it with the bin icon and make a new one. One key per workspace. Make it in the Tantra workspace where your mailboxes and senders live, or Funnel OS will see an empty account.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS > Connections > Tantra > paste the key in the key field > Save > the connection shows a green check]

In Funnel OS, open Connections, choose Tantra and paste the key into the key field. Not into a chat message. The connection screen keeps it out of your conversation history. Save it, and Funnel OS checks it by reading your accounts.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Check my Tantra connection and list my accounts" > the reply lists the outreach mailboxes as Connected and Cold, the calendar senders at 20 a day, LinkedIn running and WhatsApp running]

Then ask it to list your accounts. You should see everything from the last three videos in one reply: your outreach mailboxes, Connected and Cold. Your calendar senders at twenty a day. LinkedIn running. WhatsApp running. If any line is wrong, this is the moment to fix it, not on launch day.

[SLIDE 5] Last piece. When someone replies, accepts your request or says yes to a calendar invite, that has to reach your CRM, or it gets lost in an inbox. Tantra sends these as events through a webhook.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Create a Tantra webhook to my Tantra CRM for RSVPs, replies, bounces, unsubscribes, LinkedIn acceptances and replies, and WhatsApp replies" > the reply shows the webhook with every trigger listed and asks for approval > type "Yes, create it"]

Ask Funnel OS to create one Tantra webhook pointing at your CRM, with every event on: calendar RSVPs, email replies, bounces and unsubscribes, LinkedIn acceptances and replies, and WhatsApp replies. Check the list it shows you. LinkedIn and WhatsApp events are off unless you switch them on, so make sure both are in the list. Then approve it.

To check it, reply to one of your own test messages from a second account, or accept a test calendar invite. Within a few minutes the event shows on that contact in your CRM. If nothing arrives, ask Funnel OS to read the webhook back and check the address and the triggers.

From now on, a reply turns into a task in your CRM for the same day, and a WhatsApp STOP turns WhatsApp off for that person. You answer every reply the same day with your booking link. That's the daily habit the whole course runs on.

Your next step: get LinkedIn and WhatsApp to running, connect the key in Funnel OS and switch on the webhook. Then watch Your CRM Pipeline, Stages and Fields.

Screen recording shot list

  1. Tantra app, Channels, LinkedIn, Connect, sign in. Blur the LinkedIn email, password field and code. About 45 seconds
  2. Tantra app, the LinkedIn account card going from syncing to running, then its limits. About 30 seconds
  3. Tantra app, Channels, WhatsApp, Connect, the QR code (blur it), then the phone screen: Settings, Linked devices, Link a device. Blur the phone number. About 45 seconds
  4. Template list with review status. Blur any sample contact names or numbers. About 20 seconds
  5. Tantra app, Connectors, API Keys, Create API Key, expiry empty, the copy icon. Blur the key entirely. About 40 seconds
  6. Funnel OS, Connections, Tantra, paste and save. Blur the key field. About 25 seconds
  7. Funnel OS chat: list accounts. Blur mailbox and sender addresses. About 30 seconds
  8. Funnel OS chat: create the webhook, show the trigger list with LinkedIn and WhatsApp on, approve. Blur the webhook address token. About 40 seconds

Build Score checklist

  • [ ] LinkedIn shows running in Tantra and no other automation tool is connected to the profile (1 point)
  • [ ] WhatsApp shows running in Tantra and template review status checked (1 point)
  • [ ] Tantra API key connected in Funnel OS and the webhook to the CRM created with LinkedIn and WhatsApp events on (1 point)
scripts/V13-your-crm-pipeline-stages-and-fields.md

V13: Your CRM Pipeline, Stages and Fields

  • Module: M3 CRM and Money
  • Day: Day 1
  • Length: about 11 minutes (about 1,350 to 1,500 spoken words)
  • Systems on screen: Tantra CRM, Funnel OS
  • Before you start: Funnel Brief approved with your offer, price and funnel (V04 to V07); Tantra CRM workspace open at crm.usetantra.com; the CRM connected in Funnel OS (V03); the Tantra webhook created (V12)
  • Done when: a test contact registers in dry run and lands in Form Filled with the Registered tag and its source filled, without you touching the deal

Slides

Slide 1: Tools Report Events, Events Move Deals

  • Every stage has a trigger
  • Moved a deal by hand? Not wired
  • The pipeline is your scoreboard

Slide 2: The Eleven Stages

  • Form Filled, Call Scheduled, No Show
  • Qualified, Unqualified, Call Held, Follow-Up
  • Proposal Sent, Proposal Accepted
  • Payment Completed, Onboarding

Slide 3: Four Deal Fields

  • Offer
  • Event date
  • Source
  • Readiness score

Slide 4: Tags for the Event

  • Registered, Attended
  • No-Show, Replay Watched
  • Tags branch the follow-ups

Slide 5: Test in Dry Run

  • One test contact, your own address
  • Nothing goes out
  • Read where it landed

Voice script

[SLIDE 1] Here's a scene I see all the time. Someone books a call, pays, or misses the webinar, and the CRM says nothing about it, because nobody remembered to drag the card across. A week later you're chasing a person who already paid, and you've lost track of the person who didn't show up. In this video you'll build a pipeline for your own offer where that can't happen, because no deal ever needs to be moved by hand.

The rule for the whole course is one line. Tools report events, events move deals. A form submit is an event. A booking is an event. A payment webhook is an event. Each event moves the deal to the right stage on its own. So if you ever catch yourself moving a deal by hand, that's not a chore to do faster. It's a step that isn't wired, and you write it down and fix it.

[SCREEN: Tantra CRM > Pipelines > the starter pipeline from your workspace template > its stage columns]

Your CRM workspace already has a starter pipeline from the template. Open Pipelines and look at it. We're going to make it yours: named for your offer, with the fields your offer needs.

One pipeline per offer you sell on a call. If you also sell a smaller product at checkout, it can live in the same pipeline, told apart by the offer field. What you don't want is five pipelines nobody looks at. One board you open every morning beats five you don't.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Set up my Tantra CRM pipeline for the offer in my Funnel Brief: the default stages, deal fields for offer, event date, source and readiness score, and the event tags" > the reply shows the pipeline name, every stage with what moves a deal into it, the fields and the tags, and asks for approval]

I ask Funnel OS to set up the pipeline for the offer in my Funnel Brief. Look at what it gives back before it builds anything. Every stage comes with a line that says which event moves a deal into it. That line is the whole point.

If a stage has no event that moves deals into it, Funnel OS marks it as not wired. That's your to-do list for the next two days. By the end of the test run, every stage has a trigger.

[SLIDE 2] Let's walk through the eleven stages, and what moves a deal into each.

Form Filled. Someone signed up on your page, or said yes to a calendar invite. The form or the Tantra webhook reports it.

Call Scheduled. They booked on your booking page. The booking reports it.

No Show. They booked and didn't turn up. The booking reports the no-show.

Qualified and Unqualified. After the call, your closer records the outcome on the call, and the outcome moves the deal. Unqualified isn't a bin. It's a list you'll invite to your next event.

Call Held. The call happened.

Follow-Up. They're interested and not ready, or they cancelled, or they asked for a refund.

Proposal Sent and Proposal Accepted. Your closer marks the proposal as sent, which starts the follow-ups at forty eight hours and seven days. When they say yes, the closer marks it accepted, and the payment link goes out.

Payment Completed. Only a signed payment webhook from your checkout can put a deal here. Never a thank-you page visit, never a person. We wire that in the payments video.

And Onboarding, right after the payment, when the welcome messages go out.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Rename the pipeline to my offer name and approve the build" > type "Yes, create this pipeline with these stages, fields and tags" > the reply confirms each item created]

I keep the stage names exactly as they are. Every sequence, report and dashboard in the course matches on the exact text, so a renamed stage breaks things quietly. I only name the pipeline after my offer. Then I approve it in my own words.

Why approve at all, when it's only a pipeline? Because the same rule runs through the whole course. Funnel OS prepares, you decide. It never sends, launches, buys or deletes without your words in the chat, and every approval is logged in your Funnel Brief. So a month from now, you can see exactly what you agreed to and when.

[SLIDE 3] Now the deal fields. Four of them carry most of the weight.

Offer. Which offer this deal is for. If you sell a smaller product at checkout and a bigger one on a call, they're two values here, so your numbers don't mix them.

Event date. The webinar, workshop or launch this deal came from. When you run your next event, you'll want to compare runs, and this is how.

Source. The channel that brought them: email, calendar, LinkedIn, WhatsApp, content or ads. It's filled from the link they clicked, so it's never typed by hand. If a deal ever shows an empty source, that's a link without tags, and we fix that in the tracking video.

Readiness score. A number from your booking page questions. It tells your closer, before the call, how ready this person is. We set the questions and the score bands in the next video.

[SCREEN: Tantra CRM > Settings > Fields > Deal fields > offer, event_date, source, readiness_score > then Contact fields > utm_source, utm_medium, utm_campaign, utm_content and the consent fields]

Here they are in the CRM under deal fields. On the contact, you'll also see the four UTM fields and the consent fields, one per channel. Those get filled by the form and the tracking we set up later. And there's one more deal field worth keeping: lost reason. Every lost deal gets a reason. Too expensive, wrong timing, chose someone else. In a month that list tells you what to fix.

Quick word on contacts and deals, because people mix them up. The contact is the person: name, email, phone, where they came from, what they agreed to receive. The deal is one chance to sell them one offer: the stage, the offer, the event, the score, the value. One person can have two deals over a year, one for this event and one for the next.

[SLIDE 4] Now tags. Stages say where a deal is in the sale. Tags say what happened at your event. Registered when they sign up. Attended when they show up. No-Show when they registered and didn't come. Replay Watched when they watch the recording.

[SCREEN: Tantra CRM > Tags > Registered, Attended, No-Show, Replay Watched, plus the sales tags the template created]

Tags are what your follow-ups branch on. The person who attended gets a different message from the person who missed it. So the tag has to be set by an event too. If No-Show never gets set, every follow-up for people who missed it silently never starts. That's the one I check most closely in every test.

The template also made the sales tags your closer uses, like booked, call held, qualified, proposal sent and proposal declined. Leave them as they are. Your sequences in the next videos trigger on their exact names.

[SCREEN: Tantra CRM > Pipelines > your pipeline > board view > one empty column per stage]

Here's the finished board. Eleven columns, empty. Now let's prove an event moves a deal.

[SLIDE 5] We test in dry run mode. Dry run means the CRM runs every automation and logs what it would send, but nothing actually goes out. So you can test with any contact and no real person gets a message.

[SCREEN: Tantra CRM > Settings > dry run mode on > a banner shows dry run is active]

Switch dry run on in the CRM settings. You'll see a banner at the top while it's on.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "In dry run, register a test contact with my own address plus the word test, source linkedin, and tell me where the deal landed" > the reply shows the deal at Form Filled, tag Registered, source linkedin, offer and event date filled]

I ask Funnel OS to register one test contact, using my own address with the word test added, with LinkedIn as the source. Then I ask where the deal landed.

[SCREEN: Tantra CRM > the test contact > the deal in Form Filled > fields: offer, event date, source linkedin > tag Registered > the activity log showing the event that moved it]

Form Filled. Tag Registered. Offer, event date and source filled in. And in the activity log, the event that moved it. I didn't touch the deal. That's what wired looks like.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Start my automation register with this line" > the reply shows a table: Form submit moves deal to Form Filled, status Live and tested]

Last, I ask Funnel OS to start my automation register. It's a simple list. Every automation gets one of four labels: live and tested, failing, built with one step left, or not built. Right now there's one line, and it's live and tested. By the end of Day 2 this list is complete, and nothing launches while any line says failing.

Leave dry run on while you build. You'll switch it off on launch day, and the launch video reminds you. And start one habit today: open this board every morning. Anything in Form Filled with no booking after twelve hours gets reviewed. A new enquiry gets a call within five minutes when you can. The board makes both easy to see.

Your next step: build the pipeline, add the four deal fields and the tags, and move one test contact with an event, in dry run. Then watch Booking Pages That Qualify Before the Call.

Screen recording shot list

  1. Tantra CRM, Pipelines, the starter pipeline. About 20 seconds
  2. Funnel OS chat: the pipeline proposal with each stage and its trigger event, then the approval. About 75 seconds
  3. Tantra CRM, Settings, Fields: the four deal fields, lost reason, then the contact UTM and consent fields. About 45 seconds
