  4. Tantra CRM, Tags list. About 20 seconds
  5. Tantra CRM, the empty pipeline board. About 15 seconds

Build Score checklist

  • [ ] Pipeline named for your offer with the eleven default stages, names unchanged (1 point)
  • [ ] Deal fields offer, event date, source and readiness score, plus lost reason (1 point)
  • [ ] Event tags Registered, Attended, No-Show and Replay Watched in the CRM (1 point)
  • [ ] Dry run on, and a test contact reached Form Filled with the Registered tag and its source, moved by the event alone (1 point)
  • [ ] Automation register started in Funnel OS with its first line marked live and tested (1 point)
scripts/V14-booking-pages-that-qualify-before-the-call.md

V14: Booking Pages That Qualify Before the Call

  • Module: M3 CRM and Money
  • Day: Day 1
  • Length: about 9 minutes (about 1,100 to 1,250 spoken words)
  • Systems on screen: Tantra CRM, Funnel OS
  • Before you start: V13 done (pipeline, readiness score field, dry run on); each host has connected their Google calendar in the Tantra CRM; your price is above about $1,000, or you also sell a call based offer
  • Done when: a test booking in dry run lands in Call Scheduled with a readiness score, a host assigned by round robin, and four pre-call messages queued

Slides

Slide 1: A Booking Page Does Two Jobs

  • Books the time
  • Tells your closer who is coming
  • Above about $1,000: book a call

Slide 2: Three to Six Questions

  • Each answer scores points
  • Revenue now, the problem, timing
  • Short enough to finish on a phone

Slide 3: Two Score Bands

  • Top band: the closer's calendar
  • Lower band: a shorter call or the next event
  • Nobody is turned away

Slide 4: Round Robin Hosts

  • Calls shared across hosts
  • Team name or first names only
  • Never a surname on the page

Slide 5: The Pre-Call Sequence

  • Now: confirmation and what to bring
  • 48 hours and 24 hours before
  • 1 hour before, one link

Voice script

[SLIDE 1] You know the call. Twenty minutes in, you find out they have no budget, no team and no timeline, and they booked because the button was there. Two of those a day and your best hours are gone. This video fixes that before the call happens. You'll build a booking page that asks a few short questions, scores the answers, sends each person to the right call, shares calls across your team, and reminds people so they actually turn up.

First, when do you need a call at all? A simple rule. If your offer is under about a thousand dollars, send people straight to checkout. Above that, book a call. Most people in this club sell above it, so this page is where your revenue gets decided.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Draft my booking page questions and score bands from my Funnel Brief" > the reply shows five questions, the points for each answer, the maximum score and two bands]

I start in Funnel OS. It knows my offer and my buyer from the Funnel Brief, so I ask it to draft the questions and the score bands.

[SLIDE 2] Keep it to three to six questions. Fewer, and you learn nothing. More, and people quit halfway on their phone. Each answer earns points. Good questions ask about things that predict a sale: their revenue today, the problem in their own words, who decides, and when they want to start. One open question is fine, for the problem in their own words. The rest are multiple choice, so they can be scored.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > the reply: "What is your monthly revenue today?" with four ranges and points; "What's the main thing in the way?" free text; "Who else is involved in the decision?"; "When do you want to start?" with points; maximum 12]

Here's mine. Monthly revenue in four ranges. The main thing in the way. Who else decides. When they want to start. Each answer has points, and the top score is twelve.

[SLIDE 3] Now the two bands. People at or above the line, say eight out of twelve, see your closer's calendar. People below it aren't rejected. They see a shorter call with a setter, or an invite to your next event, and they go into your long-term nurture. Nobody is turned away. Someone who's not ready this month is often ready in three. So write the lower band message kindly. Thank them, tell them exactly what happens next, and give them one useful thing to do in the meantime.

The score goes into the readiness score field on the deal. So before every call, your closer opens the deal and sees the number and the answers.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Create this booking page in my Tantra CRM: 45 minutes, Google Meet, phone required, these questions and bands, round robin across my hosts, deals into Call Scheduled" > the reply shows the booking page settings and asks for approval > type "Yes, create the booking page"]

Then I ask Funnel OS to create it in the Tantra CRM. Forty five minutes. Google Meet as the location. Phone number required, so reminders can reach them on WhatsApp if they tick the box. Every booking creates or moves a deal into Call Scheduled. I read the settings it shows me and approve.

It also writes the page text from my Funnel Brief. The title says what the call decides, for example whether the club fits your business this quarter. The description opens with the pain, in my buyer's words, then two lines on what they'll walk away with. No hype. A person deciding whether to give you forty five minutes wants to know what they get.

[SLIDE 4] Now the hosts. With round robin, calls are shared across your team, so no single calendar fills up and nobody waits a week for a slot.

[SCREEN: Tantra CRM > Booking pages > your page > Hosts > add each host > distribution: round robin > each host's Google calendar shows connected]

In the CRM, open the booking page and then Hosts. Add each person who takes these calls and set the distribution to round robin. Each host needs their own Google calendar connected, so the page only offers times they're actually free.

Set three small things while you're here. A buffer of fifteen minutes between calls, so nobody runs late. A minimum notice of a few hours, so nobody books a slot that starts in ten minutes. And a booking window of about two weeks ahead. Further out than that, and people forget why they booked.

One rule on what the page shows. Use your team name, or first names only. The page should never need a host's surname. People book with your company, and if a host leaves next month, your pages don't need rewriting.

[SCREEN: Tantra CRM > Booking pages > your page > the public preview on a phone width: title, short description, the questions, the time picker, the consent boxes]

Here's the page at phone width. A clear title that says what the call decides. Two lines on what they'll get from it. The questions. The times. And unticked consent boxes, one for WhatsApp and one for SMS, so they choose how you remind them.

After they book, they land on a confirmation page. Keep it simple: the time in their own time zone, an add to calendar button, and the three numbers to have ready. If your team uses Slack, you can also send the host an alert with the readiness score the moment a call is booked.

[SLIDE 5] Now the part that decides how many people actually show up: the pre-call sequence. Four touches.

[SCREEN: Tantra CRM > Sequences > "Pre-call" > step 1 on booking: email and WhatsApp > step 2: 48 hours before > step 3: 24 hours before > step 4: 1 hour before > stop when cancelled or rescheduled]

Right now, the moment they book: a confirmation by email, and by WhatsApp if they opted in. It says the time in their time zone, the link, and what to bring. Ask for three numbers: their revenue now, their leads last month, and their calls booked. With those numbers in hand, the call starts at the real conversation.

Forty eight hours before: a short note with one question to think about before the call.

Twenty four hours before: a reminder with the time and an easy way to reschedule. A rescheduled call beats a no-show every time.

One hour before: one line and one link. The call starts in an hour, here's your link.

Each message carries one link. Write them in your own voice, short, the way you'd text a client. The sequence stops on its own if they cancel or reschedule, and starts again for the new time. And if someone doesn't show, the no-show message goes out within five minutes with a link to pick a new time. We build that one in the sales automations video.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "In dry run, book a test call on my booking page with high band answers and show me what happened" > the reply shows the deal in Call Scheduled, readiness score 10, the host assigned, four messages queued in dry run]

Now test it. Dry run is still on, so nothing goes out. I ask Funnel OS to book a test call with answers that score in the top band.

[SCREEN: Tantra CRM > the test deal > Call Scheduled > readiness score 10 > host assigned > Sequences tab: four steps scheduled > Activity: the booking event]

Call Scheduled. Readiness score ten. A host assigned by round robin. Four messages queued with the right times. And the activity log shows the booking event that moved it. Then I run a second test with low answers and check it gets the lower band. Look at what that person was offered, and read their messages in the dry run log. They should feel looked after, not rejected. Both go in the automation register as live and tested.

Your next step: build your booking page with three to six questions and two bands, add your hosts on round robin, and test one booking in each band. Then watch Payments: Checkout, Webhooks and the First Test Sale.

Screen recording shot list

  1. Funnel OS chat: draft the questions and bands, show the points table. About 45 seconds
  2. Funnel OS chat: create the booking page, show the settings, approve. About 40 seconds
  3. Tantra CRM, Booking pages, Hosts, round robin, each calendar connected. Show first names only; blur host email addresses. About 40 seconds
  4. Tantra CRM, the public booking page preview at phone width with the consent boxes unticked. About 30 seconds
  5. Tantra CRM, Sequences, the pre-call sequence: four steps and the stop conditions; open the confirmation message. About 70 seconds
  6. Funnel OS chat: the high band test booking. Blur the test email and phone number. About 30 seconds
  7. Tantra CRM, the test deal: stage, readiness score, host, queued steps, activity. Blur the email and phone number. About 40 seconds
  8. Funnel OS chat: the low band test result and the register lines. About 25 seconds

Build Score checklist

  • [ ] Booking page live with 3 to 6 scored questions writing to the readiness score field (1 point)
  • [ ] Two score bands routed: top band to the closer, lower band to a shorter call or the next event (1 point)
  • [ ] Hosts on round robin with their calendars connected, and no surname on the page (1 point)
  • [ ] Pre-call sequence on booking, 48 hours, 24 hours and 1 hour, one link each, stopping on cancel or reschedule (1 point)
  • [ ] One test booking per band in dry run, landing in Call Scheduled with the right score and host (1 point)
scripts/V15-payments-checkout-webhooks-and-the-first-test-sale.md

V15: Payments: Checkout, Webhooks and the First Test Sale

  • Module: M3 CRM and Money
  • Day: Day 2
  • Length: about 13 minutes (about 1,600 to 1,750 spoken words)
  • Systems on screen: Dodo Payments, Razorpay, Stripe, Tantra CRM, Funnel OS
  • Before you start: payment account created and live verification started on Day 0 (V02); V13 and V14 done (pipeline with Payment Completed, dry run available); your price and currency in the Funnel Brief
  • Done when: a test payment moved a test deal to Payment Completed and then Onboarding, and its refund moved it to Follow-Up with the refunded tag, with no one touching the deal

Slides

Slide 1: A Sale Counts Only on the Webhook

  • Signed webhook from your checkout
  • Never a thank-you page visit
  • The webhook moves the deal

Slide 2: Pick Your Checkout

  • Dodo: merchant of record, adds local tax
  • Razorpay: India, KYC first
  • Stripe: restricted key only

Slide 3: Dodo, Step by Step

  • Test mode first, then the key
  • Product and checkout link
  • Webhook: payment succeeded, refund succeeded
  • Test sale, then refund

Slide 4: Test and Live Are Separate

  • Live verification: 1 to 3 business days
  • New live key
  • Rebuild product, link and webhook in live

Slide 5: Razorpay and Stripe

  • Razorpay: KYC, then international payments
  • Stripe: write on four things only
  • Never the main account key

Slide 6: Proof It Works

  • Test sale: Payment Completed, Onboarding
  • Refund: Follow-Up, tagged refunded
  • Both in the automation register

Voice script

[SLIDE 1] Here's how money goes missing. Someone pays, the payment provider knows, and your CRM doesn't. So the buyer keeps getting "are you still thinking about it" messages, and you find out from an annoyed reply. Or the opposite: someone lands on your thank-you page without paying and gets counted as a sale. In this video you'll set up checkout so the only thing that marks a sale is the signed message from your payment provider, and you'll run a test sale and a refund to prove it.

That signed message is a webhook. When a payment succeeds, your provider sends a signed notice to your Tantra CRM. The CRM checks the signature, finds the deal and moves it to Payment Completed. A thank-you page visit never counts, because anyone can open a page. Someone can bookmark it, share it, or land on it after a card was declined. If your pages counted that as a sale, your revenue number would be fiction, and your follow-ups would stop chasing people who never paid.

[SLIDE 2] Which provider? Dodo Payments is the default in this course. It's a merchant of record, which means it sells on your behalf and adds the right local tax for each buyer's country, so you don't register for tax everywhere. If your business and most of your buyers are in India, use Razorpay. If you already run on Stripe, you can use Stripe with a restricted key. One provider is enough. Pick one.

One thing that's the same whichever you pick: Funnel OS never charges a card, never refunds and never takes real money on its own. It builds, you approve, and refunds are something you do yourself in your provider's dashboard.

You started verification on Day 0. Dodo usually takes one to three business days. That's why we build everything in test mode today, and switch to live when the approval lands.

[SLIDE 3] Let's do Dodo first.

[SCREEN: Dodo dashboard > the mode switch at the top > Test mode on > Developer > API Keys > Add API Key > name "Funnel OS" > write access ticked > Create > copy]

In the Dodo dashboard, switch to Test mode first. Then open Developer, API Keys, and click Add API Key. The order matters. A key made before you switch is a live key. Name it Funnel OS, tick write access, create it and copy it. It's shown once.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS > Connections > Dodo > paste the key > Save > the connection shows test mode]

In Funnel OS, open Connections, choose Dodo and paste the key. Never into a chat message. Funnel OS checks it and tells you it's working in test mode.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Create my offer as a Dodo product in test mode with the price and currency from my Funnel Brief, make the checkout link, and create the payment webhook to my Tantra CRM" > the reply shows the product name, price, currency, the test checkout link and the webhook with payment.succeeded and refund.succeeded, and asks for approval > type "Yes, create these in test mode"]

Now I ask Funnel OS to create three things in test mode: the product, the checkout link and the webhook. The product carries the name and price from my Funnel Brief, one price in one currency. The checkout link sends the buyer back to my thank-you page after paying. And the webhook points at my Tantra CRM's payment connection with two events on: payment succeeded and refund succeeded. I read it, then approve.

If you sell in more than one currency, that's one product per currency, each with its own price and its own link. Don't let a buyer in London see a dollar price if you can give them pounds. And if your offer is a monthly membership rather than a one time payment, tell Funnel OS so it makes a subscription product instead.

[SCREEN: Tantra CRM > Settings > Payments > the Dodo connection > status connected > events received: none yet]

In the Tantra CRM, under the payment connections, Dodo now shows as connected. Funnel OS stored the webhook's signing key there, so the CRM can check every message is really from Dodo. Nothing has arrived yet.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Register a test contact with my own address plus the word test and give me its test checkout link" > the reply shows the deal in Form Filled and the link]

Now the test sale. I register a test contact with my own address plus the word test, so the deal exists in Form Filled. Then I open its checkout link.

[SCREEN: Dodo test checkout > the order summary at the top > pay with Dodo's test card > the thank-you page loads]

This is Dodo's test checkout. Order summary at the top. I pay with Dodo's test card, which moves no real money. The thank-you page loads. Remember, that page proves nothing. Let's look at the CRM.

While you're on the checkout, look at it as a buyer would. Order summary at the top, so they see exactly what they're paying for. Wallets like Apple Pay and Google Pay first, card below. And your refund policy linked under the button, in plain words.

[SCREEN: Tantra CRM > the test deal > stage Payment Completed, then Onboarding > tag purchased > deal value filled > activity log: payment.succeeded received, signature valid > Sequences: welcome started, every chasing sequence stopped]

Payment Completed, then Onboarding. Tagged purchased, with the deal value filled in. The value comes from the payment itself, not from a number someone typed, so your Revenue dashboard can be trusted. The activity log shows the payment succeeded event with a valid signature. The welcome sequence has started, and every chasing sequence has stopped. Nobody touched this deal. Open the welcome email in the dry run log too. It should thank them, say what happens next and when, and give them one link to take the first step. If it still has a placeholder in it, fix it now, while the only buyer is you.

[SCREEN: Dodo dashboard > Payments > the test payment > Refund > full amount > confirm > Tantra CRM > the test deal moves to Follow-Up > tag refunded > purchased removed]

Now the refund, because you need to know it works before a real buyer asks. In the Dodo dashboard, open the test payment and refund the full amount. Back in the CRM, the deal moves to Follow-Up, tagged refunded. Both go in the automation register as live and tested.

If the deal didn't move, here's how to find out why. Open the payment connection in the CRM and look at events received. Nothing there means the webhook address is wrong, so ask Funnel OS to read the webhook back. An event marked signature invalid means the signing key in the CRM doesn't match the webhook, which usually happens after a webhook was made twice. An event that arrived but matched no deal usually means the product on the checkout isn't the product the CRM is watching. Fix the one thing, then run the test sale again. Never move the deal by hand to make the test pass.

[SLIDE 4] Now, going live. Test and live in Dodo are completely separate. A test key only works in test, a live key only in live, and nothing you built in test exists in live.

[SCREEN: Dodo dashboard > verification status: approved > switch to Live mode > Developer > API Keys > Add API Key with write access > Funnel OS > Connections > Dodo > Replace the key]

When Dodo approves you, switch the dashboard to Live mode, make a new key with write access, and replace the key in Funnel OS Connections.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Rebuild my Dodo product, checkout link and webhook in live mode and update my pages" > the reply shows the three live items and asks for approval > type "Yes, take real payments with this live product"]

Then ask Funnel OS to rebuild the product, the checkout link and the webhook in live mode, and put the live link on your pages. It asks for your approval, in your own words, before anything can take real money. Then do one real purchase at your lowest price and refund it. Money moves, the deal lands in Payment Completed, the refund moves it to Follow-Up. Now you know live works too.

The most common live mistake is leaving one test piece behind. A page still showing the test link, or the CRM still holding the test signing key. So after the live rebuild, open every page with a checkout button and check the link goes to the live checkout. Going live before Dodo approves you only means your payouts are held, so wait for the approval.

[SLIDE 5] If you're on Razorpay, the shape is the same, with two extra waits.

[SCREEN: Razorpay dashboard > Account and Settings > the KYC status > International payments > request > status pending]

First, KYC: your business documents and bank account. Then, once KYC is approved, request international payments under Account and Settings, so buyers outside India can pay. Both take time, so they should have been started on Day 0. Build in Razorpay's test mode meanwhile. Razorpay gives you its own pair of test keys from its API keys screen. They go into Funnel OS Connections, the same way as Dodo, never into the chat.

[SCREEN: Razorpay dashboard > Account and Settings > Webhooks > Add new webhook > the webhook address from your Tantra CRM's payment connection > events: payment captured and refund processed > Create]

For the webhook, open Account and Settings, Webhooks, and add a new one. Paste the address your Tantra CRM gives you for the Razorpay connection, set the signing value the CRM shows, tick the payment captured and refund processed events, and save. Then the same test sale and refund. India also has strict rules on SMS, so buyers there get their reminders by WhatsApp and email.

[SCREEN: Stripe dashboard > sandbox > Developers > API keys > Create restricted key > Write on Products, Prices, Payment Links, Webhook Endpoints > every other permission None > Create key > the two-step check]

If you're on Stripe, there's one rule above all. Use a restricted key, never the main key Stripe shows first. That main key can move money. In your sandbox, open Developers, API keys, and create a restricted key. Set Write on exactly four things: Products, Prices, Payment Links and Webhook Endpoints. Leave everything else at None. If you forget Webhook Endpoints, the product and link build fine, and then sales never reach your pipeline.

Funnel OS then makes the product, the payment link and the webhook with three events: checkout completed, async payment succeeded and charge refunded. When Stripe activates your account, make the same restricted key in live mode and rebuild there. Same as Dodo: test and live are separate, so the live product, link and webhook are new, and you run one small real payment and refund before you call it done.

[SLIDE 6] So what does done look like? A test payment moved a test deal to Payment Completed and then Onboarding, without you touching it. A refund moved it to Follow-Up with the refunded tag. And when your account is approved, the same thing works in live, with one small real payment and refund.

After that, money lands in your bank on your provider's payout schedule. The Revenue dashboard in your CRM shows what's been collected, deal by deal. And when someone pays for a call based offer, your sales clock says their paid kickoff happens within three to seven days. The welcome sequence sends them the booking link for it, so nobody pays and then waits a week to hear from you.

Your next step: build your checkout in test mode, run the test sale and the refund, and add both to your automation register. Then watch Tracking: Links, UTMs, Analytics and Consent.

Screen recording shot list

  1. Dodo dashboard, switch to Test mode, then Developer, API Keys, Add API Key with write access. Blur the key entirely. About 45 seconds
  2. Funnel OS, Connections, Dodo, paste and save. Blur the key field. About 20 seconds
  3. Funnel OS chat: create product, checkout link and webhook in test mode; show the two events; approve. Blur the webhook address token. About 60 seconds
  4. Tantra CRM, Settings, Payments, the Dodo connection. Blur the signing key. About 20 seconds
  5. Funnel OS chat: register the test contact. Blur the email. About 20 seconds
  6. Dodo test checkout and the thank-you page. Use Dodo's test card only; blur the card fields and the email. About 40 seconds
  7. Tantra CRM, the test deal at Payment Completed then Onboarding, tags, value, activity, sequences. Blur the email. About 45 seconds
  8. Dodo dashboard, refund the test payment; Tantra CRM deal at Follow-Up with refunded. Blur buyer details. About 40 seconds
  9. Dodo live mode, new live key, Replace the key in Funnel OS; the live rebuild approval. Blur keys and bank details. About 60 seconds
  10. Razorpay dashboard: KYC status, International payments request, Webhooks, Add new webhook with two events. Blur business documents, account numbers and the signing value. About 60 seconds
  11. Stripe sandbox: Developers, API keys, Create restricted key with Write on the four permissions. Blur the key and the two-step code. About 50 seconds

Build Score checklist

  • [ ] Payment key connected in Funnel OS: Dodo test key with write access, Razorpay test keys, or a Stripe restricted key (never the main key) (1 point)
  • [ ] Product and checkout link built in test mode and placed on your pages (1 point)
  • [ ] Webhook to the Tantra CRM with the payment and refund events, signing key stored in the CRM (1 point)
  • [ ] Test sale moved a test deal to Payment Completed then Onboarding, with no hand moves (1 point)
  • [ ] Test refund moved the deal to Follow-Up with the refunded tag (1 point)
  • [ ] Live verification approved and product, link and webhook rebuilt in live with one real payment and refund, or the date you expect approval saved in the Funnel Brief (1 point)
scripts/V16-tracking-links-utms-analytics-and-consent.md

V16: Tracking: Links, UTMs, Analytics and Consent

  • Module: M3 CRM and Money
  • Day: Day 2
  • Length: about 10 minutes (about 1,250 to 1,400 spoken words)
  • Systems on screen: Tantra CRM, GA4, Microsoft Clarity, Funnel OS
  • Before you start: V13 to V15 done; a Google Analytics account under your sign-up address; your page domain chosen in the Funnel Brief
  • Done when: a click on a tracked test link leads to a sign-up that shows the right source on the CRM contact and a generate_lead event in GA4 Realtime, with consent recorded

Slides

Slide 1: Every Click Carries Its Source

  • No source, no worst step
  • Set it before the first visitor
  • The CRM keeps it on the contact

Slide 2: The UTM Rule

  • source: the channel
  • medium: outreach, paid or organic
  • campaign: the event
  • content: A or B

Slide 3: Tracked Short Links

  • One link per channel and step
  • Made in the Tantra CRM
  • Never shorten a personal link

Slide 4: Three Tags on Every Page

  • CRM tracking snippet
  • GA4: generate_lead and book_call
  • Clarity: watch where people leave

Slide 5: Consent

  • One unticked box per channel
  • Cookie banner for EU and UK
  • Analytics load after accept

Voice script

[SLIDE 1] A week after launch, you'll want to know one thing: which step is broken. Is it the message, the page, the reminders or the call? If your links don't say where each visitor came from, you can't answer that. You end up guessing, and changing the wrong thing. In this video you'll make sure every click carries its source, every sign-up and booking is counted, and every visitor's consent is recorded properly. It takes about half an hour and it pays you back every week.

[SLIDE 2] Start with one rule for tagging links, and write it into your Funnel Brief so every link follows it. utm source is the channel: email, calendar, LinkedIn, WhatsApp, content or meta. utm medium is how it got there: outreach, paid or organic. utm campaign is the event, for example your webinar's name and date. And utm content is the version, A or B, so when you test two messages you can tell which one brought the sign-ups.

Keep the values in lower case and spell them the same way every time. LinkedIn with a capital L and linkedin in lower case show up as two different sources, and your numbers split in half without you noticing. The rule lives in your Funnel Brief so Funnel OS uses it every time it writes a link.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Save my UTM rule in the Funnel Brief and list every link I will need, per channel and step" > the reply shows the rule and a table: channel, step, utm_source, utm_medium, utm_campaign, utm_content]

I ask Funnel OS to save the rule and list every link the funnel will need. It reads my channels and sequences from the Funnel Brief and gives me a table, one row per channel and step.

The tags also land on the contact itself. Your form reads them from the address and saves them into the four UTM fields in your CRM. That matters, because Google Analytics tells you how many people came from LinkedIn, but only the CRM tells you which of those people booked a call and paid.

[SLIDE 3] Now tracked short links. Instead of pasting long tagged addresses into messages, you use short links made in your Tantra CRM. Each one sends the person to your page with the right tags, and records the click on the contact's journey in the CRM.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Create tracked short links in my Tantra CRM for every row in this table" > the reply shows each short link next to its full tagged address and asks for approval > type "Yes, create these links"]

I ask Funnel OS to create a tracked link for every row, and approve. One link per channel and step, so your numbers don't blur together.

[SCREEN: Tantra CRM > Tracking > Links > the list of short links with clicks, each with its destination and UTM values]

Here they are in the CRM, under Tracking. Each one shows its destination, its tags and, later, its clicks. Before you use any of them, click two or three in a real browser and check the tags survive all the way to your page address. Some email and messaging apps add their own redirects, and you want to know now, not after a week of untagged clicks.

One rule. Never shorten a link that's personal to one person, like their own webinar join link. A shared short link would send everyone to the same place. Personal links go in through the contact's merge field instead.

[SLIDE 4] Next, three tags on every page you publish.

First, the CRM tracking snippet. It's a small piece of code that ties a page visit and every button click to the contact, so the CRM knows this person read your page twice before booking.

[SCREEN: Tantra CRM > Tracking > Snippet > the snippet and your page domain > Funnel OS chat > "Add my CRM tracking snippet to every page you build and deploy" > the reply confirms it in the page layout]

In the CRM, under Tracking, you'll see the snippet and the domain it's set up for. You don't copy it anywhere. Ask Funnel OS to put it into the shared layout of every page it builds, so no page ever ships without it. That includes the thank-you page and any page with your booking calendar on it. A page without the snippet is a gap in the journey.

[SCREEN: Google Analytics > Admin > Data collection and modification > Data streams > Add stream > Web > website URL and stream name > Create stream > Stream details: the Measurement ID starting with G]

Second, Google Analytics 4. In Google Analytics, open Admin, then Data streams, and add a web stream for your page domain. Copy the Measurement ID. It starts with G. If you already have a Google Analytics property for your main website, you can add this stream to it. Just keep the page domain right, so your funnel numbers don't mix with your blog traffic. Not the stream ID, the G one. Give it to Funnel OS, and it adds the tag to every page.

[SCREEN: Google Analytics > Admin > Data display > Key events > New key event > generate_lead > Save > New key event > book_call > Save]

Then mark what counts. Open Admin, Data display, Key events, and click New key event. Type generate underscore lead and save. Then again for book underscore call. Generate lead fires on every sign-up. Book call fires on every booking. These two are what your weekly numbers count, so spell them exactly. You need the Marketer role or higher on the property to add them. Funnel OS can also send them from the server when someone signs up or books, so they still count when a visitor's browser blocks the tag.

[SCREEN: Microsoft Clarity > New project > name and page domain > the project ID > Funnel OS chat > "Add Clarity project ID to every page"]

Third, Microsoft Clarity. It's free, and it records how people move on your page: where they scroll, where they stop, where they leave. Create a project for your page domain, copy the project ID and give it to Funnel OS. Once traffic arrives, watch ten recordings of people who left before the form. Write down the spot where most of them stop. Look for three things: people tapping a field that shows an error, people stopping where the price appears, and people scrolling straight past the form on a phone. Each one points at a different fix.

[SLIDE 5] Now consent, and this part isn't optional.

[SCREEN: the landing page form at phone width > email field > an unticked box for WhatsApp reminders > an unticked box for SMS reminders > the privacy link]

On your form, one unticked box per channel. One for WhatsApp, one for SMS, and one for email marketing if your buyer's country needs it. Unticked, so the person chooses. A ticked box becomes the consent field on the contact in your CRM, and your sequences only message people on the channels they ticked. That's what keeps your WhatsApp number from getting blocked.

Link a short privacy page under the form that says what you collect, why, and how to stop hearing from you. Every email carries an unsubscribe link, and every WhatsApp and SMS message says reply STOP. When someone stops, the CRM turns that channel off for them, so they never get one more message.

[SCREEN: the landing page opened from a UK address > the cookie banner: Accept, Reject, Settings > click Reject > the page works, Analytics and Clarity do not load > click Accept on a second visit > they load]

If you have visitors from the EU or the UK, you also need a cookie banner. Ask Funnel OS to add one to every page, and to load Google Analytics and Clarity only after the visitor clicks accept. If they reject, the page still works and the form still submits. Only the analytics stay off. Your CRM still records the sign-up, because that's the person giving you their details on purpose, with their consent boxes.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Add a cookie banner to every page for EU and UK visitors and load analytics only after accept" > the reply shows the banner text and asks for approval > type "Yes, add it"]

I approve the banner and the rule.

[SCREEN: a tracked LinkedIn A test link > the landing page > sign up with your own address plus the word test, tick WhatsApp > Tantra CRM > the contact: utm_source linkedin, utm_medium outreach, utm_campaign, utm_content A, WhatsApp consent yes > Google Analytics > Reports > Realtime > generate_lead in the event count card]

Now test the whole path. I click my LinkedIn version A test link, accept the banner, and sign up with my own address plus the word test, ticking WhatsApp. In the CRM, the contact shows utm source LinkedIn, medium outreach, my campaign, content A, and WhatsApp consent yes. In Google Analytics, Reports, Realtime, generate lead shows up in the event count within a few minutes. Then I book a test call and check book call appears too.

Add every line to your automation register: links, snippet, both key events, Clarity, and the banner, each marked live and tested.

That's Module 3 done. Your CRM, your booking page, your checkout and your tracking all report events on their own. Tomorrow's pages plug straight into them. And from week two, the dashboard reads these same numbers, by source and by version, so the worst step is a number, not a guess.

Your next step: make your tracked links, put the three tags and the banner on your pages, and run one tagged test sign-up. Then watch Build and Deploy Your Landing and Thank-You Pages.

Screen recording shot list

  1. Funnel OS chat: save the UTM rule and list the links per channel and step. About 40 seconds
  2. Funnel OS chat: create the tracked short links and approve. About 30 seconds
  3. Tantra CRM, Tracking, Links list. About 25 seconds
  4. Tantra CRM, Tracking, Snippet, then the Funnel OS request to put it in the layout. Blur the workspace token inside the snippet. About 35 seconds
  5. Google Analytics, Admin, Data streams, add a web stream, the Measurement ID. Blur the property ID and account details. About 45 seconds
  6. Google Analytics, Admin, Data display, Key events, generate_lead and book_call. About 40 seconds
  7. Microsoft Clarity, new project, the project ID, then Funnel OS. About 30 seconds
  8. The landing page form at 375 pixels wide with unticked consent boxes. About 20 seconds
  9. The cookie banner: reject, then accept, showing analytics load only after accept (developer tools network tab, briefly). About 40 seconds
  10. Funnel OS chat: approve the banner. About 15 seconds
  11. The tagged test sign-up, the CRM contact fields, GA4 Realtime. Blur the test email and phone number. About 60 seconds

Build Score checklist

  • [ ] UTM rule saved in the Funnel Brief and a tracked short link made in the Tantra CRM for every channel and step (1 point)
  • [ ] CRM tracking snippet in the shared layout of every page (1 point)
  • [ ] GA4 tag on every page with generate_lead and book_call as key events, and Clarity installed (1 point)
  • [ ] One unticked consent box per channel and a cookie banner that loads analytics only after accept, checked with a tagged test sign-up (1 point)
scripts/V17-landing-and-thank-you-pages.md

V17: Build and Deploy Your Landing and Thank-You Pages

  • Module: M4 Build
  • Day: Day 2
  • Length: about 14 minutes (about 1,750 to 1,900 spoken words)
  • Systems on screen: Funnel OS, Cloudflare, Tantra CRM
  • Before you start: the Funnel Brief is approved (V03 to V07), the Cloudflare API token is connected in Funnel OS (V09), the CRM pipeline exists (V13) and the tracking links and snippet are set up (V16)
  • Done when: a test sign-up from your phone, through a tagged link, lands in the Tantra CRM in Form Filled with its UTM source, and the thank-you page opens

Slides

Slide 1: The Page Every Channel Points At

  • every click lands here
  • weak page wastes every send
  • written from your Funnel Brief

Slide 2: Nine Blocks, Top to Bottom

  • trust strip, headline, short muted video
  • three proof points, the form
  • method, testimonials, FAQ
  • sticky button

Slide 3: Form Rules

  • above the fold, again lower down
  • first name, last name, work email
  • one unticked consent box per channel
  • posts straight to the Tantra CRM

Slide 4: The Thank-You Page

  • confirm and add to calendar
  • WhatsApp or community
  • what happens next, and when
  • a second door: book or buy now

Slide 5: Review on Three Screens

  • 375 pixels, phone
  • 768 pixels, tablet
  • 1280 pixels, laptop

Slide 6: Deploy Only on Your Approval

  • you type the approval yourself
  • your Cloudflare domain
  • test sign-up from your phone
  • check the CRM, not the page

Voice script

[SLIDE 1] You've sent a hundred people to a page and watched three of them sign up. Most of the time it isn't the traffic. It's a page that answers no objection, hides the form, or loses the source so you can't tell which channel worked.

In this video, Funnel OS writes your landing page and your thank-you page from the Funnel Brief, you review them on a phone, a tablet and a laptop, you approve, and Funnel OS deploys both to your own domain on Cloudflare. By the end, a sign-up from your phone shows up in the Tantra CRM with its source. That's the finish line for today.

Every channel you build later in this course points at this one page. Cold email, calendar invites, LinkedIn, content, ads. So we build it once, properly, and we build it before any traffic exists.

[SLIDE 2] Here's the shape. Nine blocks, in this order. A trust strip at the top, so the reader knows they're in the right place. The headline and a one line subhead: the promise, then the mechanism. A short video, sixty to ninety seconds, muted, with captions. Three proof points, as numbers, never adjectives. The form. Your method, named, in three to five steps. Testimonials, each one answering one objection. An FAQ with your objection list answered. And a sticky button that follows the reader down the page.

Every block has one job: answer an objection or drive the sign-up. If a block does neither, it comes out.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Write my landing page from the Funnel Brief, nine blocks, with two headlines: A benefit led and B problem led" > the reply shows the nine blocks, two headline options and the event line]

Let's do it. I'm in Funnel OS, in the chat for my funnel. The Funnel Brief is already here: offer, method, buyer, list, funnel, channels and proof. So I don't paste anything. I type: write my landing page from the Funnel Brief, nine blocks, with two headlines, A benefit led and B problem led.

Look at what comes back. The trust strip line. Headline A leads with the outcome. Headline B leads with the objection, in the buyer's own words, from the brief. The subhead names the method. Then the video script, written to be read in about seventy seconds with captions on.

Now the proof block. This is where most pages lie. Funnel OS only uses what's in your proof list. If you have no number for a slot, it leaves a gap marked fill, instead of inventing one. Leave the gap rather than make something up. A page with two honest proof points beats a page with three invented ones.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > scroll to the event line under the headline > the date, the time and "your time zone" label are visible]

The event line sits under the headline: the title, the date, and the time shown in the visitor's own time zone, with an add to calendar button and a countdown. A wrong time zone quietly kills show-up, so check this line twice.

Keep scrolling. The method block takes the method you named in video four and turns it into three to five steps, one line each. This is the block that answers "why would this work when nothing else did". If a step needs a paragraph to explain, the step isn't clear yet. Fix it in the brief, not on the page.

Then the testimonials. Funnel OS doesn't write testimonials. It tells you where each one goes and which objection it has to answer. "Will this work in my industry?" needs a quote from someone in a different industry. "I don't have time" needs a quote that mentions time. Paste in only what real people said, word for word, with their permission. If you don't have any yet, leave the block out. An empty block is honest. A made-up quote is the fastest way to lose the buyer who checks.

The FAQ takes six objections from your buyer section and answers each in two or three sentences. Read these out loud. If an answer sounds like a brochure, rewrite it the way you'd say it on a call.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Do I need the video before I deploy?" > the reply explains the page ships with a still image and the caption script until the video is uploaded]

What if you haven't recorded the video yet? I asked Funnel OS exactly that. The page ships with a still image and the script as text, and you swap the video in later with one line in the chat. Don't hold the whole funnel back for a seventy second clip. Record it this week on your phone, with captions, and send it to Funnel OS when it's done.

One more rule: no navigation links. No menu, no link to your blog, no link to your home page. Every link that leaves the page is a door out. The only ways forward are the form and the sticky button, and both lead to the same sign-up.

[SLIDE 3] Now the form. It sits above the fold on a laptop screen without scrolling, and again further down. Three fields: first name, last name, work email. Add a field only if you will use it. If you're going to send WhatsApp reminders or an AI reminder call, you need a mobile number field, and then one unticked consent box per channel. One for WhatsApp. One for texts if you use them. One for the call. Unticked. The person ticks it or you don't message them on that channel.

Under the button, a consent line with links to your privacy policy and your terms.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Show me where the form posts and which fields it carries" > the reply lists the Tantra CRM form, the visible fields, the hidden UTM fields and the consent boxes]

Here's the part that matters most. The form posts straight to the Tantra CRM. I ask Funnel OS: show me where the form posts and which fields it carries. It lists the CRM form, the three visible fields, the consent boxes, and the hidden fields: UTM source, medium, campaign and content, read from the page address. That's what you set up in the tracking video. When someone signs up, the CRM creates the contact, opens a deal in Form Filled, adds the tag Registered, and saves the source. Nobody copies a spreadsheet. Tools report events, events move deals.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Write the thank-you page from the Funnel Brief" > the reply shows the confirmation, the calendar button, the WhatsApp group invite, what happens next and the second door]

[SLIDE 4] Next, the thank-you page. Everyone who signs up sees it, at the moment they're most interested. A bare "thanks, you're in" wastes the most-read page in your funnel.

I type: write the thank-you page from the Funnel Brief. Five things come back. The confirmation, with the event line. Add to calendar: a Google Calendar button and a calendar file. The invite to your WhatsApp group or community, for people who want reminders. Exactly what arrives next, and when: "You'll get a confirmation email in the next minute, and a reminder the day before at eight in the morning your time." And the second door: people who are ready now can book a call or buy before the event. Most funnels make their most motivated people wait. Yours won't.

If you picked the video funnel, the thank-you page is different: it opens Part 2 straight away. We build that in the next video.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Build both pages as a preview" > the reply shows a private preview link > click it > the landing page opens in the browser]

Now I ask Funnel OS to build both pages as a preview. It gives me a private preview link. Nothing is live yet.

[SLIDE 5] Review on three widths: 375 pixels for a phone, 768 for a tablet, 1280 for a laptop. Most of your visitors will be on a phone, so start there.

[SCREEN: browser preview > open the developer view > set width to 375 > scroll the whole page > set width to 768 > scroll > set width to 1280 > scroll]

At 375, I check four things. The headline reads in two or three lines, not seven. The form is reachable without hunting. The sticky button doesn't cover the form fields. The video plays muted with captions. At 768 I check the proof points still line up. At 1280 I check the form is visible without scrolling.

Then I read every line as the buyer. Does it sound like me? Does it claim only what I can prove? Anything I'd be embarrassed to say on a call comes out.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "On mobile the subhead wraps to five lines. Cut it to under twelve words. Remove the second testimonial, it answers no objection" > the reply shows the revised blocks and a new preview link]

Changes are just chat. I type: on mobile the subhead wraps to five lines, cut it to under twelve words, and remove the second testimonial, it answers no objection. Funnel OS rewrites those two blocks and gives me a fresh preview. I check the phone width again. Better.

[SLIDE 6] Now the deploy. Funnel OS never publishes without you typing an approval in your own words. Not a button. Your words.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Yes, deploy the landing page and the thank-you page to my main event domain" > the reply shows the plan: the domain, the two page addresses and the DNS record it will add > the reply ends "Deployed"]

I type: yes, deploy the landing page and the thank-you page to my main event domain. Funnel OS shows me exactly what it's about to do: the domain on my Cloudflare account, the two page addresses, and the record it adds. Then it deploys through the Cloudflare token I connected earlier. A note: the pages live on your main domain or a subdomain of it, the one buyers know. Never on the outreach domains you set up for cold email. Those stay for sending only.

[SCREEN: Cloudflare dashboard > the domain > Workers and Pages > the new project shows Active > blur any account identifiers]

In Cloudflare I can see the project is active. I don't need to do anything here. I'm only looking.

Now the real test. Not on my laptop. On my phone.

[SCREEN: phone screen recording > open a tracked link from V16 with utm source test > the landing page loads > fill the form with a test address > tick WhatsApp consent > submit > the thank-you page opens]

I open one of my tracked links on my phone, the one with source set to test. The page loads. I fill the form with my own test address, tick the WhatsApp box, and submit. The thank-you page opens. I tap add to calendar: the right date, the right time, in my time zone. I tap the WhatsApp group link: it opens.

[SCREEN: Tantra CRM > Contacts > search the test contact > the contact opens > fields show utm source test, the Registered tag and the deal in Form Filled > blur the email address]

Now the check that counts. In the Tantra CRM, I open Contacts and find the test contact. The source says test. The tag says Registered. The deal sits in Form Filled. The WhatsApp consent is recorded. If the page worked but the CRM shows nothing, the page didn't work. Go back to Funnel OS and type: my test sign-up isn't in the CRM, check the form connection. It reads the connection and tells you what broke.

One more thing before you leave this video. Remember the A and B headlines? Funnel OS serves them half and half, and the version each visitor saw is saved on the contact as the content tag. We'll set that up as your first experiment in video twenty-two. For now, just check that both versions load. Add v equals a or v equals b to the page address and look at each one.

Two mistakes I see every cohort. First, asking for a phone number you'll never call. Every extra field costs sign-ups, so if you aren't sending WhatsApp reminders or a reminder call, take the phone field out. Second, editing the live page by hand in Cloudflare. Don't. Every change goes through the Funnel OS chat, so the brief, the page and the preview always match, and the next deploy doesn't quietly undo your fix.

That's your landing page and your thank-you page, written from your brief, reviewed on three screens, live on your domain, and wired to the CRM.

Next, do the phone test yourself and post a screenshot of the CRM contact with its source in the WhatsApp group, with the email blurred. Then open the next video, The Conversion Step: Webinar, Video or Call.

Screen recording shot list

  1. Funnel OS chat for the member's funnel, Funnel Brief visible in the side panel. Type the landing page request and let the full reply render, scroll slowly through all nine blocks and both headlines (about 90 seconds)
  2. Close-up on the proof block showing a "fill" gap where no proof exists, then on the event line with the time zone label (about 20 seconds)
  3. Type the form question; show the reply listing the CRM form, visible fields, hidden UTM fields and consent boxes (about 30 seconds)
  4. Type the thank-you page request; scroll through the five parts of the reply (about 45 seconds)
  5. Build the preview, open the private preview link, use the browser's device view at 375, 768 and 1280 pixels, scrolling the full page at each width (about 2 minutes)
  6. Type the two revision requests, open the new preview at 375 pixels (about 40 seconds)
  7. Type the deploy approval in full words; show the plan and the "Deployed" reply (about 40 seconds)
  8. Cloudflare dashboard, Workers and Pages, the project shows Active. Blur the account ID and any token (about 15 seconds)
  9. Phone screen recording: open the tracked link, fill the form with a test address, tick WhatsApp consent, submit, tap add to calendar, tap the group link. Blur the email and phone number (about 60 seconds)
  10. Tantra CRM, Contacts, open the test contact: source, Registered tag, deal in Form Filled, consent. Blur the email and phone number (about 30 seconds)
  11. Browser: load the page with v=a, then v=b, show both headlines (about 15 seconds)

Build Score checklist

  • [ ] Landing page written in nine blocks with an A and a B headline, proof only from your proof list (1 point)
  • [ ] Thank-you page written with confirmation, calendar, group, what happens next and the second door (1 point)
  • [ ] Both pages reviewed and fixed at 375, 768 and 1280 pixels (1 point)
  • [ ] Both pages deployed to your Cloudflare domain after your typed approval (1 point)
  • [ ] Test sign-up from your phone appears in the Tantra CRM in Form Filled with its UTM source and the Registered tag (2 points)
scripts/V18-conversion-step.md

V18: The Conversion Step: Webinar, Video or Call

  • Module: M4 Build
  • Day: Day 2
  • Length: about 13 minutes (about 1,600 to 1,750 spoken words)
  • Systems on screen: Funnel OS, your webinar platform, Tantra CRM
  • Before you start: the landing and thank-you pages are live and a test sign-up reached the CRM (V17); your funnel is picked in the Funnel Brief (V07); the booking page exists (V14)
  • Done when: your one conversion step is written and set up, and a test poll answer, watch depth or booking lands on the test contact in the Tantra CRM

Slides

Slide 1: Value First, Then One Offer

  • teach something worth paying for
  • then one clear offer, once
  • pick one conversion step

Slide 2: Three Paths

  • webinar: live, 60 minutes
  • video page: three acts, always on
  • book a call: the call converts

Slide 3: The Webinar Clock

  • open 0 to 8, credibility to 10
  • problem to 16, old vs new to 19
  • method to 34, proof to 39
  • offer 39 to 43, Q and A to 60

Slide 4: Four Polls, Five Roles

  • persona, challenge, priority, call interest
  • host, content, tech, chat, DMs
  • one person, two roles at most

Slide 5: Into the CRM

  • registrations and attendance as tags
  • poll answers as tags
  • connect before registration opens

Slide 6: The Video Page

  • Part 1 teaches, no form in front
  • button at the off-ramp second
  • opt-in past 75 percent
  • application scores the next step

Voice script

[SLIDE 1] Most people who run a webinar and get no bookings did one of two things. They pitched before they taught anything, or they taught for an hour and never made a clear offer. Either way the room leaves and nobody books.

This video builds your conversion step: the moment a person who signed up becomes a booked call or a buyer. The rule is the same for every funnel. Teach something genuinely useful first. Then make one clear offer, once.

[SLIDE 2] You'll pick one of three paths. The webinar: one live session, sixty minutes, the offer near minute forty. The video page, which we call the Reverse Squeeze Page: a recorded video that teaches before it asks, with an application at the end. Or book a call: the booking page itself is the step, and the call does the converting. Your funnel is already in your Funnel Brief from video seven. If you weren't sure, you picked the webinar, so I'll show that one in full, then the other two briefly.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Write my session story for a 60 minute webinar in ten sections, using the Funnel Brief" > the reply shows ten sections with minutes, slide titles, key lines and poll positions]

[SLIDE 3] Start with the clock. Sixty minutes. Open and promise, zero to eight. Credibility, eight to ten. Problem and stakes, ten to sixteen. Old way and new way, sixteen to nineteen. The method, nineteen to thirty-four. Proof, thirty-four to thirty-nine. The offer, thirty-nine to forty-three. Objections, forty-three to forty-four. Call to action, forty-four to forty-six. Then questions and answers to sixty.

In Funnel OS I type: write my session story for a sixty minute webinar in ten sections, using the Funnel Brief. Here it comes. Each section has its minutes, slide titles, the key lines I say, and where the polls and chat prompts go.

Look at credibility. Two minutes. Three numbers I can show, then my story in three beats: where I was, what broke, what I found. Not a CV. Nobody remembers titles. They remember what broke.

Look at the problem section. Every number needs a source. Funnel OS writes "check" wherever it inferred something, and leaves a gap where I have no proof. The rule from the brief holds here too: no source, no number.

The method section is the longest, fifteen minutes. One slide per step of your method, a chat prompt, a short demo, a poll and a checkpoint. This is where you earn the right to make an offer.

Then proof: one case study. Who, where they started, what you did, the result with a number, and when. And honest ranges of what to expect, with the condition each one needs.

The offer gets four minutes, once. A bridge line, what's included, the price or how to start, and the guarantee. No price, no offer button, nothing about buying before minute thirty-nine.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Turn the session story into a slide by slide deck outline" > the reply lists slides with title, on-slide text under 20 words, what I say and a visual note]

Next, the deck. I type: turn the session story into a slide by slide deck outline. Each slide gets a title, on-slide text under twenty words, what I say, and a one line visual note. One idea per slide, big type, because people watch on laptops. Slide one carries the promise under the title. The last slide carries the next step and its link. I build these slides in the slide tool I already use. It takes an hour with the outline in front of you.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Write my run of show, four polls, chat prompts and three DMs" > the reply shows a minute by minute table with four lanes: host, chat and polls, DMs, tech]

[SLIDE 4] Now the run of show. If the host is fixing audio, the session is lost. So I type: write my run of show, four polls, chat prompts and three DMs. The reply is a minute by minute table with four lanes: host, chat and polls, direct messages, and tech, marked against my slide numbers.

Four polls, in order. Poll one, persona: who's in the room. Poll two, challenge: their main problem. Poll three, investment priority, around minute ten to fifteen: it separates browsers from buyers. Poll four, on the offer slide: discovery call interest. It splits "book me now" from "not yet". Poll four's "book me now" list is the hottest list you'll have all week.

Three direct messages: at minute three, "can you hear us?", at minute fifteen, "what's your biggest blocker right now?", and at minute forty, "any question for the Q and A?". And the offer link in the chat every five minutes once the offer is made.

Five roles, with real names, not job titles. Host, who teaches and makes the offer and touches nothing else. Content moderator, who runs the polls on the clock. Tech moderator, who owns the platform. Chat and Q and A. And the DM operator, who sends the booking link to every "book me now" within the hour. One person can cover two roles. Nobody covers three. I type the names into the chat and Funnel OS puts them in the table.

It also writes three moments word for word: the first sixty seconds, the bridge from teaching to the offer, and the close of the questions. Practise the bridge out loud until it sounds like you.

[SCREEN: your webinar platform > session settings > registration fields, recording on, replay expiry date, four polls added, co-hosts added > blur any attendee data]

[SLIDE 5] Now the room, in your webinar platform. Five settings. Registration fields: first name, last name, work email. Recording on, and you'll say "this is recorded" out loud on the day. The replay expiry date, matching what your emails will promise. The four polls, added before the day. Your team, added as co-hosts in their roles.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Connect my webinar platform to the CRM: registrations, attendance and poll answers as tags" > the reply shows the connection plan and the webhook it will add > type the approval]

Then the connection. This is the step people skip and regret. Registrations, attendance and poll answers must reach the Tantra CRM as tags. Attended, No-Show, Replay Watched, and each poll answer. Most platforms never resend past events, so connect this before registration opens. I ask Funnel OS to connect my webinar platform to the CRM. It shows me the plan and the webhook it will add. I type my approval. It also connects the other direction: when someone signs up on your landing page, they're registered on the webinar platform, so each person gets a personal join link.

[SCREEN: your webinar platform > answer a test poll as a test attendee > Tantra CRM > the test contact shows the poll answer tag > blur the email]

To test it, I join as a test attendee from a second device, answer a test poll, and open the test contact in the CRM. The poll answer is there as a tag. That's the check.

Last, put the dry run in your calendar: two or three days before the event, the whole session, on the real platform, with two or three people watching. That's where you find the muted microphone and the broken link while it costs nothing.

On the day itself, three habits. Open the doors five minutes early and ask people where they're joining from, so the chat is moving before you start. Have someone log the attendee count every five minutes. That curve shows you exactly where people leave, and it's the first thing you'll look at when you improve the session. And run questions by upvote. Answer the most voted first. If you seed questions to get things going, the team asks them openly as "questions people often ask". Never post a question under an invented attendee name.

Why bother with all of this? Because one live room can carry a lot of buyers at once, and the polls tell you who's ready. Shweta's coaching group, Growth Idea, ran their best webinar on the twenty-eighth of August 2025: 1,990 registered, 321 came live, and 25 sales calls were booked from that one session. That's what a written clock, real polls and a clear offer slide make possible. It isn't a promise of your numbers. Your numbers come from your list and your offer, and you'll compare them to your own last session, never to anyone else's.

[SLIDE 6] Now the second path, briefly: the video page, the Reverse Squeeze Page. It plays a video with no form in front of it. Nothing expires, so it can run for months.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Write my video script in three acts with the off-ramp line, then build the Part 1 page" > the reply shows act one hook and proof, act two the teaching, act three the offer in Part 2, and the off-ramp line marked]

Funnel OS writes the script in three acts. Act one, the hook and the proof. Act two, the teaching: the beliefs your buyer has to change, one at a time. Act three, the offer, which lives in Part 2. Early in Part 1 there's an off-ramp line: "If you already know you want help with this, click the button below this video." You record the long version once, and cut a twelve minute and a six minute version. Upload them to a video host that reports watch depth, and write down the second the off-ramp line starts in each cut.

Then Funnel OS builds the page. The button stays hidden until that off-ramp second. The opt-in, "Part 2 goes deeper, where should I send it?", appears once the viewer passes seventy-five percent. Watch depth at twenty-five, fifty, seventy-five and a hundred is saved on the CRM contact. Part 2 ends with a short application. Each answer scores points: a high score goes straight to your booking page, a middle score gets a personal reply within a day, a low score gets a warm message and your newsletter. Nobody is turned away. The score only picks the next step.

[SCREEN: Tantra CRM > Booking pages > the booking page from V14 with its qualifying questions > a test booking lands in Call Scheduled]

The third path: book a call. Here the booking page you built in video fourteen is the conversion step. Funnel OS writes the page copy above the calendar: who the call is for, what they leave with even if they never buy, and how long it takes. Name the call after what it gives them, not "discovery call". Funnel OS also writes your discovery, walkthrough and close scripts, and two battlecards for your most common objections. Test it with one booking: it should land in Call Scheduled with every answer on the contact.

Whichever path you picked, the done check is the same: a test poll answer, a watch depth or a booking appears on the test contact in the CRM.

Next, build your one path and run that check. Then open the next video, Nurture: Confirmation, Reminders and AI Calls.

Screen recording shot list

  1. Funnel OS chat, type the session story request, scroll the ten sections slowly, pause on credibility, the problem section with a "check" mark, and the offer section (about 2 minutes)
  2. Type the deck outline request, scroll the first ten slides of the outline (about 40 seconds)
  3. Type the run of show request, show the four lane table, the four polls, the three DMs, type the five role names and show them in the table (about 90 seconds)
  4. Your webinar platform, session settings: registration fields, recording, replay expiry, polls, co-hosts. Blur attendee names and emails (about 60 seconds)
  5. Funnel OS chat, the connection request, the plan with the webhook, the typed approval. Blur any webhook token in the address (about 40 seconds)
  6. Second device joins as test attendee, answers a poll; Tantra CRM test contact shows the poll tag. Blur the email (about 40 seconds)
  7. Funnel OS chat, the video script request, scroll the three acts with the off-ramp line highlighted, then the page build reply showing button second and 75 percent opt-in (about 60 seconds)
  8. Tantra CRM booking page from V14, make a test booking, show the deal in Call Scheduled with the answers. Blur the email and phone (about 40 seconds)

Build Score checklist

  • [ ] Your one conversion step chosen and its content written in Funnel OS (session story and deck outline, video script, or booking page copy and call scripts) (2 points)
  • [ ] Webinar: run of show with four polls and five named roles, room set up; or video page built with the button and opt-in timings; or booking page live with qualifying questions (1 point)
  • [ ] A test poll answer, watch depth or booking lands on the test contact in the Tantra CRM (2 points)
scripts/V19-nurture-reminders-ai-calls.md

V19: Nurture: Confirmation, Reminders and AI Calls

  • Module: M4 Build
  • Day: Day 2
  • Length: about 12 minutes (about 1,500 to 1,650 spoken words)
  • Systems on screen: Tantra CRM, Funnel OS
  • Before you start: the landing page posts to the CRM (V17), your conversion step is set up (V18), WhatsApp templates were submitted on Day 0 (V02, V12) and the SMS sender is verified if you use texts
  • Done when: the reminder sequence and both after-event branches are built in the Tantra CRM in dry run mode, and a dry run on a test contact shows every step with one working link

Slides

Slide 1: Sign-Ups Who Never Show

  • every no-show is a paid-for lead
  • nothing reminded them hard enough
  • one sequence fixes most of it

Slide 2: The Reminder Clock

  • on sign-up: confirmation
  • 7 days, 24 hours at 08:00 local
  • 1 hour, 15 minutes, live
  • one link per message

Slide 3: Channel Rules

  • email for every touch
  • WhatsApp only with a ticked box
  • texts for 15 minutes or live only
  • plain facts, STOP in every message

Slide 4: The AI Call Step

  • consent per number
  • says it is automated
  • under 30 seconds, inside the send window
  • never retried

Slide 5: Two Branches After the Event

  • attended: offer, objections, last call
  • no-show: replay, the missed idea, closes
  • book, buy or reply stops both

Voice script

[SLIDE 1] You'll get the sign-ups. Then a big share of them won't turn up. Life happened, and nothing reminded them hard enough. Every person who signs up and doesn't show is a lead you already paid for, in time or in money.

This video builds the sequence that gets them into the room. A confirmation, five timed reminders, an AI reminder call for people who said yes to one, and two branches after the event: one for people who came, one for people who missed it. All of it lives in the Tantra CRM, and Funnel OS writes every word.

[SLIDE 2] Here's the clock, timed from the event start. On sign-up, the confirmation. Seven days before, a value primer: one useful idea from the session. Twenty-four hours before, at eight in the morning in their own time zone, what to prepare. One hour before, the join link again. Fifteen minutes before, "we start in fifteen". And at the start, "we're live, join now".

One rule holds for every message: one link. Two links is a decision, and a decision fifteen minutes before a session is a lost attendee.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Write my confirmation and reminder sequence from the Funnel Brief: email for every touch, WhatsApp for 24 hours, 1 hour and live, a text for 15 minutes" > the reply shows six messages with subject, preheader, body and the WhatsApp versions]

In Funnel OS I type: write my confirmation and reminder sequence from the Funnel Brief, email for every touch, WhatsApp for twenty-four hours, one hour and live, and a text for fifteen minutes.

Look at the confirmation first. It's the most opened message you'll ever send, because it arrives the moment they sign up. Subject: what they signed up for, plainly. The body: you're in, the date and time in their time zone, their personal join link, one thing to prepare, and the group invite. Under a hundred and fifty words.

Two things to check in the confirmation. The join link must be the personal one, from your webinar platform, not the generic room link. With a generic link, nobody can tell who attended, and your no-show branch has nothing to run on. And if your webinar platform sends its own confirmation with the calendar hold attached, keep that switched on too. It's often the one that puts the session in their calendar.

The seven day primer is not a reminder. It's a reason to come. One useful idea from the session, or one small exercise to do before it, under two hundred words. People who did the exercise arrive invested, and invested people stay to the offer.

The twenty-four hour email tells them what to prepare. Two hundred words or so. The one hour message is short: here's your link. The fifteen minute message is under fifty words. And each one has exactly one link.

[SLIDE 3] Now the channel rules. Email goes to everyone. WhatsApp goes only to people who ticked the WhatsApp box on your form. Texts, if you use them, only for the fifteen minute or the live touch, and only to people who ticked that box.

WhatsApp reminders are plain statements of fact. "The session starts tomorrow at eleven, here's your link." No urgency words. Urgency turns a utility template into a marketing one, which costs more and needs stronger opt-in. Every WhatsApp message carries STOP, so people can leave. And remember from video twelve: marketing templates don't deliver to US numbers, so US contacts get the utility version. The templates you submitted on Day 0 should be approved by now. Check before you build.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Create this as a sequence in the Tantra CRM, triggered by the Registered tag, timed from the event start, in dry run mode" > the reply shows the plan: sequence name, trigger, eight steps with timing and channel > type the approval > the reply shows the sequence link]

Now I ask Funnel OS to build it. I type: create this as a sequence in the Tantra CRM, triggered by the Registered tag, timed from the event start, in dry run mode. Funnel OS shows me the plan: the name, the trigger, each step with its timing and channel. I read it, then I type my approval. It creates the sequence through the CRM connection and gives me the link.

[SCREEN: Tantra CRM > Automation > the new sequence > the step list with email, WhatsApp and SMS steps, each with its timing before the event > open the 24 hour step > the send time shows 08:00 in the contact's time zone]

Here it is in the Tantra CRM. Every step in order, each with its channel and its timing before the event. I open the twenty-four hour step: it sends at eight in the morning in each contact's own time zone, not mine. That matters. A reminder that lands at four in the morning for half your audience is a reminder nobody reads.

Check the send window for this sequence. Reminders are due to the minute, so the window has to cover every day of the week and every hour the event could need. A narrow office hours window will hold a reminder back until it's useless.

[SLIDE 4] Now the AI call step. A short phone call, placed by an AI voice, a few minutes before the event. A call gets answered when an email doesn't. But it comes with rules, and the CRM enforces them.

One: consent per number. Only people who ticked the call box on your form get called. No consent on record, no call. Two: the call says it's automated, in the first line. Three: it's under thirty seconds. A reminder, not a conversation. Four: it only goes out inside the send window, in the contact's time zone. If the reminder time falls outside the window, the call is skipped, not delayed. Five: it's never retried. A failed call doesn't ring again, so nobody gets called twice. And if anyone opts out on a call or by text, every later call to that number is blocked.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Add an AI call step 12 minutes before the event: under 30 seconds, says it is an automated reminder, for contacts with call consent" > the reply shows the opening line and script > Tantra CRM > the sequence > the AI call step appears with the opening line, the script and Call through set to Automatic]

I type: add an AI call step twelve minutes before the event, under thirty seconds, says it's an automated reminder, for contacts with call consent. Funnel OS writes the opening line: "Hi, this is an automated reminder from" and my business name, then the session time and that the link is in their inbox. That's it.

In the CRM, the step shows the opening line, the script and "Call through", which I leave on Automatic. Why twelve minutes before? The calls go out in a batch, a few a minute, so starting a little early means everyone has been called before the session starts.

The call's result comes back to the deal timeline: answered, voicemail, no answer, busy, failed or opted out. You can branch on it later. For now, the call is one step, and the sequence moves on.

You can also add a short confirmation call a couple of minutes after someone signs up, with the same rules. Start with the reminder call only. Once you've seen a few results on your own timeline, decide whether the confirmation call earns its place. And check how it lands: if people are confused by it, shorten it or drop it. You'll measure it the honest way, by comparing show-up for people who got the call against people who didn't, in the same week, on your own list.

Before any of this goes near a real list, run each email through a spam checker. Funnel OS can flag words that tend to trip filters, but a real checker reads the whole message the way a mail server does.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Write the attended and no-show branches and add them to the CRM, triggered by the Attended and No-Show tags, in dry run mode" > the reply shows two branches of four messages each > Tantra CRM > two new sequences]

[SLIDE 5] Now what happens after the event. Two branches, and nobody ever gets the other branch's messages.

People who attended get four touches. Straight after: the offer and the booking link, while they're still warm. One day later: a recap and your first objection, answered in the buyer's own words. Two days later: proof and the second objection. Three days later: last call, with a real deadline that you keep.

People who missed it get four different touches. They aren't cold. They signed up. Twenty-four hours after: the replay, with its expiry date. Two days after: the one idea they missed, with the minute it starts, not "here's the recording". Five days after: the replay closes tonight. Then actually close it. Seven days after: the next session date.

I ask Funnel OS to write both branches and add them to the CRM, triggered by the Attended and No-Show tags from your webinar platform. It builds them in dry run mode, like the reminders.

The exit rule is the most important line in both sequences. Anyone who books, buys or replies stops receiving them, straight away. A buyer who keeps getting "last call" emails after paying is a refund request waiting to happen. Open each sequence and check the exit rule says: a booking, a payment or a reply removes the contact.

If you picked the video funnel, you don't use a clock. Funnel OS builds follow-ups by watch depth instead: under twenty-five percent, twenty-five to seventy-five, over seventy-five without an opt-in, and opted in. One message per band. Never run the clock reminders beside them. If you picked book a call, your reminders are the booked call reminders, and we build those in the next video.

[SCREEN: Tantra CRM > the reminder sequence > Dry run > pick the test contact > the run log lists each step: would send email, would send WhatsApp, would call, each with its time and one link > blur the email and phone]

Last, the dry run. I pick my test contact from the last video and run the sequence in dry run mode. The run log lists every step it would send, when, and on which channel. The AI call step says "would call" because my test contact has call consent. On a contact without consent, it says "would not call: no consent on record". I click every link in the log. Each one works, and each message has only one.

Nothing has gone to a real person. The full test with two test contacts comes in video twenty-one, and the sequences only leave dry run mode at launch.

Next, build your reminder sequence and both branches, and run the dry run on your test contact. Then open the next video, From Booked Call to Paid: Your Sales Automations.

Screen recording shot list

  1. Funnel OS chat, type the sequence request, scroll the six messages with the WhatsApp and text versions, pause on the confirmation (about 90 seconds)
  2. Type the build request, show the plan with eight steps, type the approval, show the sequence link (about 40 seconds)
  3. Tantra CRM, Automation, open the sequence, show the step list, open the 24 hour step with the 08:00 contact time zone setting, show the send window (about 60 seconds)
