  4. Funnel OS chat, type the AI call request, show the script; Tantra CRM, the AI call step with opening line, script and Call through set to Automatic (about 60 seconds)
  5. Funnel OS chat, type the branches request, scroll both branches; Tantra CRM, the two new sequences, open one and show the exit rule (about 60 seconds)
  6. Tantra CRM, dry run on the test contact, the run log with every step, click two links. Blur the email and phone number (about 60 seconds)

Build Score checklist

  • [ ] Confirmation and five reminders written, one link each, WhatsApp and text only to ticked boxes (1 point)
  • [ ] Reminder sequence built in the Tantra CRM on the Registered tag, timed in the contact's time zone, in dry run mode (1 point)
  • [ ] AI call step added with consent required, an automated disclosure, under 30 seconds (1 point)
  • [ ] Attended and no-show branches built, each with an exit rule for book, buy or reply (1 point)
  • [ ] Dry run on the test contact shows every step with one working link (1 point)
scripts/V20-sales-automations.md

V20: From Booked Call to Paid: Your Sales Automations

  • Module: M4 Build
  • Day: Day 2
  • Length: about 11 minutes (about 1,350 to 1,500 spoken words)
  • Systems on screen: Tantra CRM, Funnel OS
  • Before you start: the pipeline and stages exist (V13), the booking page is live (V14), the test payment webhook works (V15), and the nurture sequences are in dry run mode (V19)
  • Done when: every pipeline stage from Form Filled to Onboarding has a sequence or a task with a clock and an exit rule, built in the Tantra CRM in dry run mode

Slides

Slide 1: A Booked Call Is Not Revenue

  • money in the account is the finish
  • five places buyers fall out
  • one clock per stage

Slide 2: The Sales Clocks

  • enquiry called within 5 minutes
  • form filled, no booking: 12 hours
  • no-show messaged within 5 minutes
  • proposal: 48 hours and 7 days

Slide 3: Booked Call Reminders

  • confirmation at once
  • 48 hours, 24 hours, 1 hour
  • reschedule link in every one

Slide 4: Paid Means the Webhook

  • signed webhook moves the deal
  • welcome email starts
  • kickoff within 3 to 7 days

Slide 5: Every Lost Deal Gets a Reason

  • a reason code
  • a re-engage date
  • tools report events, events move deals

Voice script

[SLIDE 1] A call gets booked, the person doesn't show, and nobody notices until Friday. Or a proposal goes out and sits there for three weeks because nobody followed up. That's where most of the money in a funnel quietly leaks.

A booked call isn't revenue. A signed proposal isn't revenue. Money in the account is. Between the offer and the money, people fall out in five places: they don't book, don't show, don't decide, don't pay, or pay and never start. This video puts a clock on every one of them in the Tantra CRM.

Quick rule before we build. Price decides the path. Under about a thousand dollars, send people straight to checkout. Above it, book a call first. If your offer goes to checkout, you'll still build the payment and welcome parts of this video, and you can skip the call reminders.

[SLIDE 2] Here are the clocks. An enquiry gets a call within five minutes in working hours. A form filled with no booking gets reviewed within twelve hours. A no-show gets a message within five minutes. A proposal gets followed up at forty-eight hours and again at seven days. A paid customer gets a kickoff within three to seven days. And every lost deal gets a reason.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Write the follow-ups for every stage of my pipeline: Form Filled, Call Scheduled, No Show, Follow-Up, Proposal Sent, Payment Completed, and lost deals. For each: trigger, exit rule, steps with timing, channel and owner" > the reply shows one table per stage with the full copy]

In Funnel OS I type: write the follow-ups for every stage of my pipeline. Form Filled, Call Scheduled, No Show, Follow-Up, Proposal Sent, Payment Completed, and lost deals. For each one, the trigger, the exit rule, and the steps with timing, channel and owner.

The reply is one table per stage. Look at the owner column. Some steps are messages the CRM sends. Others are tasks for a person: call this lead now. A clock with no owner is a wish.

[SLIDE 3] Start with the booked call reminders. If you build only one sequence today, build this one. Most missed calls are forgotten calls.

Confirmation the moment they book: the time, what the call covers, one question to think about, and the reschedule link. Forty-eight hours before: one proof story from someone like them, only from your proof list. Twenty-four hours before: the agenda, by email and text. One hour before: the meeting link and the reschedule link.

The reschedule link goes in every message. People who can't make it will move the call instead of quietly vanishing.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Create the booked call reminders in the CRM, triggered when a deal enters Call Scheduled, in dry run mode" > the plan shows four steps > type the approval > Tantra CRM > Automation > the new sequence with four steps, each showing the reschedule link]

I ask Funnel OS to create it, triggered when a deal enters Call Scheduled, in dry run mode. I read the plan and type my approval. In the CRM, there it is: four steps, timed before the call, each with the reschedule link.

Notice what moves the deal into Call Scheduled. Not me. The booking page does. When someone books, the CRM moves the deal and the reminders start. That's the rule for this whole course: tools report events, events move deals. If you moved a deal by hand, that step isn't wired.

[SCREEN: Tantra CRM > Automation > the No Show sequence > steps: text with new times at 5 minutes, email at 15 minutes, two call tasks the same day, then a 7 day sequence]

Let me show you the copy for the twenty-four hour reminder, because it does more work than people think. It doesn't say "just a reminder". It says what you'll cover, in three lines, using what they told you on the booking page. They said their goal was more booked calls from their list. So the agenda says we'll look at your list, your current booking rate, and the one step to fix first. A call about them gets attended. A call about you gets forgotten.

Next, the no-show. Someone booked, and didn't turn up. Within five minutes, a text with new times. Within fifteen, an email with the booking link. Two call tasks the same day for you or your closer. Then a seven day sequence. The rebooking link goes out inside the hour, never the next morning.

Now the form filled with no booking. They filled your form or your application but didn't pick a time. The CRM creates a task to review them within twelve hours. Good fits get a call. Everyone else gets the booking link. And a direct enquiry, someone who asked to talk, gets a call task within five minutes during working hours, then one call a day for three days.

[SCREEN: Tantra CRM > Automation > the Proposal Sent sequence > steps at 48 hours and 7 days > the exit rule shows: stage changes to Proposal Accepted or Payment Completed]

After a call that's held, the deal moves to Call Held, and then either Follow-Up, Proposal Sent, or Unqualified. Follow-Up gets a recap email the same day, in their words, with the next step. Then weekly follow-ups for high intent, every two weeks for medium. Funnel OS wrote the recap template. You fill in two lines after each call: their goal and the next step. That's two minutes, not twenty.

Proposal Sent. A follow-up at forty-eight hours, another at seven days. Ask Funnel OS for a proposal template too, from the brief: their goal in their words, the situation, your method applied to them, scope, timeline, the investment, the guarantee and an expiry date. A proposal that takes ten minutes goes out the same day. One that takes an afternoon goes out next week, and next week the buyer has cooled. And the exit rule: the moment the deal moves to Proposal Accepted or Payment Completed, the follow-ups stop.

[SCREEN: Tantra CRM > a sequence > the step picker > Slack step selected > message "New booking: first name, call time"]

One step that helps a small team: the Slack step. I add one to the booked call sequence so our team channel gets a message when a call is booked. Same for a no-show. Nobody has to watch the pipeline to know something happened.

And Unqualified isn't a dead end. Those people get a warm, honest message about what comes next by email, and they stay on your long-term list. Some of them will be a fit in six months, and we'll keep them warm in video thirty-four.

[SLIDE 4] Now the money. A sale counts only when the signed payment webhook arrives. Never from someone landing on a thank-you page. Anyone can open a thank-you page. Only your payment provider can send the signed webhook.

You connected that webhook in video fifteen. Now we hang the automations on it.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "When the payment webhook arrives, move the deal to Payment Completed, start the welcome sequence and create a kickoff task due in 3 working days" > the plan shows the trigger and three actions > type the approval > Tantra CRM > the Payment Completed sequence]

I type: when the payment webhook arrives, move the deal to Payment Completed, start the welcome sequence, and create a kickoff task due in three working days. Funnel OS writes the welcome sequence from the brief: the welcome email with what they bought and the very next step, a day one message on getting their first quick win, a check-in on day three, and what week two looks like on day seven.

Why so much care at the start? The first forty-eight hours decide whether a customer stays. Kickoff within three to seven days, not three weeks. The kickoff task goes to whoever runs delivery, with the customer's goal from the booking answers already on the deal, so the first call starts where the sales call ended.

If you sell by invoice instead of checkout, add one more clock: reminders three days before the due date, on the due date, and three and seven days late. Polite, factual, with the payment link. Nobody enjoys chasing money, which is exactly why a sequence should do it.

And check the exits from everything earlier. A buyer must stop getting the attended follow-ups, the no-show messages and the proposal reminders the moment the payment lands. Open each sequence and look for the exit rule.

[SLIDE 5] Last, lost deals. When a deal is lost, the CRM asks for a reason. Price, timing, wrong fit, chose someone else, no reply. Make the reason field required. Then a short door-open email goes out, and a re-engage date goes on the deal. In a month you'll know exactly why people say no, and that's the input for your next offer and your next FAQ.

[SCREEN: Tantra CRM > Pipeline > drag a test deal to Lost > the reason field appears and is required > pick "Timing" > set a re-engage date]

Watch. I move a test deal to Lost. The reason field appears, and it won't save without one. I pick timing and set a re-engage date three months out.

Here's the full picture. Form Filled has a twelve hour review. Call Scheduled has four reminders. No Show has a five minute text. Follow-Up has a same day recap. Proposal Sent has forty-eight hours and seven days. Payment Completed has the welcome and the kickoff. Lost has a reason. Every sequence has an exit rule, and all of them sit in dry run mode until the test run in the next video.

Next, build the booked call reminders first, then the rest, and check every exit rule. Then open the next video, The Test Run: Two Fake Leads Through Every Step.

Screen recording shot list

  1. Funnel OS chat, type the pipeline follow-ups request, scroll the tables, pause on the owner column (about 60 seconds)
  2. Funnel OS chat, create the booked call reminders, the plan, the typed approval; Tantra CRM, the sequence with four steps and the reschedule link visible (about 60 seconds)
  3. Tantra CRM, the No Show sequence steps, then the Form Filled review task and the enquiry call task (about 50 seconds)
  4. Tantra CRM, the Proposal Sent sequence and its exit rule (about 20 seconds)
  5. Tantra CRM, step picker, add a Slack step with the booking message. Blur the workspace and channel names (about 20 seconds)
  6. Funnel OS chat, the payment webhook automation request, the plan, the approval; Tantra CRM, the Payment Completed sequence and the kickoff task (about 60 seconds)
  7. Tantra CRM pipeline, drag a test deal to Lost, the required reason field, set a re-engage date. Blur any contact details on the cards (about 30 seconds)

Build Score checklist

  • [ ] Booked call reminders built: confirmation, 48 hours, 24 hours, 1 hour, reschedule link in each (1 point)
  • [ ] No-show, form filled with no booking, enquiry and proposal clocks built with owners and exit rules (1 point)
  • [ ] Payment webhook moves the deal to Payment Completed, starts the welcome sequence and creates a kickoff task (1 point)
  • [ ] Lost deals require a reason and get a re-engage date (1 point)
scripts/V21-test-run.md

V21: The Test Run: Two Fake Leads Through Every Step

  • Module: M4 Build
  • Day: Day 2
  • Length: about 12 minutes (about 1,500 to 1,650 spoken words)
  • Systems on screen: Funnel OS, Tantra CRM, your webinar platform, payments
  • Before you start: pages live (V17), conversion step set up (V18), nurture and sales sequences built in dry run mode (V19, V20), payments in test mode with the webhook connected (V15)
  • Done when: both test contacts have been through every automation, and the automation register in Funnel OS has no line that says Failing

Slides

Slide 1: Most Funnels Were Never Tested

  • found now, a leak costs nothing
  • found later, an empty column
  • two test contacts, every step

Slide 2: Two Test Contacts

  • your own inbox, never a real person
  • contact A attends
  • contact B misses
  • both from a tagged link

Slide 3: What You Check After Every Step

  • the right message, on time
  • the right channel, one working link
  • only its own branch
  • tags, source and stage in the CRM

Slide 4: Booking, Payment, Refund

  • book, miss, rebook
  • test payment moves the deal
  • refund recorded on the deal

Slide 5: The Automation Register

  • Live and proven
  • Failing, with the reason
  • Built, one step left
  • Not built

Slide 6: The Launch Rule

  • nothing launches while a line says Failing
  • fix, then rerun with new contacts
  • rerun after any change

Voice script

[SLIDE 1] Here's how most funnels fail. Everything looks fine, the launch goes out, and three weeks later you notice the no-show column is empty. Not because everyone came. Because the no-show tag never arrived, so the replay email never went to anyone.

Most funnels that "don't work" were never tested from the first click to the payment. In this video you'll walk two fake leads through every automation you built, write down what you find in an automation register, and fix every failure. A leak found today costs nothing. Found after launch, it costs a whole run.

[SLIDE 2] Two test contacts. Both use your own inbox, never a real person. If your email is on Google Workspace, add a plus sign and a word before the at sign: your name plus came, and your name plus missed. Both land in your inbox, and the CRM treats them as two people. Contact A will attend. Contact B will miss.

If you use WhatsApp reminders or the AI call, use your own phone number on contact A and tick those boxes. Leave them unticked on contact B. That tests consent both ways.

[SCREEN: phone > open the tracked link with utm source test run > fill the landing form as contact A, tick WhatsApp and call consent > submit > thank-you page > repeat as contact B with no boxes ticked > blur both email addresses and the phone number]

On my phone I open a tracked link with the source set to test run. I sign up as contact A, tick WhatsApp and the call box, and submit. The thank-you page opens. Then I sign up again as contact B, with no boxes ticked.

[SCREEN: Tantra CRM > Contacts > filter by source test run > both contacts show tag Registered, deal in Form Filled, consent fields > blur the emails and phone]

[SLIDE 3] In the Tantra CRM I filter contacts by source: test run. Both are here. Both carry the Registered tag. Both deals sit in Form Filled. Contact A has WhatsApp and call consent. Contact B has none. That's the first check passed: the form, the source and the consent.

One housekeeping step. I add the tag Test to both contacts. Your dashboard in video thirty leaves anything tagged Test out of every count, so your first real week isn't padded with two fake sign-ups. Do it now, before you forget.

And in my inbox, the confirmation for both, each with a personal join link. Two different links. If they're the same link, your platform is handing out the generic room link and attendance won't track. That's a failure, and it goes in the register.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Run the test run on my two test contacts: move the clock to each reminder, read both contacts back after every step, and tell me what each one received" > the reply walks through 7 days, 24 hours, 1 hour, 15 minutes and live for each contact]

Now the timed messages. You can't wait a week for the seven day reminder, so the CRM's dry run mode does the waiting. I ask Funnel OS: run the test run on my two test contacts, move the clock to each reminder, read both contacts back after every step, and tell me what each one received.

Look at the reply. Seven days before: both get the primer by email. Twenty-four hours: both get the email at eight in the morning their time, and only contact A gets WhatsApp. One hour: same. Fifteen minutes: contact A gets the text. And the AI call step: contact A, "would call". Contact B, "would not call, no consent on record". That's exactly right. A message on the wrong channel, or to someone without consent, is a failure, the same as a message that never went.

[SCREEN: your webinar platform > a short test session > contact A joins from a second device and answers poll 4 "book me now" > contact B stays away > end the session > Tantra CRM > contact A shows Attended and the poll tag, contact B shows No-Show]

Next, attendance. I run a short test session on my webinar platform. Contact A joins from a second device and answers poll four with "book me now". Contact B doesn't join. I end the session.

In the CRM, contact A has the Attended tag and the poll answer. Contact B has No-Show. Watch contact B most closely. A missing No-Show tag is silent: nothing errors, and every follow-up for people who missed the session simply never fires.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Move the clock through the attended and no-show branches for both test contacts" > the reply shows contact A received the offer, recap, proof and last call; contact B received the replay, missed idea, replay closes and next session; neither got the other branch]

I ask Funnel OS to move the clock through both branches. Contact A got the offer straight after, then the recap, the proof and the last call. Contact B got the replay, the missed idea with its minute, the replay closing, and the next session. Neither got the other's messages. Pass.

If you picked the video page, your test looks a little different. Instead of attending and missing, you run three viewing sessions: stop at about ten percent, stop at about half, and watch past seventy-five percent. Each one should show the right button and the right opt-in, and each depth should land on its test contact. Then send three applications: an ideal fit, a maybe and a poor fit. The ideal fit should land on your booking page. The maybe gets the "we'll reply within a day" message. The poor fit gets the warm message. Each one only gets the follow-up for its own depth or band.

If you picked book a call, skip the session and go straight to the booking part below. Your test contact B is the one who books and never shows.

[SLIDE 4] Now the sales path. Booking, missing it, rebooking, paying, refunding.

[SCREEN: booking page > book a call as contact A > Tantra CRM > the deal moves to Call Scheduled > mark the call No Show > the no-show text and email show in the dry run log > rebook from the link > deal back in Call Scheduled]

As contact A, I book a call on the booking page. In the CRM the deal moves to Call Scheduled by itself, and the booked call reminders appear in the log. Then I mark the call as a no-show. The five minute text and the fifteen minute email with new times show up in the log. I rebook from the link, and the deal is back in Call Scheduled. Important: once the booking happened, the attended follow-ups for contact A stopped. Check that. A booked lead who keeps getting "last call" emails is a broken exit rule.

[SCREEN: checkout in test mode > pay as contact A with the provider's test card > Tantra CRM > deal moves to Payment Completed > welcome sequence starts > kickoff task created > payment provider dashboard > refund the test payment > the deal timeline shows the refund > blur card and account details]

Then the payment. I open the checkout in test mode and pay as contact A with the provider's test card. A few seconds later the signed webhook arrives. The deal moves to Payment Completed, the welcome sequence starts, and the kickoff task is created. Every other follow-up for contact A has stopped.

Last, the refund. In the payment provider's dashboard I refund the test payment. The refund shows on the deal's timeline. Refunds are part of the funnel. Test them now, not the first time a real customer asks.

While you're in the payment step, check one more thing. Open the thank-you page after the payment, then open it again in a fresh browser without paying. The deal must not move the second time. If visiting a page can mark someone paid, anyone with the address can. Only the signed webhook moves a deal to Payment Completed.

Then two more checks. I reply to one of my own test emails from Tantra, and the reply tag lands on the contact in the CRM. That's the reply webhook. And I move a test deal to Lost: the reason field is required.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Write my automation register from this test run" > the reply shows a table: automation, trigger, tool, status, with one line marked Failing]

[SLIDE 5] Now the automation register. I type: write my automation register from this test run. Funnel OS writes one line per automation, with its trigger, the tool it runs on, and an honest status. Four statuses only. Live and proven: it fired for a test contact and did the right thing. Failing: it didn't, with the reason. Built, with one step left: it exists but something's missing, like a WhatsApp template still waiting for approval. Not built: you haven't made it.

Here are my lines. Landing form to CRM: Live and proven. Confirmation and reminders: Live and proven. AI reminder call: Live and proven. Attendance and poll tags: Live and proven. Attended branch, no-show branch: both Live and proven. Booking moves the deal, booked call reminders, no-show recovery: Live and proven. Payment webhook and welcome: Live and proven. Refund on the timeline: Live and proven. Tantra replies to the CRM: Failing. The reason: LinkedIn replies aren't switched on in the webhook.

Be honest when you write the statuses. It's tempting to call something Live and proven because you're fairly sure it works. Fairly sure isn't a status. If a test contact didn't go through it today, it hasn't passed. Funnel OS only marks a line Live and proven when it read the result back from the CRM itself.

[SLIDE 6] And here's the rule. Nothing launches while any line says Failing. Not one channel, not a small test. Failing means a real person falls through a hole you already know about.

What about "Built, with one step left"? That's a judgement call, and you make it in writing. If the WhatsApp template is still waiting for approval, email still carries every reminder, so you can launch and add WhatsApp when it clears. If the payment webhook is the step left, you can't launch an offer that goes to checkout. Write the decision next to the line, so your coach can see it on the weekly call.

[SCREEN: Tantra app > Connectors > Webhooks > the CRM webhook > switch on LinkedIn replies > save > Funnel OS chat > type "LinkedIn replies are switched on. Rerun the test run with two new test contacts" > the register shows every line Live and proven]

So I fix it. In Tantra, I open the webhook to the CRM and switch on LinkedIn replies. Save. Then I ask Funnel OS to rerun the whole test with two new test contacts. Not just the line that failed. The whole thing, because fixes have a habit of breaking something next door. Use new plus words each run so old messages don't confuse you.

The register now reads Live and proven on every line. It's saved in Funnel OS, and you'll rerun this test after any change to a page or a message, for as long as the funnel runs.

Next, run your test, write your register, and fix every Failing line. Then open the next video, Your First Experiments, Set Before Launch.

Screen recording shot list

  1. Phone screen recording: open the tracked link, sign up as contact A with WhatsApp and call boxes ticked, then as contact B with none. Blur both emails and the phone number (about 60 seconds)
  2. Tantra CRM contacts filtered by source test run, open each contact: tags, stage, consent. Then the inbox with both confirmations and two different join links. Blur emails, phone and the join link tokens (about 50 seconds)
  3. Funnel OS chat, the test run request, scroll the reply through each reminder for both contacts, pause on the AI call lines (about 60 seconds)
  4. Webinar platform test session from a second device, poll 4 answer, end the session; CRM shows Attended with poll tag and No-Show. Blur attendee details (about 60 seconds)
  5. Funnel OS chat, move the clock through both branches, the reply (about 30 seconds)
  6. Booking page, book as contact A; CRM deal in Call Scheduled; mark No Show; dry run log; rebook. Blur contact details (about 60 seconds)
  7. Checkout in test mode with the provider's test card; CRM deal in Payment Completed; welcome sequence; kickoff task; provider dashboard refund; timeline entry. Blur card and account details (about 75 seconds)
  8. Funnel OS chat, the register request, the table with one Failing line (about 30 seconds)
  9. Tantra app webhook settings, switch on LinkedIn replies, save; Funnel OS rerun; register all Live and proven. Blur the webhook address (about 45 seconds)

Build Score checklist

  • [ ] Two test contacts signed up from a tagged link, one with consent boxes ticked and one without (1 point)
  • [ ] Every reminder, branch, booking, no-show, payment and refund checked on both contacts (1 point)
  • [ ] Automation register written in Funnel OS with an honest status on every line, and no line says Failing (1 point)
scripts/V22-first-experiments.md

V22: Your First Experiments, Set Before Launch

  • Module: M4 Build
  • Day: Day 2
  • Length: about 8 minutes (about 1,000 to 1,100 spoken words)
  • Systems on screen: Funnel OS
  • Before you start: the test run passed with no line Failing (V21); your channels are picked in the Funnel Brief (V07); tracked links exist (V16)
  • Done when: every channel you will launch has one experiment written in the Funnel OS experiment log, with its A, its B, its own utm_content and a read point

Slides

Slide 1: Launch Without a Test, Learn Nothing

  • one result, nothing to compare
  • set the test before the first send
  • one element per channel

Slide 2: One Experiment Per Channel

  • cold email: step 1, A vs B
  • LinkedIn: the connection note
  • calendar invites: the title
  • landing page: the headline

Slide 3: A and B

  • A benefit led, the outcome
  • B problem led, their objection
  • same offer, same list, same page

Slide 4: Split, Tag, Read

  • list split 50/50
  • separate utm_content per half
  • read at a set point, never hours

Slide 5: When It Counts

  • a few hundred per half
  • gap bigger than 2 x root of A plus B
  • otherwise run it again

Voice script

[SLIDE 1] You launch, a week goes by, and you've got a number. Twelve sign-ups from cold email. Is that good? You have no idea, because there's nothing to compare it with. And you've just spent your first, cleanest run learning nothing.

This video fixes that before you send anything. You'll set one experiment per channel, write the A and the B, give each half its own tag, decide when you'll read it, and log it in Funnel OS. Ten minutes now, and your first run teaches you something.

[SLIDE 2] The rule is one element per channel. Not the subject and the body and the send time. One thing. If you change three things and B wins, you don't know which one did it.

Here are the first experiments I'd set. Cold email: step one only, the subject and the opening, A against B. Steps two to five stay identical. LinkedIn: the connection note, A against B, same five messages after. Calendar invites: the title. Landing page: the headline. If you're running ads, the two ads differ only in angle: same image style, same page, same audience.

[SLIDE 3] And what's the difference between A and B? Always the same pairing for a first test. A is benefit led: it opens with the outcome. B is problem led: it opens with the buyer's objection, in their own words, from your Funnel Brief. Same offer, same list, same page. Only the angle changes. Never test a second, thinner page against your real one. Test one line.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Set my first experiments before launch: one per channel I'm launching, A benefit led and B problem led, using the Funnel Brief" > the reply shows a table: channel, element, version A, version B, utm_content, deciding number, read point]

Let's set them. In Funnel OS I type: set my first experiments before launch, one per channel I'm launching, A benefit led and B problem led, using the Funnel Brief.

The reply is a table. Channel, the element being tested, version A, version B, the utm content for each half, the number that decides it, and the read point. Look at the cold email row. Version A's opening line leads with the outcome. Version B leads with the objection my buyers say most: they've tried outreach before and it burned their domain. Steps two to five are the same in both.

Now the LinkedIn row. Note A opens with the outcome they want. Note B opens with what they're tired of. Both under three hundred characters, both with one specific detail about the person and one easy question. And the calendar invite row: title A names the outcome like a meeting would, title B names the problem. Both have no dollar sign, no percent and no "free", because those rules come from the channel, not from the test.

Read every A and B out loud before you save them. If you can't tell which one is which, the test is too small to show anything. Make the difference clear: one talks about where they want to be, the other about what's in their way.

[SLIDE 4] Now the split. Each channel's list is split fifty fifty. Half gets A, half gets B. In Tantra that means two campaigns per channel, one per half, which we build in the next video. Every link in the A half carries utm content variant a. Every link in the B half carries variant b. That's how the dashboard counts them as two separate rows. Without separate tags, both halves land in one number and the test is gone.

The landing page is different. You don't split a list. Funnel OS already serves headline A and headline B half and half, remembers which one each visitor saw, and saves it on the contact as the content tag.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > scroll to the deciding number and read point columns > cold email row: "Visit rate per person reached. Read after 500 sends per half."]

Next, the deciding number. Pick one before the run, not after. For cold email, it's page visits per person reached. For the landing headline, it's sign-ups per page visit. For calendar invites, registrations per person invited. Not opens. Not clicks on their own. The number that leads to a sign-up.

Then the read point: when you'll look. A finished run, or a set number of sends. Never a few hours in. Numbers read after a morning kill things that were working. Write it down: "read after five hundred sends per half", or "read when the event registration closes".

[SLIDE 5] And how do you know the result is real? Two rules. First, you need a few hundred people per half at minimum. With forty people per half, chance decides. Second, when the run ends, look at the gap between A and B in conversions. Only call it when that gap is bigger than twice the square root of A plus B.

Here's an illustration with made up numbers. A gets sixty sign-ups. B gets seventy-five. The gap is fifteen. A plus B is a hundred and thirty-five. The square root of that is about eleven and a half, so twice that is about twenty-three. Fifteen is smaller than twenty-three. So this isn't decided. You run the same test again on the next run before you call it. You don't need to do this sum yourself. Funnel OS does it when you read the experiment. But you should know why it says "not decided yet".

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Save these to my experiment log" > the experiment log opens with one row per channel: date, channel, element, A, B, utm_content, deciding number, read point, expected result, status Open]

Now save them. I type: save these to my experiment log. The log opens: one row per channel. The date, the channel, the element, A, B, the utm content, the deciding number, the read point, what I expect to move, and the status: open. When you read it later, Funnel OS fills in the result and the decision: keep A, keep B, or run again. The loser gets reverted in the brief, so every future message uses the winner.

Two last rules. Never run two experiments on the same step at the same time. And never end one early because one half looks ahead after a day. Early leads flip all the time. Wait for the read point you wrote down, then read both halves together.

Next, write one experiment for every channel you'll launch and save them to your log. Then open the next video, Cold Email and LinkedIn Campaigns in Tantra, where you build each half.

Screen recording shot list

  1. Funnel OS chat, type the experiments request, let the table render, close-up on the cold email row with both opening lines (about 60 seconds)
  2. Scroll to the utm_content column, show variant a and variant b for each channel (about 20 seconds)
  3. Scroll to the deciding number and read point columns, pause on each row (about 30 seconds)
  4. Funnel OS chat, type "Save these to my experiment log", show the log with one Open row per channel (about 40 seconds)

Build Score checklist

  • [ ] One experiment per launch channel, with an A benefit led and a B problem led version and the same offer (1 point)
  • [ ] Each experiment saved in the Funnel OS experiment log with its own utm_content per half, a deciding number and a read point (1 point)
scripts/V23-cold-email-and-linkedin-campaigns.md

V23: Cold Email and LinkedIn Campaigns in Tantra

  • Module: M5 Traffic
  • Day: Day 3
  • Length: about 13 minutes (about 1,600 to 1,750 spoken words)
  • Systems on screen: Tantra app, Funnel OS, Tantra CRM
  • Before you start: mailboxes connected and warming (V10), LinkedIn connected and the reply webhook to the CRM created (V12), your clean list loaded in Tantra (V08), the experiment log written (V22), the test run passed (V21)
  • Done when: your cold email A and B campaigns (or your omnichannel A and B) and your LinkedIn A and B campaigns are saved as drafts in Tantra, read through, test sends checked, and a test reply lands in the Tantra CRM

Slides

Slide 1: Two Ways to Burn a Channel

  • cold email before mailboxes warm
  • LinkedIn faster than it allows
  • build now, send on the right day

Slide 2: Separate Campaigns or Omnichannel

  • cold email A and B, LinkedIn A and B
  • or one omnichannel sequence, A and B
  • never both on the same people

Slide 3: The Cold Email Thread

  • days 0, 3, 6, 9, 13
  • subject on step 1 only
  • one link, one opt-out line
  • held until mailboxes are warm

Slide 4: LinkedIn Pace

  • 15 to 20 requests a day
  • 200 a week at most
  • note under 300 characters
  • messages on days 0, 3, 7, 12, 25

Slide 5: Every Reply Reaches the CRM

  • replies, bounces, unsubscribes
  • LinkedIn and WhatsApp replies too
  • answered the same day, with the booking link

Voice script

[SLIDE 1] There are two fast ways to wreck an outreach channel. Send cold email from a mailbox that's three days old, and the domain gets flagged before anyone reads a word. Or push a LinkedIn profile too hard in week one, and LinkedIn restricts it. Both are hard to undo.

In this video, Funnel OS drafts your cold email and LinkedIn campaigns straight into Tantra, each one split into the A and B halves you set in the last video. You read them, test them, and leave them as drafts. LinkedIn goes out at launch. Cold email waits until your mailboxes are warm.

[SLIDE 2] First, a choice. You can run separate campaigns: cold email A and B, and LinkedIn A and B. Or you can run one omnichannel sequence that mixes email and LinkedIn steps for the same person, split into A and B. Either works. What never works is both on the same people. If someone sits in your cold email campaign and your omnichannel sequence at the same time, they get two threads from you about the same event. That reads as spam, because it is. So pick one setup. If you pick omnichannel, it replaces the separate campaigns. It never runs beside them.

Not sure? Start with separate campaigns. They're simpler to read on the dashboard, one row per channel.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Draft my cold email campaigns in Tantra: A and B, each on half the list, five steps, using the approved copy and my experiment log" > the reply shows the plan: two campaign names, the list halves, senders, schedule, the five steps and the tagged link]

[SLIDE 3] Let's build cold email. In Funnel OS I type: draft my cold email campaigns in Tantra, A and B, each on half the list, five steps, using the approved copy and my experiment log.

Here's the plan it shows me. Two campaigns, because Tantra's steps don't have an A/B switch: the test is two campaigns, each with half the list. Five steps, on days zero, three, six, nine and thirteen. Only step one has a subject. Steps two to five are short replies in the same thread, each with one new reason: a proof point, the one idea they'll learn, the date again, an easy way out. Steps two to five are identical in A and B. Only step one changes, exactly as your experiment log says.

Every step has one link, to your landing page, tagged with source email, medium outreach, the event as the campaign, and variant a or variant b as the content. And every step ends with a one line way out: "Not for you? Reply no and you won't hear from me again."

Merge fields: first name and company, nothing else. Funnel OS writes sentences that still read well if the company is missing.

The senders are your connected mailboxes set to Cold. Never your calendar invite senders, and never your main domain. The schedule is Monday to Friday, business hours in your buyer's time zone, fifty a day per mailbox once warm.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > the reply ends with "Cold email can start on" and the warm-up date > type "Yes, create both as drafts" > the reply shows two draft links]

Look at the last line. Funnel OS checks the warm-up date of every mailbox and tells me when cold email can start: fourteen days after the mailboxes started warming. It won't launch these before then, even if I ask. I type: yes, create both as drafts. Two draft links come back.

So what do you do with your list for the next eleven days? Two things. LinkedIn starts at launch, inside its daily pace. And you email people who already know you: past customers, past enquiries, people who've replied to you before. That's not cold email. Those are warm contacts, and Funnel OS can draft a short personal note to them from your own mailbox, with the same tagged link and its own source, so they show up as their own row.

While you wait, do the sum. Mailboxes times fifty times the working days left before your event, divided by five, because each person can get up to five emails. That's how many people cold email can reach for this event. If it's too few, add mailboxes. Never push one mailbox harder.

[SCREEN: Tantra app > Campaigns > the cold email A draft > step list with five steps and delays > open step 1 > subject and body > Send test > the test email in the inbox with merge fields filled > blur all email addresses]

Now I check it in the Tantra app. Campaigns, the A draft. Five steps, with their delays. I open step one: the subject and body match the approved copy. I send a test to myself. In my inbox, the first name is filled, the company is filled, and the link opens my landing page with the right tags in the address. If a merge field shows up as raw text in curly brackets, it's misspelled, and it would go to everyone that way. I check the B draft the same way.

Then the list itself. Open the contacts on each draft and check two things. First, A and B don't overlap: nobody is in both halves. Second, nobody on these lists is also on your calendar invite list or your LinkedIn list for the same event, unless you chose that on purpose. One person, one campaign, one thread. Funnel OS checks this for you if you ask: are any contacts in more than one of my campaigns for this event? Ask it before every launch.

Here's the gate before cold email starts, and Funnel OS reads it back to you on the launch day: mailboxes past their warm-up date, a clean list with no overlap, every step read inside Tantra, an opt-out line on every step, tagged links, and a booking link that opens in a real browser and shows free slots. Six checks. If one fails, cold email waits.

[SLIDE 4] Now LinkedIn. This one doesn't need a warm-up, so it can start at launch. But it has its own pace. Fifteen to twenty connection requests a day per profile, never more than two hundred a week. The note is under three hundred characters. Messages only go to people who accept, on days zero, three, seven, twelve and twenty-five after they accept. Requests nobody answered are withdrawn after fourteen days. And never, ever a second automation tool on the same profile.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Draft my LinkedIn campaigns in Tantra: note A and note B, each on half the LinkedIn list, the five messages after acceptance" > the reply shows two campaigns, the notes, the five messages and the daily limit > type "Yes, create both as drafts"]

I type: draft my LinkedIn campaigns in Tantra, note A and note B, each on half the LinkedIn list, the five messages after acceptance. Two campaigns again, one per half. Note A opens with the outcome. Note B with the objection. Each has one specific detail about the person, one line on the problem, and one easy question. No link in the note.

The first message after they accept is useful on its own, with nothing to sell. The event link waits for a later message, and it carries source linkedin and its own variant. One campaign per sender, and each person sits on one sender's list only.

Read the five messages as the person receiving them. Message one, on the day they accept: a thank you and one useful idea, nothing to click. Message two, three days later: a short question about their situation. Message three, a week in: the event, with the link. Message four: one proof point or the one thing they'll learn. Message five, at day twenty-five: a light last note. Each one is under sixty words and sounds like a person, not a campaign.

I type my approval and both drafts are created.

Before LinkedIn goes anywhere, look at the sender's profile. Every person who gets a note clicks the sender's name. If the headline lists job history and the banner promotes last year's event, the note dies on the profile. The headline should say who you help and the outcome, in the same words as your offer sentence, and the featured link should be the same landing page the messages point to.

[SCREEN: Tantra app > Campaigns > LinkedIn A draft > the timeline view showing the request and five messages > the daily limit set to 20]

In Tantra I open the LinkedIn A draft and look at the timeline: the request, then the five messages. I check the daily limit says twenty. Then I open every link in a real browser to make sure the tags survive the click.

[SCREEN: Tantra app > Campaigns > Create Campaign > Omnichannel > steps: email day 0, LinkedIn request day 1, email day 3, LinkedIn message after acceptance, email day 9]

If you chose omnichannel instead, here's how it looks. Campaigns, Create Campaign, Omnichannel. The steps mix channels: an email on day zero, a LinkedIn request the next day, a follow-up email, a LinkedIn message once they accept. Funnel OS drafts it the same way, A and B on half the list each. WhatsApp steps only go to contacts who opted in, so for a cold list, your omnichannel is email plus LinkedIn. And the email steps still wait for warm mailboxes.

How will you know it's working? In our teaching model, cold email brings a visit rate of 1.06 percent per person reached. In our teaching model, LinkedIn brings 0.71 percent. Those are teaching numbers, not targets and not results. After the first run, the only comparison that matters is against your own last week.

[SLIDE 5] Last, replies. A reply is the hottest signal in the whole funnel. You created the webhook from Tantra to the CRM in video twelve. Now check that it covers everything these campaigns produce: email replies, bounces and unsubscribes, LinkedIn acceptances and replies, and WhatsApp replies.

[SCREEN: Tantra app > Connectors > Webhooks > the CRM webhook > every trigger ticked > save > reply to a test email from your own inbox > Tantra CRM > the test contact shows the Replied tag and a task due today > blur all addresses]

Every box is ticked. Then I test it: I reply to my own test email. In the Tantra CRM, the test contact now has the Replied tag and a task due today. And in Tantra, a reply ends that person's thread, so they don't get step three while you're mid conversation.

From launch on, this is your daily habit. Fifteen minutes. Open the reply inbox in Tantra and answer every real reply the same day, with the booking link. Bounces and unsubscribes go on the do-not-contact list automatically. And if a mailbox's bounces climb above five percent, pause that mailbox's campaign the same day. Never push through. A paused mailbox recovers. A burned domain mostly doesn't, and you'd be back to fourteen days of warm-up on a new one.

Next, draft your campaigns, read every step, send yourself a test, and reply to it. Then open the next video, Calendar Invite Campaigns.

Screen recording shot list

  1. Funnel OS chat, the cold email draft request, the plan with two campaigns, five steps and the tagged link, scroll to the warm-up date line, type the approval, show the draft links (about 90 seconds)
  2. Tantra app, Campaigns, cold email A draft, the five steps and delays, step 1 subject and body, Send test; the inbox with merge fields filled; open the link and show the tags in the address bar. Blur every email address (about 75 seconds)
  3. Funnel OS chat, the LinkedIn draft request, the two notes and five messages, type the approval (about 60 seconds)
  4. Tantra app, LinkedIn A draft timeline and daily limit of 20 (about 30 seconds)
  5. Tantra app, Create Campaign, Omnichannel, the mixed steps, without saving (about 40 seconds)
  6. Tantra app webhook settings with every trigger ticked; reply to a test email; Tantra CRM test contact with the Replied tag and a task. Blur the webhook address and every email (about 50 seconds)

Build Score checklist

  • [ ] Cold email A and B (or omnichannel A and B) drafted in Tantra, each on half the list, five steps, held until the mailboxes are warm (1 point)
  • [ ] LinkedIn A and B drafted, each on half the list, daily limit 20 or under, five messages after acceptance (1 point)
  • [ ] Every step read in Tantra, a test send checked for merge fields and tagged links (1 point)
  • [ ] Reply webhook covers email, LinkedIn and WhatsApp, and a test reply lands in the Tantra CRM (1 point)
scripts/V24-calendar-invite-campaigns.md

V24: Calendar Invite Campaigns

  • Module: M5 Traffic
  • Day: Day 3
  • Length: about 9 minutes (about 1,100 to 1,250 spoken words)
  • Systems on screen: Tantra app, Funnel OS, Tantra CRM
  • Before you start: calendar invite senders connected in Tantra and ramping since Day 0 (V11), the reply and RSVP webhook to the CRM created (V12), the event line and date set (V07), the calendar invite experiment in your log (V22)
  • Done when: the calendar invite campaigns are saved as drafts in Tantra, and a test Yes RSVP from your own calendar registers you in the Tantra CRM with source calendar

Slides

Slide 1: The Invite Lands in Their Calendar

  • reads as a meeting, not an ad
  • one decision: yes, maybe or no
  • yes and maybe register them

Slide 2: Copy Rules

  • meeting style title
  • no $, % or "free"
  • time zone in line one
  • no merge fields

Slide 3: Sender Pace

  • 20 a day per sender to day 60
  • then 50, never above 200
  • send 8 to 12 days before

Slide 4: RSVPs Into the CRM

  • Yes and Maybe: registered
  • No: declined, left alone
  • count registrations, not RSVPs

Slide 5: When the Counters Read Zero

  • check the sender's own calendar
  • never build a second campaign
  • people would be invited twice

Voice script

[SLIDE 1] You launch a calendar invite campaign, open the dashboard an hour later, and every counter reads zero. So you build it again. And now everyone on your list has two invites from you for the same session. That one mistake costs more than any bad title.

In this video, Funnel OS drafts your calendar invite campaigns in Tantra, you check every field, and you connect the RSVPs so a Yes or a Maybe registers that person in the Tantra CRM, exactly as if they'd filled in your form. Then their reminders start on their own.

A calendar invite lands in the buyer's own calendar. It reads as a meeting, not an ad, and they make one decision: yes, maybe or no. It can reach a lot of people. Shweta's coaching group, Growth Idea, reached 99,859 people for their best webinar, and 98,000 of those were reached by calendar invite.

[SLIDE 2] The rules first, because the invite is the only copy they read. The title is named like a meeting: plain words, the topic and the outcome, short enough to read in a calendar grid. No dollar sign, no percent, no "free". The description's first line is the date, the time and the time zone, in words. Most people only see the preview, and each guest gets their own calendar's default reminder, so the time has to be right in line one. The next two lines say who it's for and what they leave with. Then one line that makes declining easy: "If it's not relevant, just decline. No follow-up from me." Then the details, and the link lower down.

No merge fields. The text is identical for every guest. A first name field in a calendar invite either breaks or looks like a mail merge, and neither helps.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Draft my calendar invite campaigns in Tantra: title A and title B from my experiment log, each on half the calendar list, from my calendar senders, as drafts" > the reply shows the plan: two campaigns, senders, guests per day, the event time and time zone, the description and the tagged link]

[SLIDE 3] In Funnel OS I type: draft my calendar invite campaigns in Tantra, title A and title B from my experiment log, each on half the calendar list, from my calendar senders, as drafts.

Here's the plan. Two campaigns, one per title. The senders are the users on your separate calendar invite Workspace from video eleven, never your main domain and never your cold email domains. Each sender is set to twenty guests a day, because the Workspace is younger than sixty days. After day sixty, that goes to fifty. Never above two hundred a day. Tantra spreads the list across your senders and over the following days rather than going over anyone's limit.

The link carries source calendar, medium invite, and variant a or b as the content. The event time matches your webinar platform to the minute. And the send date: eight to twelve days before the event. Earlier and they forget. Later and their calendar is already full.

Do the capacity sum before you approve. Senders times guests a day times the days in your sending window. Three senders at twenty a day over ten days is six hundred invites. If your calendar list is bigger than that, the rest go to your next event, or you add senders. You never raise one sender past its limit to squeeze them in.

Read the description out loud. Here's mine, roughly: "Thursday the twentieth, eleven in the morning London time. For founders of coaching businesses who want more booked calls from the list they already have. You'll leave with the three step plan we use, and the one number to fix first. If it's not relevant, just decline. No follow-up from me." Then two short lines of detail and the link. If the promise isn't visible in the first two lines, it's below the preview, and most people will never see it.

I type my approval to create them as drafts. Funnel OS creates drafts only. It never uses the route that sends at once.

[SCREEN: Tantra app > Campaigns > the calendar invite A draft > Name and Tags > Audience > Senders with Guests/day > Event Details: title, Time Zone, start and end, Location / Link, description > UTM Tracking with Enable UTM Parameters ticked and source calendar > Review]

Now I check it in the Tantra app. Name and Tags. Audience: half the calendar list. Senders: every calendar sender ticked, each with twenty guests a day. Event Details: the title, the time zone, the start and end time, the registration link in Location, and the description with the time zone in line one. UTM Tracking: Enable UTM Parameters is ticked, and the source says calendar. Then Review: the contact count, the event time, the number of senders, the link. I stop here. Nothing launches until launch day, with my approval in my own words.

[SCREEN: Tantra app > Name and Tags > Link to Webinar Broadcast > your webinar platform's session selected > Register contacts who RSVP yes or maybe switched on]

One setting to catch now. If your webinar platform shows up under Link to Webinar Broadcast, pick your session and switch on register contacts who RSVP yes or maybe. It can't be added after the campaign is built.

[SLIDE 4] Now the RSVPs. The webhook from Tantra to the CRM you created in video twelve carries calendar RSVPs too. A Yes or a Maybe registers the person in the Tantra CRM: a contact, the Registered tag, source calendar, a deal in Form Filled. So the confirmation and every reminder from video nineteen start for them, exactly like a form sign-up. A No gets tagged Declined, and nothing else goes to them.

[SCREEN: Tantra app > Connectors > Webhooks > the CRM webhook > calendar RSVP yes, maybe and no ticked > Funnel OS chat > type "Send a one-person test invite to my second address" > type the approval > Google Calendar on the second account > the invite > click Yes > Tantra CRM > the contact with Registered tag and source calendar > blur all addresses]

I check the webhook: RSVP yes, maybe and no are all ticked. Then a real test. I ask Funnel OS to send a one-person test invite to my own second address, and I type my approval, because even a test is a real send. In that calendar, the invite shows the title and the time zone in the preview. I click Yes. In the CRM, there's the contact: Registered, source calendar, deal in Form Filled, and the confirmation is queued. That's the done check.

Maybe matters more than people think. FintechCircle, Susanne's group in London, sent 38,138 invites for episode four and got 187 Yes and 221 Maybe RSVPs. There were more Maybes than Yeses. If only a Yes registered someone, more than half the interested people would never get a reminder.

[SLIDE 5] After launch, two rules. First, report registrations, not RSVPs. An accepted invite isn't a sign-up until it's in your CRM, and the dashboard counts it by source calendar. In our teaching model, calendar invites bring a visit rate of 0.17 percent per person reached. That's a teaching number, not a target. Compare against your own last run.

Second, the counters. They can read zero for a while after invites have gone out. Check the campaign says running, then open one sender's own Google Calendar and look for the event with guests on it. Never build a second campaign because a counter reads zero. Everyone would be invited twice.

And raise each sender's daily limit once a week while replies and bounces stay healthy, up to fifty after day sixty.

Next, draft both campaigns, check every field, and run the one-person test until your Yes shows up in the CRM. Then open the next video, Content OS 1: Your Context and Your First Batch.

Screen recording shot list

  1. Funnel OS chat, the calendar invite draft request, the plan with two campaigns, senders at 20 a day, the event time, the description and the tagged link, type the approval (about 75 seconds)
  2. Tantra app, Campaigns, calendar invite A draft, each screen: Name and Tags, Audience, Senders with Guests/day, Event Details, UTM Tracking, Review. Do not click Launch. Blur sender addresses and the contact list (about 90 seconds)
  3. Tantra app, Link to Webinar Broadcast and the yes or maybe switch (about 20 seconds)
  4. Tantra app webhook with the three RSVP triggers ticked; Funnel OS one-person test invite with the typed approval; the second account's Google Calendar, the invite preview, click Yes; Tantra CRM contact with Registered and source calendar. Blur all addresses and the webhook address (about 75 seconds)
  5. One sender's own Google Calendar showing the event with guests, for the "counters read zero" point. Blur guest names and addresses (about 20 seconds)

Build Score checklist

  • [ ] Calendar invite A and B drafted in Tantra: meeting style title, time zone in line one, no merge fields, tagged link, 20 guests a day per sender (1 point)
  • [ ] Send date set 8 to 12 days before the event and every field checked on the Review screen, nothing launched (1 point)
  • [ ] A test Yes RSVP registers you in the Tantra CRM with source calendar and the Registered tag (1 point)
scripts/V25-content-os-context-and-first-batch.md

V25: Content OS 1: Your Context and Your First Batch

  • Module: M5 Traffic
  • Day: Day 3
  • Length: about 11 minutes (about 1,400 to 1,550 spoken words)
  • Systems on screen: Content OS
  • Before you start: your Funnel Brief is approved in Funnel OS, your event line is set and your registration page is live
  • Done when: two weeks of dated posts, written and imaged, are waiting for approval in the Content OS

Slides

Slide 1: Why Founder Posts Stall

  • a blank box every morning
  • generic lines with no proof
  • no link back to the event

Slide 2: The Context Bucket

  • offer, method and buyer from your brief
  • one voice section per person who posts
  • every fact with its source
  • a registry of angles already used

Slide 3: Formats and Cadence

  • three formats to start
  • posts per week per profile
  • one a day is the ceiling
  • same days, same time

Slide 4: The Batch in Five Moves

  • date every post first
  • you pick the ideas worth building
  • copy and images to your brand
  • numbers listed for your sign-off

Slide 5: Rules That Keep Posts True

  • no source, no number
  • clients named only with a written yes
  • an angle never runs twice

Slide 6: Done When

  • two weeks of dated posts
  • every image rendered
  • waiting in the approval queue

Voice script

[SLIDE 1] You know the morning. You promised yourself you'd post today, it's already ten past eight, and you're staring at an empty box on LinkedIn trying to sound clever. You write three lines, delete two, and post something that could have come from anyone. Or you don't post at all.

In this video we fix that for the next thirty days. You'll set up the Content OS once, feed it everything Funnel OS already knows about your business, choose your formats and your cadence, and write your first two weeks of posts in one sitting. Your job from now on is one decision a week: which ideas are worth building. The Content OS does the rest, and nothing goes public until you approve it. That part is the next video.

Here's why this matters for the funnel you built. Content is the slowest channel you have, and it's also the only one that compounds. A post keeps working after the day it goes out. And it warms up everyone your outreach touches. When someone gets your calendar invite or your LinkedIn message, the first thing they do is look at your profile. If the last post there is from March, you've lost them. If there are three useful posts from this week, you've earned the click.

[SCREEN: Content OS > sign in with your Tantra account > the empty home screen shows Context, Formats, Batches and Approvals in the sidebar]

This is the Content OS. It lives in the Tantra app, at content dot usetantra dot com, and you sign in with the same account you already use. Down the left you can see the four places we'll work: Context, Formats, Batches and Approvals.

[SLIDE 2] Let's start with the context bucket. Ever read a post and known within one line that a machine wrote it? That happens when the writer has nothing true to say about you, so it fills the gap with generic claims. The context bucket is the fix. It's a set of plain files that hold what you sell, who buys it, how you talk, what's true, and what you've already posted.

[SCREEN: Content OS > Context > click "Import from Funnel Brief" > the files fill: Company, Positioning, Voice, Facts, Brand, Event, Case Studies, Registry]

You never build this from a blank page. Click Import from Funnel Brief and watch it fill. Company holds what you sell and your price. Positioning holds your offer sentence word for word, your method and its steps, and your buyer's pain in their own words. Event holds your event title, the date, the time with its time zone, and the registration link. Every post that promotes your event copies from here, so if the date ever changes, you change it once in Funnel OS and it flows through.

Now open Voice. There's one section for each person who posts. If it's just you, that's one section. If your co-founder posts too, add them. For each person, write who they are, what they talk about, how they sound, and the lines they never cross. Add your time zone and the time you want your posts to go out.

One more thing in Positioning: your content pillars. These are three to six themes every post ladders up to. The Content OS suggests them from your method steps and your buyer's biggest challenge, and you confirm them. A good set for most of you is one pillar per step of your method, plus one for the mistakes your buyer makes, plus one for proof. Pillars keep a batch balanced, so you don't end up with six posts on the same idea.

[SCREEN: Content OS > Context > Voice > add a profile > name, personal profile or company page, time zone, posting time 09:30]

Then Facts. This is the file that keeps you out of trouble. Every number a post is allowed to use sits here as a row, with its source and the name of the person who approves it. Your own results come from your Funnel Brief. Anything the Content OS cannot trace to a source goes to the bottom, under Unverified, and it is never used. Not rounded, not softened. Never.

[SCREEN: Content OS > Context > Facts > show three rows with a source column and an approver column > scroll to the Unverified section at the bottom]

Case Studies is the same idea for clients. A client goes in only if they said yes in writing to being named. If you don't have one yet, leave it empty. An empty file is far better than a borrowed result.

The last file is the Registry. It's every angle you've already posted, so the Content OS never offers you the same idea twice. If you've posted on LinkedIn before, paste your last ten posts in here and they count as used.

The registry grows on its own after that. Every batch adds its angles, including the posts you reject, so a rejected idea never comes back unless you ask for it.

[SLIDE 3] Now formats and cadence. Open Formats. Start with three, not ten. A carousel that teaches one step of your method. A text playbook, which is a post with numbered steps. And one proof format, which is a real screenshot or a real result from your Facts file.

[SCREEN: Content OS > Formats > tick Carousel, Text playbook and Proof post > the other formats stay unticked]

Then the cadence. Set how many posts each profile puts out per week. One post per profile per day is the ceiling, not the target. For most members I'd start with three a week, Monday, Wednesday and Friday, at the same time each day in your buyer's time zone. Same days, same time. Consistency beats volume here.

[SCREEN: Content OS > Formats > Cadence > Mon, Wed, Fri > 09:30 > the buyer's time zone]

While you're here, set your call to action. The main one is your event: the Content OS fills it in from the Event file. Then set the fallback, which is what posts promote once the event has passed. That's your next event date if you have one, or your booking link. A post dated after your event must never promote it. The Content OS refuses to build one that does.

[SLIDE 4] Now the first batch. Click Batches, then New batch. Ask for two weeks. With three posts a week, that's six posts per profile.

[SCREEN: Content OS > Batches > New batch > two weeks > must-cover topics box: type "the three mistakes buyers make before they hire us" > click Plan]

There's a box for must-cover topics. Put in anything you want this batch to talk about. If your buyers keep asking the same question on calls, that's a topic. Then click Plan.

The first thing it does is put a date and a time on every post. It does that before writing a single word, so that when a post says "this Thursday", it really is this Thursday on the day it goes out. That one rule stops most of the embarrassing mistakes I see.

[SCREEN: Content OS > the batch plan > a table of six dated slots per profile, each with a format and a call to action badge]

Next it writes one idea per post, from your context bucket. And here's the only moment it stops and waits for you. Read the ideas. Tick the ones worth building. Untick the ones that feel generic, or that you wouldn't say out loud to a buyer. If you untick one, it writes a replacement.

[SCREEN: Content OS > Batches > Ideas > tick five, untick one > click "Replace" on the unticked idea > a new idea appears > click "Build the batch"]

This takes me about five minutes a week, and it's the most important five minutes in the whole system. You're the editor. The Content OS is the team.

Click Build the batch. Now it writes each post to its format and builds each image to your brand colours and fonts. A carousel comes out as a set of pages. A proof post uses the real screenshot you gave it. It checks each one against your rules before it shows you anything.

[SCREEN: Content OS > Batches > Batch 1 > building > the post cards fill one by one with copy and images]

[SLIDE 5] While it builds, three rules worth knowing. First, no source, no number. Every number in every post is listed on that post's card under Numbers to sign off, so you can clear the whole batch in one pass. Second, a client is named only with their written yes. Third, an angle never runs twice. A fact can come back, but only with a new hook, a new argument and a new takeaway.

[SCREEN: Content OS > Batch 1 > open one post card > show the copy, the image, the first comment with its tagged link, and the Numbers to sign off panel]

Let's open one finished post. Here's the copy, the image, and underneath, the first comment. That's where the link to your registration page goes, and it already carries its tags so you'll know exactly which post brought each sign-up. We'll go deep on those tags in video twenty seven. And here's the Numbers to sign off panel. Read every line. If a number is wrong, or you'd rather not say it, leave a note on the card and the Content OS rewrites that post.

[SCREEN: Content OS > the post card > type a note "drop the second number, keep the first" > click Rewrite > the updated copy appears]

One thing I want you to notice. Nothing here is public. The batch sits in your approval queue, and it stays there until you approve each post. Scheduling and publishing happen only after you say so, in your own words.

[SLIDE 6] So here's where you should be. Your context bucket is filled from your Funnel Brief. Your voice, facts and registry are checked. You've chosen three formats and a cadence. And two weeks of dated posts, written and imaged, are sitting in the approval queue.

Next week, it's even faster. The Content OS reads last week's results before it writes, so the batch gets better each time. Your first batch takes about forty minutes. By week three it's closer to fifteen.

Do this now: build your first two week batch and leave it in the approval queue. Then watch the next video, Content OS 2: Approve and Publish on Every Channel.

Screen recording shot list

  1. Content OS home screen after sign in, sidebar visible with Context, Formats, Batches, Approvals. Blur the account email in the top corner. About 15 seconds.
  2. Context > Import from Funnel Brief, the nine files filling one after another. About 30 seconds.
  3. Context > Event file open: title, date, time with time zone, registration link visible. About 15 seconds.
  4. Context > Voice: add one profile with name, profile type, time zone and 09:30. About 30 seconds.
  5. Context > Facts: three rows with source and approver columns, then scroll to Unverified. Use a demo business, no real client data. About 30 seconds.
  6. Context > Case Studies (empty) and Registry with pasted past posts. About 20 seconds.
  7. Formats: tick Carousel, Text playbook, Proof post. Cadence set to Mon, Wed, Fri at 09:30. Call to action and fallback filled. About 45 seconds.
  8. Batches > New batch > two weeks > type the must-cover topic > Plan. Show the dated plan table. About 40 seconds.
  9. Ideas gate: tick five, untick one, Replace, then Build the batch. About 45 seconds.
  10. Batch building, cards filling with copy and images. Speed up 4x. About 30 seconds.
  11. One finished post card: copy, image, first comment with its tagged link, Numbers to sign off panel. Leave a note and Rewrite. About 60 seconds.
  12. Approval queue showing six posts waiting, none scheduled. About 15 seconds.

Build Score checklist

  • [ ] Context bucket imported from the Funnel Brief, with Voice, Facts and Registry checked (1 point)
  • [ ] Three formats, a weekly cadence, the event call to action and the fallback set (1 point)
  • [ ] First two week batch written and imaged, waiting in the approval queue (1 point)
scripts/V26-content-os-approve-and-publish.md

V26: Content OS 2: Approve and Publish on Every Channel

  • Module: M5 Traffic
  • Day: Day 4
  • Length: about 10 minutes (about 1,300 to 1,450 spoken words)
  • Systems on screen: Content OS
  • Before you start: your first two week batch is waiting in the approval queue (V25)
  • Done when: your publishing accounts are connected, the batch is approved and scheduled, and the first post has gone out with its first comment and a working tagged link

Slides

Slide 1: Approve From Your Phone

  • each post as it looks in the feed
  • one approval box per reviewer
  • notes the whole team can see

Slide 2: Connect Each Account Once

  • LinkedIn profile and company page
  • X, Instagram, YouTube, TikTok
  • Instagram needs a Creator or Business account

Slide 3: What No App Can Post

  • Facebook personal profiles
  • Facebook groups
  • TikTok first comments

Slide 4: Schedule Only After You Say So

  • approved by every reviewer
  • your approval in your own words
  • a day between approval and the first post

Slide 5: Check the First Post

  • live at its time
  • first comment under it
  • tagged link opens your page

Slide 6: Done When

  • accounts connected
  • batch approved and scheduled
  • first post checked live

Voice script

[SLIDE 1] Here's how most content plans die. The posts are written. They're sitting in a folder. And then they sit there, because approving them means opening a laptop, copying each one into LinkedIn, pasting the link, remembering to add the comment, and doing it again on Wednesday. By Friday nobody has posted anything.

In this video you'll approve your batch from your phone in about ten minutes, connect each of your social accounts to the Content OS once, and schedule the whole batch so every post goes out on its day, at its time, with the first comment already underneath. Then we'll check the first one live together.

[SCREEN: phone > Content OS > Approvals > Batch 1 > the first post shows as it looks in the LinkedIn feed, with the image and the first comment]

Open the Content OS on your phone and tap Approvals. Every post in the batch shows the way it will look in the feed: your name, the first lines before "see more", the image or the carousel you can swipe, and the first comment as you. That matters. You're not reviewing a document. You're seeing what your buyer will see while they scroll.

Under each post there's an approval box for each reviewer. If it's just you, there's one box. If a co-founder or a teammate also reviews, they get their own box, and a post counts as approved only when every box is ticked.

[SCREEN: phone > tick "Approved by me" on post one > swipe to post two > type a note "make the hook shorter" > tap Send]

Read each one like a buyer would. Is the first line strong enough to stop the scroll? Is every number true? Would you say this out loud on a call? If yes, tick it. If no, leave a note. The note goes to the Content OS and to anyone else reviewing, and the post is rewritten. You'll see the new version appear on the same card, waiting for your tick.

My rule: if I'd hesitate to read a post out loud to a buyer, I don't approve it. It's that simple.

Three things I check on every post. The first two lines, because that's all anyone sees before they decide to tap see more. If those two lines could belong to anyone, it goes back. The first page of a carousel, because it works like a headline: one promise, big enough to read on a phone. And the first comment. It should say in one line what the event solves, then the link on its own line. If the comment just says register here, that's a wasted chance. Tell people what they get.

[SLIDE 2] Now let's connect your accounts. You do this once.

[SCREEN: laptop > Content OS > Settings > Publishing accounts > the list: LinkedIn profile, LinkedIn company page, X, Instagram, YouTube, TikTok, each with a Connect button]

Go to Settings, then Publishing accounts. You'll see each channel the Content OS can post to: your LinkedIn profile, your LinkedIn company page, X, Instagram, YouTube and TikTok. Click Connect next to LinkedIn. You sign in to LinkedIn in its own window and approve the access. The Content OS never sees your password.

[SCREEN: Content OS > Publishing accounts > Connect LinkedIn > LinkedIn sign in window, blur the email field > Allow > back to the list, LinkedIn shows Connected with your name]

For the company page, connect it the same way, signed in as a person who is an admin of that page.

Instagram needs one change first. A personal Instagram account can't be posted to by any app. So open Instagram on your phone, go to Settings, Account type, and switch to a Creator account. It's free, it takes two taps, and your profile stays exactly the same. Then come back and click Connect.

YouTube and TikTok connect the same way: click Connect, sign in, allow. On YouTube, the Content OS posts your video and adds the first comment under it. If you don't make video yet, leave those two for later. Nothing breaks if a channel isn't connected. Posts meant for it simply wait in the queue.

[SCREEN: phone > Instagram > Settings > Account type and tools > Switch to professional account > Creator > Done]

[SLIDE 3] Now, three honest limits, so nobody wastes an afternoon. Facebook personal profiles cannot be posted to by any app. Meta took that away years ago. Facebook groups are the same. If those matter to you, the Content OS gives you a Copy post button and a Copy first comment button, and you post them by hand in about thirty seconds. And TikTok has no way for any app to add a comment, so on TikTok, put your link in your bio and say so in the caption.

Start with the channels where your buyers actually are. For most of you selling to businesses, that's LinkedIn first. Connect two channels well rather than six badly.

[SLIDE 4] Now scheduling. Go back to the batch.

[SCREEN: Content OS > Batches > Batch 1 > the status bar reads "6 of 6 approved" > click Schedule]

The Content OS only schedules posts that every reviewer has approved. Anything still waiting stays in the queue, untouched. Click Schedule and it shows you the plan: each post, its account, its date and time in your time zone, and the first comment it will add.

[SCREEN: Content OS > Schedule plan > table of posts with account, date, time, time zone, first comment preview > the approval box: type "Yes, schedule batch 1 as shown" > click Confirm]

Read the plan. Then type your approval in your own words. Something like, yes, schedule batch one as shown. That's on purpose. A scheduled post goes public on its date, so the Content OS needs your words, not a stray click.

One habit I recommend: leave at least a day between approval and the first post. It gives you one more chance to reread the batch with fresh eyes. Scheduled posts stay editable right up to their time. If you spot something, open the post, edit it, and it updates in the schedule.

[SCREEN: Content OS > Calendar view > the two weeks with posts on Mon, Wed and Fri > click one post > Edit > change one word > Save > the calendar shows it updated]

Here's the calendar view. Two weeks, posts on Monday, Wednesday and Friday. If you post from a company page as well as your profile, they show as two rows, and the Content OS spreads them so they don't land at the same minute.

A note on reposts. When your company page posts, reshare it from your profile with one line of your own. When your profile posts, ask two or three teammates to comment in the first hour with something real, not "great post". Early comments help a post reach further, and they're a lot more useful than likes.

And reply to every comment on your own posts within the day. A comment is a person raising their hand. If someone asks a real question under a post, answer it in the thread, then send them a direct message with the registration link. That's not spam. They asked.

[SLIDE 5] Now the part most people skip. When the first post goes out, check it.

[SCREEN: LinkedIn on phone > your profile > the post just published > the first comment under it from you > tap the link > the registration page opens]

Open LinkedIn on your phone at the post's time. The post is there. The first comment is there, from you, with the link. Tap the link. Your registration page should open. Now look at the address bar. You should see the tags on the end of the link: source LinkedIn, medium organic, the campaign name, and the content tag for this post. If the link opens and the tags are there, every sign-up from this post will be counted against it.
