[SCREEN: phone browser > the registration page with the address bar expanded, tags visible > back to Content OS > Batches > Batch 1 > post one shows "Published" with a link to the live post]

Back in the Content OS, the post now shows Published, with a link to the live post. If something went wrong, say the account was disconnected, the post shows Failed with the reason, and nothing else in the batch is affected. Reconnect the account and click Retry.

The most common reason is a password change on LinkedIn, which disconnects every connected app. If you change your password, reconnect the account the same day.

Check the first post on every channel you connected. After that, a quick look once a week is enough. Ten minutes on this check saves you two weeks of posts with a link that goes nowhere.

[SLIDE 6] So here's where you should be. Your publishing accounts are connected. Your first batch is approved from your phone and scheduled, two weeks out. And the first post is live, with its first comment and a tagged link that opens your registration page.

From now on your week looks like this. Monday morning, five minutes picking ideas. Later that day, ten minutes approving on your phone. Then the posts go out on their own while you run the rest of the funnel.

Do this now: connect your accounts, approve and schedule batch one, and check the first post live. Then watch the next video, Content OS 3: Posts That Fill Your Funnel.

Screen recording shot list

  1. Phone screen recording: Content OS > Approvals > Batch 1, first post in feed view with image and first comment. About 20 seconds.
  2. Phone: tick the approval box on post one, leave a note on post two, show the rewritten version arriving. About 45 seconds.
  3. Laptop: Content OS > Settings > Publishing accounts list. About 15 seconds.
  4. Connect LinkedIn: the LinkedIn sign in window. Blur the email field and never show the password field filled. Back to Connected. About 30 seconds.
  5. Phone: Instagram > Settings > Account type > switch to Creator. Blur the account handle if it is not a demo account. About 25 seconds.
  6. Content OS > the post card buttons Copy post and Copy first comment, for Facebook profile and groups. About 15 seconds.
  7. Batch 1 > "6 of 6 approved" > Schedule > the plan table > type the approval words > Confirm. About 45 seconds.
  8. Calendar view, two weeks; open one post, edit one word, save. About 30 seconds.
  9. Phone: LinkedIn post live with first comment, tap the link, registration page opens, address bar expanded to show the tags. Use a demo profile or blur commenter names. About 40 seconds.
  10. Content OS > post one shows Published; show an example Failed state with Retry. About 20 seconds.

Build Score checklist

  • [ ] Publishing accounts connected in the Content OS, with Instagram switched to Creator or Business if you use it (1 point)
  • [ ] Batch one approved on your phone and scheduled with your approval in your own words (1 point)
  • [ ] First post checked live: first comment present and the tagged link opens your registration page (1 point)
scripts/V27-content-os-posts-that-fill-your-funnel.md

V27: Content OS 3: Posts That Fill Your Funnel

  • Module: M5 Traffic
  • Day: Day 4
  • Length: about 9 minutes (about 1,150 to 1,300 spoken words)
  • Systems on screen: Content OS, Tantra CRM
  • Before you start: your publishing accounts are connected and batch one is scheduled (V26)
  • Done when: a LinkedIn event points at your registration page, at least 40% of your pre-event posts promote the event, and a test sign-up from one post shows its source in the CRM

Slides

Slide 1: Likes Do Not Fill Seats

  • posts exist to fill the event
  • one clear ask, said plainly
  • every sign-up traced to its post

Slide 2: The LinkedIn Event

  • created on your profile or page
  • links to your registration page
  • an Attend click is not a sign-up

Slide 3: The Promotion Share

  • at least 40% before the event
  • a countdown, true on the day
  • one registration post every week
  • after the event, the fallback

Slide 4: One Tag Set Per Post

  • source: the channel
  • medium: organic
  • campaign: the event
  • content: the post

Slide 5: Read in This Order

  • registrations first
  • then clicks
  • then impressions

Slide 6: Done When

  • LinkedIn event live
  • share at 40% or more
  • test sign-up traced in the CRM

Voice script

[SLIDE 1] I've watched founders post every day for a month, get hundreds of likes, and fill four seats. The posts were good. They just never asked anyone to do anything, or they asked so quietly nobody noticed.

In this video we turn your content into registrations. You'll set up a LinkedIn event that sends people to your page, make sure enough of your posts actually promote the event, tag every post so you know which one brought each sign-up, and learn the order to read the results in. Likes come last.

[SLIDE 2] Start with the LinkedIn event. It gives your webinar its own page on LinkedIn, it shows up for your connections, and every post you share from it carries the date and time.

[SCREEN: LinkedIn > your profile > Create > Event > Online > Event format: External event link > paste the registration page link with its tags > name, date, time and time zone from your event line > Create]

On LinkedIn, click Create, then Event. Choose Online, and for the format choose an external event link. Paste your registration page link here, with its tags. Funnel OS gives you that link: just ask it for the LinkedIn event link and it adds source LinkedIn, medium organic, your event as the campaign, and LinkedIn event as the content tag. Then copy the title, the date, the time and the time zone straight from your event line, so they match your page exactly.

Here's the trap. When someone clicks Attend on LinkedIn, that is not a registration. They haven't given you an email, and they won't get a single reminder from you. So the event description opens with one line: to get the link and the reminders, register here. And then the link again.

[SCREEN: LinkedIn > the event page > Share > Invite connections > pick connections who match your buyer > Send]

Once it's live, share the event as a post, and invite your connections who match your buyer. Not everyone. The people you'd actually want on the call.

Then use the event page itself. Post in it once or twice before the day. Everyone who clicked Attend can see what you post there, so use it to remind them that the link and the reminders come from registering on your page. On the day, post there again with the time it starts.

[SLIDE 3] Now the share of posts that promote. Open the Content OS and go to your batch.

[SCREEN: Content OS > Batches > Batch 1 > the summary bar shows "Promotes the event: 3 of 6, 50%" > the call to action badge on each promoting post]

At the top you'll see how many posts in the batch promote your event. The rule is at least 40% of your posts before the event. Below that, people simply don't notice there's an event. The Content OS sets 40% by default, and you can raise it as the date gets closer.

Those promoting posts run as a countdown. Two weeks out, one week out, this Thursday, tomorrow. That's why the Content OS dates every post before it writes it. "Tomorrow" has to be true on the day the post goes out.

On top of that, one post every week is a pure registration post. Not a teaching post with a link at the bottom. A post whose only job is to say: here's the session, here's the date, here's exactly what you'll walk away with, here's where to register. Say the promise plainly, in your buyer's words. Never make people guess why they should come.

Here's a shape that works. Line one: the outcome, in your buyer's words. Line two: the date, the time and the time zone. Then three short lines on what they'll leave with. Then who it's for, and who it isn't for. Then the ask: the link is in the first comment. That's it. No clever story. A registration post is an invitation, and invitations should be easy to read.

[SCREEN: Content OS > open the weekly registration post > the copy names the outcome in the first line, the date and time zone in the second > the first comment carries the tagged link]

And once the event has passed, no post promotes it. Any post dated on or after the event switches to the fallback you set in the first Content OS video: your next event date, or your booking link. The Content OS refuses to schedule a post that breaks this rule.

[SLIDE 4] Now the tags. Every promoting post gets its own tagged link in the first comment. Four tags. Source is the channel, so LinkedIn. Medium is organic, because you didn't pay for it. Campaign is your event. And content is the post itself, its own short code, so two posts never share a tag.

[SCREEN: Content OS > a post card > first comment > hover the link > the tags show source linkedin, medium organic, campaign your event, content the post code]

You don't type any of this. The Content OS writes the link for each post. But you do need to check that the tags arrive in your CRM, because a tag that gets lost on the way means a sign-up you can't trace.

[SCREEN: phone > tap the link in a published post's first comment > registration page > register with a test address > Tantra CRM > Contacts > search the test address, blur the email > the contact shows source linkedin, medium organic, campaign and content filled]

So do one test. Tap the link in a published post. Register with a test address. Then open the Tantra CRM, find the contact, and look at the source fields. Source LinkedIn, medium organic, your campaign, and the post's own content tag. If all four are there, every real sign-up from your posts will be counted against the post that brought it. Then delete the test contact or mark it as a test, so it doesn't count.

[SLIDE 5] Now how to read the results. Every Monday, before you pick the next batch's ideas, open Results in the Content OS.

[SCREEN: Content OS > Results > last week > table per post: registrations, clicks, impressions > sorted by registrations > roll up by format and by profile]

Read it in this order. Registrations first: which posts brought sign-ups. Then clicks: which posts got people to the page. Then impressions: which posts were seen. Never the other way round. If you only have time for one column, read registrations. A post with ten thousand impressions and no sign-ups did less for your funnel than a post with eight hundred impressions and three sign-ups.

The registrations column comes from your CRM, counted by each post's content tag. The clicks and impressions come from your connected accounts. If a number can't be read yet, it shows as not yet, never as zero.

Then the Content OS writes three lines at the top of next week's ideas. Keep: the formats with the most registrations per post. Change: the weakest one, and what to try instead, maybe a new kind of hook, or moving the call to action to the first page of a carousel. And repeat the mechanism, never the angle: what made the best post work, carried into a new idea.

One warning. Two weeks of six posts is a small sample. If your best post won by two sign-ups, that's a hint, not a pattern. Keep your format mix balanced until a pattern holds for three batches in a row.

And look at registrations by profile too. If your co-founder's posts bring more sign-ups than yours, that's not a competition. It's a sign their voice lands with the buyer, so give their profile more of the promoting posts in the next batch.

[SLIDE 6] So here's where you should be. Your LinkedIn event is live and points at your registration page. At least 40% of your pre-event posts promote the event as a countdown, with one pure registration post every week. Every promoting post has its own tagged link. And one test sign-up shows its source in the CRM.

Do this now: create the LinkedIn event, check your promotion share, and run the test sign-up. Then watch the next video, Ads Built Paused, With a Target Cost.

Screen recording shot list

  1. LinkedIn > Create > Event > Online > External event link; paste the tagged link; fill name, date, time, time zone. Use a demo profile or blur your profile name and photo if preferred. About 50 seconds.
  2. Funnel OS chat: type "Give me the tagged link for my LinkedIn event" and show the link returned. About 15 seconds.
  3. The event description's first line pointing to registration. Then Share and Invite connections; blur every connection name and photo. About 30 seconds.
  4. Content OS > Batch 1 summary bar showing the promotion share and the call to action badges. About 20 seconds.
  5. The weekly registration post card: outcome in line one, date and time zone in line two, tagged first comment. About 25 seconds.
  6. A post card with the first comment link hovered, tags visible. About 15 seconds.
  7. Phone: tap a published post's first comment link, register with a test address. Then Tantra CRM > Contacts > search the test contact; blur the email address; show the four source fields. Mark it as a test. About 60 seconds.
  8. Content OS > Results: per post table sorted by registrations, then roll up by format and profile; show one "not yet" cell. About 40 seconds.
  9. The three lines at the top of next week's ideas: Keep, Change, Repeat the mechanism. About 20 seconds.

Build Score checklist

  • [ ] LinkedIn event created with its tagged link to your registration page and a register line at the top (1 point)
  • [ ] At least 40% of pre-event posts promote the event, with one registration post each week and the fallback after the event (1 point)
  • [ ] Every promoting post carries its own tags, and a test sign-up from one post shows all four tags in the CRM (1 point)
scripts/V28-ads-built-paused-with-a-target-cost.md

V28: Ads Built Paused, With a Target Cost

  • Module: M5 Traffic
  • Day: Day 5
  • Length: about 9 minutes (about 1,150 to 1,300 spoken words)
  • Systems on screen: Meta, LinkedIn, Funnel OS
  • Before you start: your registration page is live with tracking and consent (V16, V17), and the test run passed (V21)
  • Done when: your target cost per sign-up is in your Funnel Brief, a test sign-up shows in the ad platform and in GA4, and two ads are built and paused

Slides

Slide 1: Spending Is Not Buying

  • clicks look like progress
  • judge by cost per sign-up
  • then by cost per buyer

Slide 2: Your Target Cost Per Sign-Up

  • most you would pay per buyer
  • times your show-up rate
  • times your close rate

Slide 3: Tracking Before Money

  • one sign-up event, counted once
  • test sign-up in the ad platform
  • the same sign-up in GA4

Slide 4: Two Ads, One Difference

  • A leads with the outcome
  • B leads with the problem
  • same image style, page and audience

Slide 5: Built Paused

  • registrants excluded
  • small fixed daily budget
  • you switch it on, not the system

Slide 6: The Weekly Ad Rule

  • at or under target: scale
  • in between: change one thing
  • three times target, no sign-ups: pause

Voice script

[SLIDE 1] Paid ads are the fastest way to spend money in this whole course. I've seen founders put a thousand dollars into a campaign, get a lovely click-through rate, and not be able to say whether a single buyer came from it.

In this video you'll do it the other way round. You'll work out what a sign-up is worth to you before you spend anything, check that every sign-up is counted, write two ads that test one idea against another, and build them in Meta and LinkedIn, paused. Switching them on is your decision, and we'll talk about when.

[SLIDE 2] Start with your target. Your target cost per sign-up is the most you'd pay to win one buyer, times your show-up rate, times your close rate.

Let me show you with made-up round numbers, just as an example. Say the most you'd happily pay to win one buyer is three hundred dollars. Say four in ten people who sign up actually show up, and one in ten of those who show up buy. Three hundred, times zero point four, times zero point one, is twelve dollars. So in that example, any ad that brings sign-ups at twelve dollars or less is buying customers at a price you're happy with.

Use your own numbers. If you don't have a show-up rate or a close rate yet, that's normal before your first run. Funnel OS will use the teaching model rates and say so clearly. In our teaching model, show-up is just under thirty percent and close rate just over seventeen percent. Those are a starting point for the maths, not a promise and not a benchmark. You'll replace them with your own after your first run.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Work out my target cost per sign-up. The most I'd pay to win one buyer is 300 dollars." > the reply shows the sum line by line, labels which rates are mine and which are the teaching model, and asks to save it to the Funnel Brief > type "Yes, save it"]

Ask Funnel OS to work it out. It shows the sum, says which rates are yours and which are the teaching model, and asks to save the target to your Funnel Brief. Say yes. From now on, every ad decision gets measured against that one number.

[SLIDE 3] Next, tracking. Before a single dollar is spent, a sign-up has to show up in the ad platform and in your analytics. Otherwise the platform can't tell which ads work and neither can you.

[SCREEN: Meta Events Manager > your dataset > Test events > open your registration page in the test browser > register with a test address > the Lead event appears in the list]

In Meta, open Events Manager, pick your dataset, which is what Meta now calls the pixel, and open Test events. Register on your page with a test address. The Lead event should appear within a minute. For LinkedIn, it's the Insight Tag and a conversion set to your thank-you page. Same test.

[SCREEN: GA4 > Reports > Realtime > the generate_lead event shows one count > then LinkedIn Campaign Manager > Analyze > Conversion tracking > your sign-up conversion shows Active]

Then check GA4 Realtime. One sign-up, counted once. If you see two sign-up events for one person, you've got a duplicate, and every number you read later will be doubled. Fix that now. And when you're done, mark the test contact as a test in the CRM so it never counts.

[SLIDE 4] Now the ads. Two of them, and they differ in one thing only: the angle. Ad A leads with the outcome your buyer wants. Ad B leads with the problem, in your buyer's words, usually their biggest objection. Same image style, same page, same audience. That way, when one wins, you know it was the message.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Write two Meta ads for my webinar. A benefit led, B problem led using my buyer's main objection." > the reply shows for each: primary text with the hook in line one, a headline under 40 characters, a description, a one line image brief with no words in the image, and the tagged link]

Ask Funnel OS for two ads. It writes from your Funnel Brief: the hook in the first line, a headline under forty characters, the body under a hundred and twenty five words, and a one-line image brief. It also gives each ad its own tagged link. Source is Meta or LinkedIn, medium is paid, campaign is your event, and content is variant A or variant B. That last tag is how you'll know which ad brought which sign-up, all the way to the sale. Read both ads. If a claim isn't in your proof list, take it out.

For images, use a real photo of you, or a simple picture in your brand colours with no words in it. Use the same image style on both ads, so the only difference between them is the words.

[SLIDE 5] Now build them, paused.

[SCREEN: Meta Ads Manager > Create > Leads objective > campaign name with the event > ad set: website conversions on the Lead event, countries, a small daily budget > Audience: exclude the registrants custom audience > two ads with the A and B copy and tagged links > leave the campaign toggle Off > Publish draft]

In Meta Ads Manager, create a campaign with the Leads objective. In the ad set, choose website conversions on your Lead event, your buyer's countries, and a small fixed daily budget. In the audience, exclude people who've already signed up. You don't want to pay to reach someone who's already registered. Then add both ads, each with its own tagged link. And before you finish, check the toggle is off. The campaign is built and paused.

[SCREEN: LinkedIn Campaign Manager > Create campaign > Website conversions > audience by job title and company size from your buyer profile > exclude registrants > two single image ads with A and B copy and tagged links > status Paused]

LinkedIn is the same shape. Campaign Manager, website conversions, your audience from your buyer profile, registrants excluded, two ads, paused. LinkedIn costs more per click than Meta, so it earns its place when your buyer is a specific job title at a specific size of company.

Before you leave either platform, open each ad's preview on mobile. Most people will see it on a phone. Tap the link in the preview and check it opens your registration page with the tags on the end. A broken or untagged link in an ad is money spent on sign-ups you can't trace.

How small is a small budget? Think in sign-ups, not dollars. In my made-up example, with a twelve dollar target, a daily budget that could bring one or two sign-ups a day is enough to read within a week or two. Less than that and you'll wait a month to learn anything.

Why paused? Because ads are the only channel here that spends money every hour they run. You switch them on yourself, in Ads Manager, on launch day or later, once your page has converted some cheaper traffic. Meta reviews each ad first, usually within a day. Funnel OS will never switch an ad on or spend for you.

[SLIDE 6] Once they're running, here's the rule, read weekly after several days, not after a few hours. If an ad brings sign-ups at or under your target, scale it: add budget, not new ideas. If it's in between, hold it and change exactly one thing. And if an ad has spent three times your target and brought no sign-ups at all, pause it. In my example, that's thirty six dollars with nothing to show.

Never change the budget and the ad in the same week, or you won't know which one moved the number. And judge by cost per sign-up, then cost per buyer. Never by clicks.

Funnel OS does the sorting for you. Because each ad has its own content tag, the weekly review counts sign-ups per ad from your CRM. Give it the spend per ad, and it tells you which side of the line each one sits on: scale, hold or pause.

So here's where you should be. Your target cost per sign-up is saved in your Funnel Brief. A test sign-up shows in the ad platform and in GA4, once. And two ads, A and B, are built and paused, with their own tagged links and registrants excluded.

Do this now: work out your target and build your two ads, paused. Then watch the next video, Launch Day and the First 24 Hours.

Screen recording shot list

  1. Funnel OS chat: the target cost per sign-up question, the sum, the teaching model label, "Yes, save it". About 40 seconds.
  2. Meta Events Manager > Test events; register a test sign-up; Lead event appears. Blur the test email and the dataset ID. About 40 seconds.
  3. GA4 Realtime showing the sign-up event counted once. About 15 seconds.
  4. LinkedIn Campaign Manager > Conversion tracking showing the sign-up conversion Active. Blur the ad account ID. About 15 seconds.
  5. Funnel OS chat: two ads written, with headlines, body, image briefs and tagged links. About 40 seconds.
  6. Meta Ads Manager: build the campaign, ad set with exclusion and small budget, both ads, toggle Off. Blur the ad account ID and any payment method. About 90 seconds.
  7. LinkedIn Campaign Manager: same build, status Paused. Blur the account ID. About 60 seconds.
  8. Tantra CRM: mark the test contact as a test. Blur the email. About 10 seconds.

Build Score checklist

  • [ ] Target cost per sign-up worked out and saved in your Funnel Brief, and a test sign-up counted once in the ad platform and in GA4 (1 point)
  • [ ] Two ads, A benefit led and B problem led, built paused with their own tagged links and registrants excluded (1 point)
scripts/V29-launch-day-and-the-first-24-hours.md

V29: Launch Day and the First 24 Hours

  • Module: M5 Traffic
  • Day: Day 5 to 7
  • Length: about 9 minutes (about 1,150 to 1,300 spoken words)
  • Systems on screen: Funnel OS, Tantra app, Tantra CRM, Content OS
  • Before you start: the automation register has no line that says Failing (V21), and your Tantra campaigns are built (V23, V24)
  • Done when: every open channel has launched to its smallest readable audience, the first 24 hours check is posted in the group, and a daily 15 minute reply slot is in your calendar

Slides

Slide 1: Small First, Then the Rest

  • a few hundred contacts per channel
  • mistakes stay cheap
  • one registration page, every link tagged

Slide 2: What Runs Before Day 14

  • LinkedIn and calendar invites
  • WhatsApp to opted-in contacts
  • content and your warm list
  • cold email starts on day 14

Slide 3: The Launch Order

  • register shows nothing Failing
  • reminder campaigns started
  • outreach started with your words

Slide 4: The First 24 Hours Check

  • bounces per mailbox
  • LinkedIn warnings
  • sign-ups in the CRM with their source

Slide 5: The Daily 15 Minutes

  • open the reply inbox
  • answer every reply the same day
  • the booking link in every answer

Slide 6: Done When

  • every open channel launched small
  • 24 hour check posted
  • reply slot in your calendar

Voice script

[SLIDE 1] Here's a launch day I see too often. Everything goes to the whole list at once, there's a broken link in the first email, and a sending domain is bouncing before lunch. Two weeks of warm-up, gone in an afternoon.

Today you launch the other way. Small first. In this video you'll start every channel that's ready to a slice just big enough to read, check the first twenty four hours for the things that break, and set up the fifteen minute routine you'll run every single day from now on.

The idea is simple. A first slice of a few hundred contacts per channel shows you bounces, warnings and broken links while they cost you a few hundred contacts, not your whole list. Once the slice is clean, the rest goes out.

[SLIDE 2] Now, which channels are open. Your new mailboxes are still warming up. That takes at least fourteen days, and nothing speeds it up. So cold email waits until day fourteen. Until then, you run on the channels that don't need warm-up.

LinkedIn, inside its daily limits, fifteen to twenty connection requests a day. Calendar invites, at twenty a day per sender. WhatsApp, only to contacts who opted in. Your content, which is already scheduled. And your warm list: past customers, past enquiries, people who already know you, written to from your normal address.

Your warm list is the most underused channel in this course. A short, personal note from your own address: I'm running a session on this date about this problem, I thought of you, here's the link. No sequence, no automation. Just you.

Don't underestimate calendar invites while you wait. When Shweta's coaching group, Growth Idea, ran their best webinar, ninety eight thousand of the ninety nine thousand eight hundred and fifty nine people they reached came by calendar invite.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Which channels can I launch today?" > the reply lists LinkedIn, calendar invites, WhatsApp opted-in list, content and warm list as ready, and cold email as "waiting for warm-up, first send date" with the date]

Ask Funnel OS which channels can launch today. It checks your mailbox warm-up dates and tells you. If you ask it to start cold email early, it will say no and give you the date it can start. That's on purpose.

[SLIDE 3] Now the launch order. First, open your automation register and check there is no line that says Failing. If there is, stop and fix it first. Nothing launches while a line says Failing.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Show my automation register" > the register lists each automation with its status, none Failing]

Second, start your reminder and follow-up campaigns. These only reach people the funnel adds, so starting them sends nothing today. But they still need your approval, because they're real messages.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Start the reminder and follow-up campaigns" > the reply lists each campaign and asks for approval > type "Yes, start the reminder and follow-up campaigns" > each shows Started]

Third, the outreach. Tell Funnel OS which channels to start and how big the first slice is. For each channel, it splits off a few hundred contacts, checks every link points at your one registration page with its tags, and shows you the plan.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Launch LinkedIn and calendar invites to the first slice, 300 contacts each" > the plan shows each campaign, the slice size, the daily caps, the tagged link > type "Yes: start sending now" > Tantra app > Campaigns > both show Running]

Read the plan. Then type your go in your own words. Funnel OS starts exactly those campaigns, nothing else. Launch all your channels in the same window, so when you read the results, each channel is one clean row.

Your ads stay paused unless you've decided to switch them on yourself.

WhatsApp follows the same plan, but only for contacts who opted in. One link per message, and STOP in every message. If a template is still waiting for Meta to approve it, it simply waits. Don't work around it. If you do, use the smallest budget that gives a readable result.

[SLIDE 4] Now the first twenty four hours. Here's the hard part: don't read the results yet. Numbers read after a few hours kill things that were working. Today you're only looking for breakage. Three checks. Give it a full twenty four hours, not two. A day gives every channel time to show its first problems.

[SCREEN: Tantra app > Mailboxes > the bounce column per mailbox > one mailbox above 5% highlighted > click Pause]

One, bounces. In the Tantra app, look at bounces per mailbox for anything that's already sending. If a mailbox goes above five percent, pause it today. A bouncing inbox burns the domain you spent two weeks warming. Then check the list for that slice: bad addresses are almost always the cause.

Calendar invites don't report bounces the same way. For invites, check each sender shows healthy in the Tantra app and the first invites show as sent. And never build a second campaign because a counter reads zero in the first hours. The counters catch up.

[SCREEN: Tantra app > LinkedIn account > status Healthy > then LinkedIn on phone > Notifications > no restriction notices]

Two, LinkedIn warnings. Check your LinkedIn account in Tantra shows healthy, and look at LinkedIn itself for any notice about unusual activity. If you see one, pause the LinkedIn campaign, stay well inside the daily limits, and never run a second automation tool on the same profile.

[SCREEN: Tantra CRM > Contacts > filter created today > each new sign-up shows source, medium, campaign and content filled, blur names and emails > the pipeline shows them at Form Filled]

Three, sign-ups in the CRM with their source. Open the Tantra CRM, filter to today, and look at each new sign-up. Every one should show where it came from: source, medium, campaign and content. If sign-ups are arriving with blank sources, a link has lost its tags somewhere. Find it now, while it's a handful of people.

While you're there, click one link from each channel yourself and make sure it opens the page. And check WhatsApp for any STOP replies, and honour every one.

[SCREEN: WhatsApp group > post the 24 hour check: channels launched, slice sizes, bounces per mailbox, LinkedIn status, sign-ups with sources]

When the three checks are clean, post them in the members' WhatsApp group. Channels launched, slice sizes, bounces, LinkedIn status, sign-ups with sources. A coach replies the same day. Then send to the rest of your list.

[SLIDE 5] Now the routine you'll keep for as long as the funnel runs. Fifteen minutes, every day.

[SCREEN: Tantra app > Inbox > replies from email, LinkedIn and WhatsApp in one list, each tagged with its channel, blur names and message text > open one > reply with the booking link > Send]

Open the reply inbox in the Tantra app. Replies from email, LinkedIn and WhatsApp all land here. When a reply is detected, the contact is tagged replied in your CRM and counted by channel. Answer every real reply the same day, and put your booking link in the answer. A reply is the hottest signal in the whole funnel. Someone who wrote back today and hears from you tomorrow has already cooled.

Most replies fall into four kinds. Yes, I'm interested: send the booking link. A question: answer it in two or three lines, then the booking link. Not now: thank them, ask when would be better, and set a reminder in the CRM. And if someone asks you to stop, stop, and mark them so they never get another message.

[SCREEN: Google Calendar > create a recurring event "Replies, 15 min" daily at 09:00 > then a weekly event "Funnel review, 60 min" after the run ends]

Put it in your calendar as a recurring slot. Same time every day. And while you're there, add the weekly review, an hour, placed after your first run ends. That's where we'll read the numbers properly, starting in the next video.

[SLIDE 6] So here's where you should be. Every channel that's open today has launched to a small slice, all pointing at one registration page with tagged links. Cold email has its start date. Your first twenty four hours check is clean and posted in the group. And you have a daily fifteen minute reply slot and a weekly review in your calendar.

Do this now: launch your first slices and run the twenty four hour check tomorrow at the same time. Then watch the next video, Your Dashboard: Six Numbers That Matter.

Screen recording shot list

  1. Funnel OS chat: "Which channels can I launch today?" with cold email showing its warm-up date. About 25 seconds.
  2. Funnel OS chat: the automation register with no Failing line. About 15 seconds.
  3. Funnel OS chat: start the reminder and follow-up campaigns, approval typed, each Started. About 30 seconds.
  4. Funnel OS chat: launch plan for LinkedIn and calendar invites at 300 each, approval typed. Then Tantra app > Campaigns showing Running. Blur any contact names in the campaign view. About 45 seconds.
  5. Tantra app > Mailboxes bounce column; one example mailbox above 5%; Pause. Blur mailbox addresses. About 30 seconds.
  6. Tantra app > LinkedIn account status Healthy; LinkedIn notifications with no notice. Blur profile details. About 25 seconds.
  7. Tantra CRM > Contacts filtered to today with source fields; pipeline at Form Filled. Blur every name, email and phone number. About 40 seconds.
  8. WhatsApp group post of the 24 hour check. Blur other members' names and numbers. About 20 seconds.
  9. Tantra app > Inbox: open one reply and answer it with the booking link. Blur names and message text. About 40 seconds.
  10. Google Calendar: daily 15 minute reply slot and weekly 60 minute review. About 20 seconds.
