  • [ ] Every open channel launched to its smallest readable slice with your approval, every link tagged to one registration page, and cold email given its day 14 start date (1 point)
  • [ ] First 24 hours check posted in the group (bounces, LinkedIn warnings, sources in the CRM) and a daily 15 minute reply slot in your calendar (1 point)
scripts/V30-your-dashboard-six-numbers-that-matter.md

V30: Your Dashboard: Six Numbers That Matter

  • Module: M6 Optimise
  • Day: Week 2
  • Length: about 9 minutes (about 1,150 to 1,300 spoken words)
  • Systems on screen: Tantra CRM, Funnel OS
  • Before you start: your first run has launched and finished (V29), and sign-ups arrive in the CRM with their sources
  • Done when: your six rates for last week are on your dashboard by channel, with cost per sign-up and cost per buyer for each paid channel, and posted for the weekly call

Slides

Slide 1: Counts Hide Problems

  • big totals feel good
  • rates show where people drop
  • one row per week, per channel

Slide 2: Six Rates, One Per Stage

  • visit, sign-up, show-up
  • booking, close, rebooking
  • each one a count over a count

Slide 3: What Money Buys

  • cost per sign-up
  • cost per buyer
  • per paid channel

Slide 4: Not Yet, Never Zero

  • a missing count is unknown
  • zero means it happened and failed
  • write when you will measure it

Slide 5: Your Only Benchmark

  • your own last week
  • never another business
  • the Funnel Score on your Academy page

Voice script

[SLIDE 1] Here's a conversation I have on almost every first weekly call. A member says, we got two hundred sign-ups, it's going great. And I ask, how many showed up? And there's a pause. Then, how many booked a call? Longer pause.

Totals feel good, and they hide everything. In this video you'll set up the dashboard you'll read every week for the rest of the course: six rates, one for each stage of your funnel, by week and by channel, plus what each sign-up and each buyer cost you. And you'll learn the one rule that keeps the whole thing honest.

[SLIDE 2] You already know the six stages. Each one has one number. Every number is a count divided by a count.

Visit rate is page visits divided by people reached. It tells you whether your outreach and your posts get people to the page.

Sign-up rate is sign-ups divided by page visits. It tells you whether your page earns the sign-up.

Show-up rate is attendees divided by sign-ups. It tells you whether your reminders work.

Booking rate is calls booked divided by attendees. It tells you whether your offer lands.

Close rate is customers divided by calls booked. It tells you whether your sales call works.

And rebooking rate is calls booked from your long-term nurture, divided by the people on that list. It tells you whether the people who didn't buy this time are still warm.

Customers equal sign-ups times show-up rate times booking rate times close rate. Which means a small lift in any one of them lifts the whole line.

These are the same counts any real funnel produces. When Shweta's coaching group, Growth Idea, ran eleven webinars, the numbers that mattered were six thousand five hundred and seventeen registered, one thousand three hundred and eighty four live, eighty sales calls and four clients. Every one of those is a count you'll see on your own dashboard.

[SCREEN: Tantra CRM > Dashboards > Acquisition > sign-ups by week, split by source: LinkedIn, calendar invites, WhatsApp, content, warm list > page visits from the tracking snippet]

Now let's look at where these live. In the Tantra CRM, open Dashboards, then Acquisition. This is your top of funnel: page visits from the tracking snippet, and sign-ups by week, split by source. Because every link carries its tags, each channel is its own row.

[SCREEN: Tantra CRM > Dashboards > Revenue > the pipeline by stage: Call Scheduled, Call Held, Proposal Sent, Payment Completed > payments by week]

Then Revenue. Calls booked, calls held, proposals, and payments, by week. Payments only count when the signed webhook arrives from your payment provider, never from someone landing on a thank-you page.

One check before you trust any of this. Open three sign-ups from last week at random and look at their source fields. If any are blank, a link lost its tags somewhere, and that sign-up sits in an unknown row. Fix the link, then note it in the review. A dashboard is only as good as its tags.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Run my weekly review for last week" > the reply builds a table: one row per channel, columns people reached, visits, sign-ups, attendees, calls booked, customers, then the six rates with numerator and denominator]

Now the part that saves you an hour a week. In Funnel OS, type: run my weekly review for last week. It pulls the counts from the Tantra app, the CRM and the Content OS through their connections and builds the table. One row per channel. People reached, visits, sign-ups, attendees, calls booked, customers. Then the six rates, each written with its top and bottom number, so you can check the maths yourself.

Notice it writes "sign-up rate, fifty four over a hundred and twenty". Not just a percentage. A percentage without its counts can't tell you whether it's based on three people or three thousand.

[SLIDE 3] Next, money. For every channel you pay for, two more numbers. Cost per sign-up is spend divided by sign-ups. Cost per buyer is spend divided by buyers.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Meta spend last week was 210 dollars, LinkedIn 340 dollars" > the review adds cost per sign-up and cost per buyer for each, and shows cost per buyer for LinkedIn as "not yet, no buyers from this channel"]

Funnel OS asks you for your ad spend if it can't read it, and adds both numbers to each paid row. Compare cost per sign-up with the target you set in the ads video. Cost per buyer is the number that matters most in the end, and it takes the longest to fill in, because buyers come weeks after sign-ups.

Outreach costs money too: your mailboxes, your sender seats, your Tantra plan. Add a monthly figure for each channel if you want a fair comparison. Funnel OS will split it by week.

One thing to know about cost per buyer. Buyers often touch more than one channel. Someone sees your post, gets a calendar invite, and then signs up from a LinkedIn message. The CRM credits the sign-up to the link they actually clicked. That's fine. It's consistent, and consistency is what lets you compare one week with the next.

[SLIDE 4] Now the rule that keeps this honest. Look at this cell.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS weekly review > the close rate cell reads "not yet: 6 calls booked, none held yet" > the rebooking cell reads "not yet: nurture starts week 3"]

Close rate says not yet. Not zero. Not yet. Zero would mean calls happened and nobody bought, which is a real problem. Not yet means the calls haven't happened, so we don't know. Those two lead to completely different decisions, and mixing them up is how people kill a funnel that was working.

So every count you can't read yet stays as not yet, with when you'll measure it. Never zero. And never an estimate. If a channel's numbers aren't connected, the review says not connected and what to do about it.

Early on you'll have a lot of not yets. Close rate needs calls held. Rebooking rate needs a few weeks of nurture. That's fine. Fill in what's real.

[SLIDE 5] Last, what to compare against. Your only benchmark is your own last week.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS weekly review > a column "last week" beside each rate and a column "change" > the Funnel Score panel shows the six rates against last week]

From your second week on, the review puts last week beside this week for every rate, with the change. That's your Funnel Score: the six rates against your own previous week. It shows on your Academy page and in your CRM, so you, your coach and the weekly report all read the same numbers.

Read it like this. A rate that went up: leave it alone, it's working. A rate that went down: that's a candidate for your worst step, which is the next video. And always read it by channel, not just in total. Without the split, a strong channel hides a weak one in the average.

You'll hear me use teaching model rates in this course. For example, in our teaching model, show-up is just under thirty percent. Those are there to teach the maths. They are not a target and not a result. Another business's numbers tell you almost nothing about yours. Your last week tells you everything.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Save this review and post the summary for my weekly call" > the reply shows a short summary: the six rates with counts, cost per sign-up and per buyer per paid channel, the not yets > copy > WhatsApp group > paste]

When the review is done, ask Funnel OS to save it and write the summary for your weekly call. Paste it in the group. That summary is what we open the call with. Once it's set up, the whole review takes about ten minutes of your time, and it's the first thing we look at every week.

So here's where you should be. Your dashboards are open in the CRM. Funnel OS has run your first weekly review: six rates by channel, each with its counts, cost per sign-up and per buyer for each paid channel, and every missing number marked not yet.

Do this now: run your weekly review and post the summary. Then watch the next video, Find the Worst Step and Fix Only That.

Screen recording shot list

  1. Tantra CRM > Dashboards > Acquisition: visits and sign-ups by week and source. Blur any contact names in drill downs. About 30 seconds.
  2. Tantra CRM > Dashboards > Revenue: pipeline by stage and payments by week. Blur deal names and amounts tied to real contacts. About 30 seconds.
  3. Funnel OS chat: "Run my weekly review for last week", the table building with counts and rates by channel. Use a demo account with demo numbers. About 60 seconds.
  4. Close-up of one rate written as numerator over denominator. About 10 seconds.
  5. Funnel OS chat: typing ad spend; cost per sign-up and per buyer added; one "not yet". About 30 seconds.
  6. The review's not yet cells for close rate and rebooking rate. About 20 seconds.
  7. Week two view: last week and change columns, the Funnel Score panel. About 30 seconds.
  8. Funnel OS chat: save and summarise; paste the summary into the WhatsApp group. Blur other members' names and numbers. About 30 seconds.

Funnel Score checklist

  • [ ] Last week's six rates by channel, each written as a count over a count
  • [ ] Cost per sign-up and cost per buyer for each paid channel
  • [ ] Every missing number marked not yet, with when you will measure it
  • [ ] The weekly review summary posted in the group before the call
scripts/V31-find-the-worst-step-and-fix-only-that.md

V31: Find the Worst Step and Fix Only That

  • Module: M6 Optimise
  • Day: Week 2
  • Length: about 10 minutes (about 1,300 to 1,450 spoken words)
  • Systems on screen: Funnel OS
  • Before you start: your weekly review has run, with six rates by channel (V30)
  • Done when: one worst step is named from your own numbers, saved to your CRM, and one change for it is chosen

Slides

Slide 1: Five Changes Teach You Nothing

  • everything moved, nothing learned
  • one step, one change
  • the loop compounds

Slide 2: Name the Worst Step

  • six rates, each against last week
  • the lowest ratio wins
  • a tie goes to the earlier step

Slide 3: Rule Out the Inbox First

  • bounces over 5%: fix delivery
  • click through under 1%: fix the message
  • starting points, not benchmarks

Slide 4: Each Step Has One Fix

  • few visits: the message or the list
  • few sign-ups: the headline
  • low show-up: one reminder
  • few offer clicks: the offer slide

Slide 5: A Worked Example

  • made-up round numbers
  • two steps tie
  • the earlier one wins

Slide 6: Done When

  • one step named
  • saved to your CRM
  • one change chosen

Voice script

[SLIDE 1] It's Monday. The numbers are in, and they're not what you hoped. So you rewrite the headline, change the reminder times, swap the offer slide and send a new email sequence. Next week some numbers are up and some are down, and you have no idea which change did what.

I've done this myself, more than once. In this video you'll learn the habit that replaced it: find the single worst step in your funnel, using only your own numbers, and change one thing there. One step, one change, every week. It sounds slow. It's the fastest thing I know.

[SLIDE 2] Here's the method. Take your six rates from the weekly review. For each one, divide this week's rate by your own last week's rate. That gives you a ratio. A ratio of one means no change. Above one, it got better. Below one, it got worse.

The step with the lowest ratio is your worst step. If two steps tie, take the earlier one, because it feeds everything after it. A leak at the top starves the steps below it, so fixing it first helps the most.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Find my worst step" > the reply lists the six rates with this week, last week and the ratio for each, marks the lowest, and names one step]

You don't do the maths by hand. In Funnel OS, type: find my worst step. It takes this week's review, puts last week beside it, works out each ratio, and names one step. Not three. One.

In your very first week you don't have a last week yet. Funnel OS then compares against the teaching model and says so clearly in the reply. Treat that first answer as a rough guide. From week two, it's your numbers against your numbers.

A ratio also catches a step that used to be strong and slipped. That's often the easiest kind to fix, because something changed, and you can usually find what.

Two more rules. Compare rates, not totals. A big count can hide the lowest rate. And never name a worst step on a run that hasn't finished. Halfway through a run, the late steps always look terrible, because the calls haven't happened yet. That's why close rate says not yet, and not yet is never the worst step.

[SLIDE 3] Before you rewrite any copy, rule out the inbox. If your visit rate dropped on cold email, it might be a weak message. Or your emails might be landing in spam. Those two look exactly the same on the dashboard, and they need completely different fixes.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Run the channel check" > the reply shows each channel with bounces, click through and conversion, and one verdict per channel: fix, hold or scale]

So run the channel check. Funnel OS reads three numbers per channel and gives one verdict each. If bounces are over five percent, that's a delivery problem: pause that mailbox today and check its list. If click-through is under one percent and delivery looks healthy, the message is the problem, not the inbox. If click-through is at least three percent, conversion at least one and a half percent and bounces at most two percent, that channel is ready to scale: add mailboxes or senders, not new ideas. Anything in between, hold and change one thing.

Those thresholds are starting points, not benchmarks. After a few weeks, replace them with your own.

Here's why this order matters. If your mailbox is landing in spam and you rewrite the email, the new email lands in spam too, and you'll decide the new copy is worse. You've just thrown away a good change because of a delivery problem. Check delivery first, every time.

[SLIDE 4] Now, once you've named the step, each one has one usual fix.

If messages go out but few people visit, change the message or the list. If people visit but few sign up, change the headline on your page. If people sign up but few show up, change one reminder or its timing. If people attend but few click the offer, change the offer slide and the call to action. If people click but few book or buy, change the booking page or the follow-up. And if one channel costs far more per buyer than the others, the funnel is fine, that channel isn't: move budget toward the cheapest one.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS reply > under the named step: "Usual fix: the landing headline" > three suggested experiments for that step, each changing one thing, ranked by expected effect and effort]

Funnel OS shows the usual fix under the step it named, then suggests three experiments for that step, each changing one thing, ranked by how much they might help and how much work they are. Pick one.

Here's a mistake I see all the time. Show-up is fine, but nobody clicks the offer, and the member rewrites the reminders. Don't. If people turned up and didn't click, the reminders did their job. Fix the step that's actually weak.

Inside each step there's usually one input that's furthest behind. For show-up, it might be one reminder in particular: the one hour reminder is fine, but the twenty four hour reminder lands at the wrong time. Funnel OS looks at the inputs inside the step and tells you which one to raise first. That's how you get from show-up is low to move the twenty four hour reminder to eight in the morning, which is something you can actually do today.

[SLIDE 5] Let me walk you through an example. These are made-up round numbers, just to show the method.

Last week: visit rate two percent, sign-up rate forty percent, show-up rate thirty percent, booking rate eight percent.

This week: visit rate two point two percent, sign-up rate thirty percent, show-up rate thirty three percent, booking rate six percent. Close rate not yet.

Now the ratios. Visit rate, two point two over two, is one point one. Better. Sign-up rate, thirty over forty, is zero point seven five. Worse. Show-up, thirty three over thirty, is one point one. Better. Booking rate, six over eight, is zero point seven five. Also worse.

So sign-up and booking tie at zero point seven five. The rule says the earlier step wins. Sign-up comes first. So the worst step is sign-up, and the usual fix is the headline on your page. Booking waits its turn. Fix sign-up, and you also send more people into the booking step, which makes next week's booking number easier to read.

Notice what we didn't do. We didn't touch the reminders, even though show-up is the step everyone loves to fiddle with. It went up. Leave it alone.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > paste the example numbers > the reply shows the two ratios of 0.75, names sign-up rate as the worst step "because it comes earlier", and suggests the landing headline]

[SLIDE 6] Once you've picked your one change, you'll test it properly. That means splitting your next run fifty fifty. Half the list sees the old version, half sees the new one, each with its own content tag, so the dashboard counts them as two rows.

Then you decide only when the gap is big enough. Here's the rule in one line: the winner has to beat the other half by more than twice the square root of both counts added together. Another made-up example: version A gets forty sign-ups and version B gets fifty two. The gap is twelve. Forty plus fifty two is ninety two. The square root of ninety two is about nine point six, doubled is about nineteen. Twelve is less than nineteen, so it isn't decided yet. Run it again. We'll go through this properly in the next video.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Save sign-up rate as my worst step and the headline test as this week's change" > the reply confirms it updated the worst step field on your CRM contact and the Funnel Score]

Last step. Tell Funnel OS to save the worst step and your chosen change. It writes the worst step to your CRM, which also shows on your Academy page, so your coach sees the same thing you do before the weekly call.

Bring three things to the weekly call: your six rates against last week, the worst step with its ratio, and the one change you picked. That's the agenda. We start with the lowest number in the room.

So here's where you should be. You've run the channel check. You've named one worst step from your own numbers. And you've chosen one change for it, and only it.

Do this now: find your worst step, save it, and bring it to the weekly call. Then watch the next video, Daily Changes, Weekly Experiments.

Screen recording shot list

  1. Funnel OS chat: "Find my worst step", the table of six rates with this week, last week and ratios, one step named. Demo numbers only. About 45 seconds.
  2. Funnel OS chat: a first week answer that says it compared against the teaching model. About 15 seconds.
  3. Funnel OS chat: "Run the channel check", verdicts per channel. Blur mailbox addresses if they appear. About 45 seconds.
  4. Funnel OS reply: usual fix and three ranked experiments for the named step. About 30 seconds.
  5. Funnel OS chat: paste the made-up example numbers, the reply showing the 0.75 tie and sign-up named. About 40 seconds.
  6. Funnel OS chat: save the worst step and chosen change; confirmation that the CRM field and Funnel Score updated. About 20 seconds.
  7. Tantra CRM: your own contact record showing the worst step field. Blur your email and phone number. About 15 seconds.

Funnel Score checklist

  • [ ] The channel check for each channel with its verdict: fix, hold or scale
  • [ ] This week's six rates against last week, with the ratio for each
  • [ ] One worst step named, saved to your CRM, with the reason in one line
  • [ ] The one change you will test on that step
scripts/V32-daily-changes-weekly-experiments.md

V32: Daily Changes, Weekly Experiments

  • Module: M6 Optimise
  • Day: Week 2
  • Length: about 10 minutes (about 1,300 to 1,450 spoken words)
  • Systems on screen: Funnel OS, Tantra CRM, Tantra app
  • Before you start: your worst step is named and one change for it is chosen (V31)
  • Done when: this week's experiment is running as a 50/50 split with its own tags, and the experiment log has its first line

Slides

Slide 1: Two Speeds of Change

  • daily: small fixes, logged
  • weekly: one test, on the worst step
  • never on the same step at once

Slide 2: Daily Small Changes

  • copy that confused a reader
  • a subject line on another step
  • a reminder sent at the wrong hour

Slide 3: Name the Test Before It Runs

  • one element
  • one deciding number
  • when you will read it

Slide 4: Split 50/50

  • a few hundred people per half
  • its own content tag per half
  • nothing touched until it ends

Slide 5: The Decision Gap Rule

  • gap between A and B
  • must beat 2 x the square root of A + B
  • smaller: run it again

Slide 6: Keep, Revert, Log

  • winner into your Funnel Brief
  • loser reverted
  • one line in the log

Voice script

[SLIDE 1] Here's the trap after the worst step video. You've found your one step, you've picked your one change, and then on Tuesday a reply comes in saying your reminder email arrived at four in the morning. Do you fix it? Or do you leave it alone because you're meant to change only one thing a week?

You fix it. In this video I'll show you the two speeds of change in this course, and how to keep them apart. Small, obvious fixes happen daily, and they're logged. One real experiment happens weekly, on your worst step, split fifty fifty and judged by a rule that stops you fooling yourself.

[SLIDE 2] Start with daily changes. These are small fixes you'd make without a second thought. A line of copy that confused someone who replied. A subject line on a step you're not testing this week. A reminder going out at the wrong hour for your buyer's time zone. A broken link.

The rule is simple: daily changes never touch the step you're testing this week. If your test is on the landing headline, you don't tweak the headline on Wednesday because it feels weak. You'd spoil the test.

How do you tell a daily change from a weekly one? Ask one question: would I be surprised if this moved a number? Fixing a typo or a wrong send time, no surprise, that's daily. A new headline, a new promise, a new offer slide, you're hoping it moves a number, so it's a test, and it's weekly.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "The 24 hour reminder is arriving at 4am for UK contacts. Move it to 08:00 their time." > the reply shows the change, notes it is not on this week's test step, and asks for approval > type "Yes, change it" > the reply confirms and adds a line to the experiment log marked "daily change"]

In Funnel OS, describe the fix. It shows you what it will change, checks it isn't on this week's test step, and asks you to approve. Once you do, it makes the change and logs it as a daily change, with the date. That log matters. When a number moves next week, you'll want to know what else changed.

And after any change to a page or a message, rerun your two test contacts through that step. It takes five minutes, and it keeps your automation register honest.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Rerun the test contacts through the reminders" > the register shows the reminder line as Live with today's date]

[SLIDE 3] Now the weekly experiment. Before anything runs, you name three things. The one element you're changing. The one number that decides it. And when you'll read it: when the run finishes, or after a set number of sends. Never after a few hours.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Set up this week's experiment: the landing headline, sign-up rate decides it, read when the run ends" > the reply restates the element, the deciding number, the reading point, and the minimum size per half]

Here's an example. The landing headline. Sign-up rate decides it. Read it when the run ends. Type that into Funnel OS and it restates it back, along with how big each half needs to be.

[SLIDE 4] How big? At minimum, a few hundred people per half at the top of the deciding number. For a headline test, that's a few hundred page visits per half. For a subject line test, a few hundred people reached per half. The smaller the difference you're hoping for, the more people you need. So if your list is small, test a bold change, not a tiny tweak. A new promise in the headline, not a new comma. And if a week can't give you a few hundred per half, run the same test across two weeks and add the halves together, as long as nothing else on that step changed.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Split it" > the reply shows version A and version B of the headline, the page set to show each to half the visitors, the tags content headline-a and headline-b, and asks for approval > type "Yes, start the headline test" > Tantra CRM > Contacts > filter by content tag headline-a, then headline-b]

Then split it. Half the people get version A, half get version B, and each half has its own content tag. For a page test, Funnel OS shows each version of the page to half the visitors. For a message test, it splits your list into two halves and builds one Tantra campaign for each. Either way, your dashboard counts them as two separate rows, all the way down to the sale.

The halves must differ in one way only. Funnel OS splits the list at random, not alphabetically and not by date added. If you split by surname, or send A on Monday and B on Thursday, the halves differ in more than one way and the result means nothing. Same day, same time, same channel, random halves.

And then you leave it alone. Never run two experiments on the same step at once. And never end one early because one half looks ahead after a day. Early leads disappear all the time.

Day one only shows you who acts fast. Wait for the reading point you named.

[SLIDE 5] Now the rule for deciding. When the run ends, take the count for A and the count for B. The gap between them has to be bigger than twice the square root of A plus B. If it isn't, the difference could just be a handful of people, and you run the same test again before you call it.

Let me show you with made-up round numbers. This is an example, not data.

Each half had five hundred page visits. Version A got sixty sign-ups. Version B got seventy five. B looks better. The gap is fifteen.

Now the rule. Sixty plus seventy five is a hundred and thirty five. The square root of a hundred and thirty five is about eleven point six. Twice that is about twenty three. Is the gap of fifteen bigger than twenty three? No. So this one is not decided. You run the same test again.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Close the experiment: A 60 sign-ups from 500 visits, B 75 from 500" > the reply shows gap 15, two times the square root of 135 is about 23, verdict "not decided, run again"]

Now say the next run, same split, gives A sixty and B a hundred. The gap is forty. Sixty plus a hundred is a hundred and sixty. The square root is about twelve point six, twice that is about twenty five. Forty is bigger than twenty five. Now it's decided. B wins.

And if a test keeps coming back not decided? That's an answer too. It means the change made little difference. Keep whichever version is simpler, log it as no clear winner, and next week test something bolder.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Rerun result: A 60, B 100, from 500 visits each" > the reply shows gap 40, threshold about 25, verdict "decided: B wins" and asks whether to keep B]

This is a rule of thumb, not a formal statistics test. But it's simple enough to do on a napkin, and it stops the most common mistake I see, which is declaring a winner off a handful of people.

[SLIDE 6] When a test is decided, keep the winner and revert the loser. The winner goes into your Funnel Brief, so every page and message written from it uses the new version. Funnel OS asks for your approval, updates the brief, redeploys the page, and reverts the old version everywhere.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Keep B" > the reply shows the Funnel Brief change and the page redeploy, asks for approval > type "Yes, keep B and redeploy" > Funnel OS > Experiment log > the new line: date, stage, element, change, expected number, result, decision]

Then the log. One line per experiment: the date, the stage, the element, the change, the number you expected to move, the result, and the decision. Funnel OS writes it, and your coach reads it on your fifteen minute slot on the weekly call.

The log also stops you repeating yourself. Three months from now you won't remember that you tested a question headline in week two. The log will. Before you open a new experiment, Funnel OS checks the log and tells you if you've tested something close to it already.

If you're not sure what to test first at a step, here's where I'd start. For traffic, the subject line or the opening line, one channel at a time. For sign-ups, the headline. For show-up, one reminder or its timing. For bookings, the offer slide and the call to action. For calls held, the timing of the reminders before calls.

Expect your worst step to move once you fix it. Next week a different step will be the worst. That isn't failure. That's the loop working.

So here's the rhythm. Daily: answer replies, refresh the numbers, make small logged fixes. Weekly: the channel check, close last week's experiment, open this week's. Monthly: the scale plan, which is in the last video. Quarterly: look at your offer and your buyer again. If that sounds like a lot, it isn't. The daily part is fifteen minutes. The weekly part is about an hour, and Funnel OS does most of the maths. What it can't do is decide for you. That part is yours.

Do this now: start this week's experiment as a fifty fifty split and check the first line of your log. Then watch the next video, Sales Calls, Proposals and Getting Paid.

Screen recording shot list

  1. Funnel OS chat: the daily reminder timing fix, approval, logged as a daily change. About 40 seconds.
  2. Funnel OS chat: rerun the test contacts; register line Live with today's date. About 20 seconds.
  3. Funnel OS chat: set up the headline experiment, the restated test and minimum size. About 30 seconds.
  4. Funnel OS chat: split it, approval typed. Tantra CRM > Contacts filtered by headline-a, then headline-b. Blur every name and email. About 45 seconds.
  5. Funnel OS chat: close the experiment with 60 and 75, verdict not decided. Demo numbers only. About 30 seconds.
  6. Funnel OS chat: rerun result 60 and 100, verdict decided. About 25 seconds.
  7. Funnel OS chat: keep B, brief change and redeploy approval. Then the experiment log with its new line. About 40 seconds.

Funnel Score checklist

  • [ ] Last week's experiment closed: both counts, the gap, twice the square root of A plus B, and the verdict
  • [ ] This week's experiment named: element, deciding number, reading point, size per half
  • [ ] The experiment log updated, with every daily change of the week listed
  • [ ] The automation register still reads Live on every line you changed
scripts/V33-sales-calls-proposals-and-getting-paid.md

V33: Sales Calls, Proposals and Getting Paid

  • Module: M6 Optimise
  • Day: Week 3
  • Length: about 11 minutes (about 1,400 to 1,550 spoken words)
  • Systems on screen: Tantra CRM, Funnel OS, Dodo Payments, Razorpay, Stripe
  • Before you start: your booking page, sales automations and live payment connection are working (V14, V15, V20), and calls are being booked
  • Done when: every call held this week has a stage, every proposal has a payment link and a follow-up date, every lost deal has a reason, and you know when your first payout reaches your bank

Slides

Slide 1: A Booked Call Is Not Revenue

  • money in your bank is
  • five places deals leak
  • every step on a clock

Slide 2: The Call in Three Parts

  • discovery, in their words
  • walkthrough of your method
  • the close: one question

Slide 3: The Proposal in Ten Minutes

  • their goal, your method applied
  • included and not included
  • price, guarantee, expiry date

Slide 4: The Payment Link and the Chase

  • link created from the deal
  • reminders before and after the due date
  • the webhook stops every chase

Slide 5: Every Lost Deal Gets a Reason

  • price, timing, fit, no decision
  • a door-open email
  • a re-engage date

Slide 6: From Payment to Bank

  • payouts differ by provider
  • check yours
  • match payments to payouts weekly

Voice script

[SLIDE 1] You've had this call. It goes well. They nod, they ask good questions, and at the end they say, this sounds great, send me something. You send something. And then nothing. A week later you're not sure whether to chase or wait.

A booked call is not revenue. A proposal is not revenue. Money in your bank account is revenue. In this video we'll run the call, write the proposal in ten minutes, send a payment link straight from the CRM, chase the payment without being awkward, record why every lost deal was lost, and follow the money all the way to your bank.

Between the offer and the money, there are five places people quietly fall out. They don't book, they don't show, they don't decide, they don't pay, or they pay and never start. Your automations already handle the first two. This video is about the other three.

[SCREEN: Tantra CRM > Pipeline > a deal at Call Scheduled > open it > the booking answers show under the deal, blur name, email and phone]

[SLIDE 2] Start before the call. Open the deal in the Tantra CRM. The answers to your booking questions sit right here: their role, where they are today, what they've tried, and when they need it fixed. Read them for two minutes before you join. That's your call prep done.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Write my call scripts: discovery, walkthrough, close and two battlecards" > the reply shows the four parts written from the Funnel Brief]

If you haven't written your scripts yet, ask Funnel OS. It writes four parts from your Funnel Brief. Let me walk you through them.

Discovery first. Open with honesty. I say something like: if this isn't a fit, I'll tell you, and I'll point you somewhere better. Then the questions. Where are you now, compared with where you want to be? What have you tried, and what happened? Who else decides? What date matters? Then, before you describe anything you sell, summarise what you heard in their words. If they say, yes, that's it, you've earned the next part.

Then the walkthrough. One line per step of your method: what you do, and what changes for them. Then one proof line from your proof list, and what was different about that case.

Keep the walkthrough short. Most founders talk too much here. They've waited all call to explain the thing, and they explain all of it. Your buyer only needs to see how each step fixes the problem they just told you about. If a step of your method doesn't touch their problem, skip it.

Then the close. Say whether it's a fit. State the offer, the price and the guarantee, exactly as they are in your Funnel Brief. Then ask one question and stop talking. If they hesitate, ask: what would need to be true for this to be an easy yes? Then answer only that.

Funnel OS also writes two battlecards for the two objections you hear most. The objection word for word, your answer, one proof line, and one question back. Keep them open on a second screen.

[SCREEN: Tantra CRM > the deal > move to Call Held > set Qualified > the recap task appears due today]

After the call, move the deal to Call Held and mark it Qualified or Unqualified. Then send a recap the same day with the next step in it. Unqualified isn't a loss. If they said not now, set a re-engage date and they go into your long-term nurture.

The recap is three lines. What you heard, what you agreed, and the next step with a date. Send it from the deal so it's logged. If more than one person has to say yes, write it so they can forward it. Their boss wasn't on the call, so the recap does the selling.

[SLIDE 3] Now the proposal. Your price decides the path. Under roughly a thousand dollars, people buy from a checkout and you rarely need a call. Above that, they usually book a call first, and many need a proposal.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Write a proposal for the deal I just held" > the reply pulls the booking answers and call notes from the CRM deal > shows: their goal in their words, current situation, the method applied to them, included, not included, timeline, investment, guarantee, next steps with an expiry date]

In Funnel OS, ask for a proposal for the deal you just held. It reads the booking answers and your call notes from the deal, and writes it from your Funnel Brief. Their goal in their own words. Where they are now. Your method's steps applied to them. What's included, and what isn't. That not included list stops scope arguments later. The timeline. The investment. Your guarantee. And next steps with an expiry date.

Read it, edit what you want, and send it. That's ten minutes, not an afternoon.

And if you can sell in one call, skip the proposal. Send the payment link straight after the call while the decision is fresh. A proposal is for buyers who need to show someone else, not a formality.

[SCREEN: Tantra CRM > the deal > move to Proposal Sent > the follow-up sequence shows steps at 48 hours and 7 days]

Then move the deal to Proposal Sent. That's what starts the follow-ups. At forty eight hours, a short note: is anything unclear, and would a fifteen minute walkthrough help? At seven days, the offer of that walkthrough again. If someone replies, books or accepts, the follow-ups stop on their own. Remember, tools report events and events move deals. If you moved a deal by hand, that step isn't wired.

[SLIDE 4] When they say yes, send the payment link from the CRM.

[SCREEN: Tantra CRM > the deal > Payments > Create payment link > your connected provider > the amount and currency from the proposal > due date > Create > copy the link > the deal moves to Proposal Accepted]

Open the deal, go to Payments, and create a payment link through the provider you connected: Dodo Payments, Razorpay or Stripe. The amount and currency come from the proposal. Set a due date. Copy the link and send it with a short thank you.

Now the chase, which most founders hate doing. So don't do it by hand. The CRM sends the reminders for you: three days before the due date, on the due date, then three days and seven days late. Each one is polite and short, with the link.

[SCREEN: Tantra CRM > the deal > Sequence tab showing the invoice reminders: 3 days before, due date, 3 days late, 7 days late > then the payment provider's test webhook arrives > the deal moves to Payment Completed, then Onboarding, and the reminders show Stopped]

The moment the payment provider's signed webhook arrives, three things happen. The deal moves to Payment Completed. Every reminder stops, so a buyer never gets chased after paying. And your welcome email goes out, and the deal moves to Onboarding. Book the kickoff within three to seven days. The first forty eight hours after payment decide whether a customer stays, so make the first win happen fast.

Never mark a deal paid by hand, and never because someone landed on your thank-you page. Only the webhook counts.

[SLIDE 5] Now the deals that don't close. Every lost deal gets a reason. No exceptions.

[SCREEN: Tantra CRM > a deal > Mark as lost > the reason list: price, timing, fit, chose another option, no decision, went quiet > pick timing > set a re-engage date three months out > the door-open email is queued > the contact gets the long-term nurture tag]

Mark it lost and pick the reason: price, timing, fit, chose another option, no decision, or went quiet. Set a re-engage date. The CRM sends a short door-open email, something like: no problem at all, if things change, here's where to find me. And the contact moves into your long-term nurture.

Once a month, look at your lost reasons together. If price comes up again and again, that's your offer or your proof, not your sales call. If an objection comes up twice, ask Funnel OS to add it to your battlecards. Lost reasons are one of the most useful things you'll collect.

Also watch how long a deal sits in each stage. A proposal that's been sitting for three weeks with no reply isn't pending. It's lost, it just hasn't been marked yet. Extend it once with a new expiry, or close it as lost with its reason. A clean pipeline tells you the truth. A full one just makes you feel busy.

[SLIDE 6] Last, the money arriving in your bank. A payment in your CRM is not the same as money in your account. Each provider pays out on its own schedule, and it differs by provider, by country and by account. So check yours.

[SCREEN: Dodo Payments dashboard > Payouts > the payout schedule and the next payout date, blur bank details > Razorpay dashboard > Settlements > settlement schedule, blur account numbers > Stripe dashboard > Balance > Payouts > payout schedule, blur bank details]

In Dodo Payments, open Payouts. Dodo is the merchant of record, so it collects the tax and pays out to you. In Razorpay, it's Settlements. In Stripe, Balance, then Payouts. Each shows your schedule and your next payout date. Write down when your first payout reaches your bank, so you're not refreshing your banking app every morning.

Then once a week, match the payments in your CRM against the payouts that arrived. Refunds and fees come out before the payout, so the numbers won't be identical, but every payment should be accounted for.

If a buyer asks for a refund, do it from the provider's dashboard. The webhook tells the CRM, the deal moves back to Follow-Up and the purchased tag comes off, so your close rate stays honest.

So here's where you should be. Every call held this week has a stage and a recap. Every proposal has a payment link and its follow-ups. Every lost deal has a reason and a re-engage date. And you know the date your first payout reaches your bank.

Do this now: go through your pipeline and fix any deal without a stage, a next step or a reason. Then watch the last video, Keep Every Lead Warm for a Year, and the Scale Plan.

Screen recording shot list

  1. Tantra CRM > Pipeline > a demo deal at Call Scheduled with booking answers. Blur name, email and phone. About 25 seconds.
  2. Funnel OS chat: call scripts and two battlecards written from the Funnel Brief. About 40 seconds.
  3. Tantra CRM: move the deal to Call Held, set Qualified, recap task due today. About 20 seconds.
  4. Funnel OS chat: proposal for the held deal, scroll through every section. Demo business only. About 45 seconds.
  5. Tantra CRM: move to Proposal Sent, follow-up steps at 48 hours and 7 days. About 20 seconds.
  6. Tantra CRM: Payments > Create payment link through the connected provider in test mode, amount, due date, copy. Blur any keys, emails and the full link. About 40 seconds.
  7. Tantra CRM: invoice reminder steps; test webhook arrives; deal moves to Payment Completed then Onboarding; reminders Stopped. About 40 seconds.
  8. Tantra CRM: Mark as lost with reason, re-engage date, door-open email queued, nurture tag. Blur contact details. About 30 seconds.
  9. Dodo Payments Payouts, Razorpay Settlements, Stripe Payouts pages. Blur every bank detail, account number and balance. About 45 seconds.

Funnel Score checklist

  • [ ] Calls held and customers this week, so your close rate has its counts
  • [ ] Every open proposal with its payment link and next follow-up date
  • [ ] Payments confirmed by webhook this week, and the payouts that reached your bank
  • [ ] Every lost deal this week with its reason and re-engage date
scripts/V34-keep-every-lead-warm-and-the-scale-plan.md

V34: Keep Every Lead Warm for a Year, and the Scale Plan

  • Module: M6 Optimise
  • Day: Week 4
  • Length: about 11 minutes (about 1,400 to 1,550 spoken words)
  • Systems on screen: Tantra CRM, Content OS, Funnel OS
  • Before you start: your first run has finished, lost deals carry reasons (V33), and your weekly review runs (V30)
  • Done when: everyone who did not buy is on the long-term nurture tag, the welcome lessons and weekly letter are live, your next event invite is set, and your five line scale plan is posted

Slides

Slide 1: Nobody Leaves the Funnel

  • most people will not buy this time
  • they only change speed
  • one tag carries them for a year

Slide 2: The Welcome Lessons

  • four weekly emails
  • one step of your method each
  • one link at the end

Slide 3: The Weekly Letter and Its Twin

  • same day, same time, every week
  • WhatsApp version an hour later
  • under 400 characters, opted-in only

Slide 4: Reactivate, Then Re-Invite

  • nine words after 90 quiet days
  • remove after 20 unopened emails
  • every new event to every non-buyer

Slide 5: The Five Line Scale Plan

  • cost per buyer against lifetime value
  • the cheapest channel
  • worst step, next offer up
  • the first-win date for buyers

Slide 6: The Next 30 Days

  • replies daily, experiment weekly
  • group coaching every week
  • scale plan every month

Voice script

[SLIDE 1] Here's the uncomfortable truth about your first event. Most of the people who signed up won't buy this time. Some weren't ready. Some had the wrong timing. Some just missed it. And if they hear nothing from you for six months, your warm list is a cold list again, and you're paying to reach them all over again.

In this last video we fix that, and then we plan the next month. You'll put everyone who didn't buy on one tag that keeps them warm for a year. You'll set up the welcome lessons, the weekly letter and its WhatsApp twin, a reactivation email, and the invite to your next event. Then you'll write your five line scale plan. And I'll show you what the next thirty days look like.

[SCREEN: Tantra CRM > Contacts > filter: Registered, not Payment Completed > select all > add tag Long-term nurture > then the automation rule: replied removes from nurture and creates a deal; Payment Completed removes the tag]

Nobody leaves the funnel. They only change speed. In the Tantra CRM, everyone who finished your event without buying, every lost deal marked not now, and everyone who went quiet gets one tag: long-term nurture. Two rules sit on that tag. Anyone who replies goes straight back into your pipeline as a new deal. And anyone who buys comes off the tag.

Why a tag and not a list? Because a tag follows the person. When they reply, book or buy, the rules move them on their own. You never have to remember who's where.

[SLIDE 2] Start with the welcome lessons. Four emails, one a week, each teaching one step of your method. Not a pitch. A lesson, with one example from your own proof, and one link at the end, your booking link.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Write my four welcome lessons for people who didn't buy" > the reply shows email 1, the roadmap, listing every step of the method, then one email per step with a task for the week and a P.S. teasing the next step, each 250 to 350 words with the booking link at the end]

Ask Funnel OS for your welcome lessons. Email one is the roadmap: it names the problem and lists every step of your method, one line each. The subject line is the outcome, not the event. Then one email per step, with one idea, one small task for the week, and a P.S. that teases the next step.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Build them as a weekly sequence on the long-term nurture tag" > the reply shows the Tantra CRM sequence: four email steps a week apart, asks for approval > type "Yes, start the welcome lessons" > Tantra CRM > Sequences > Welcome lessons > Active]

Then ask it to build them as a sequence on the nurture tag. Approve it in your own words, and it starts. Every new person on the tag gets lesson one, then the next one a week later.

One rule for every message on this tag: teach more than you sell. If every email ends in a hard pitch, people stop opening. If they learn something every week, they'll book when they're ready, and they'll tell you why when they do.

[SLIDE 3] After the four lessons, the weekly letter. One email a week, at the same day and time, every single week. Never move it. People start to expect it.

The topics rotate. The problem restated. One step of your method. A customer story, only with their written yes. A common mistake. A tool or a template. And an invitation back in. Keep about four useful letters for every one that asks for something.

[SCREEN: Content OS > Results > last week's best post by registrations > click "Turn into weekly letter" > Funnel OS drafts the letter from the post: subject, preheader, body under 400 words, one link > and the WhatsApp twin under 400 characters ending with a question and how to stop]

Here's a shortcut. Your best post of the week is often your best letter. In the Content OS, open Results, take last week's top post by registrations, and turn it into this week's letter. It comes out with a subject line, a short body teaching one idea, and one link.

And it comes with its WhatsApp twin. That goes out an hour after the email, only to people who opted in to WhatsApp. Under four hundred characters. Either a one-line pointer to the email, or the email squeezed into a few lines that end with a question. One link, and how to stop, in every message.

[SCREEN: Tantra CRM > Sequences > Weekly letter > schedule: every Tuesday 09:00 buyer time zone > WhatsApp twin step one hour later, audience filter WhatsApp consent = yes > approval typed > Active]

Approve the letter and its twin, and schedule them on your fixed slot.

And keep SMS for time-critical messages only, like a call reminder or the date of the next session. Never text with every letter.

[SLIDE 4] Two cleanups keep the list healthy. First, anyone who hasn't opened twenty emails in a row comes off the list. Every email also has a one-click unsubscribe. A smaller list that reads you is worth more than a big one that doesn't, and it protects your sending domain.

Second, reactivation. For anyone who's been quiet for ninety days, send a nine word email. Their first name as the subject. One plain question. Something like: still looking to book more sales calls this quarter? That's it. Anyone who replies goes back into the welcome lessons, or into the pipeline if they want to talk. Anyone silent after ten sends comes off.

[SCREEN: Tantra CRM > Sequences > Reactivation > trigger: no activity in 90 days > one email, subject first name, nine word body > the removal rule: silent after 10 sends]

Now the most valuable send of all: the invite to your next event. Every new event goes to everyone on the nurture tag, plus your new outreach. Each event makes the next one easier.

That's what happened with FintechCircle, Susanne's community in London. Their outreach grew from ten thousand invites to twenty thousand and forty six, then thirty thousand. By episode four, they sent thirty eight thousand one hundred and thirty eight invites and got a hundred and eighty seven Yes and two hundred and twenty one Maybe RSVPs.

The lesson isn't the size of those numbers. It's that every episode started with everyone the last one reached. The list you're building now is the starting line for every event after this one.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "My next event is on the date I picked. Set it up and re-invite the long-term nurture list" > the reply shows the new event line, the pages to update, the invite campaign to the nurture tag across email, WhatsApp and the weekly letter, and asks for approval]

Give Funnel OS your next event date. It updates your event line and pages, and builds the invite to the nurture list across email, WhatsApp and the weekly letter. Approve it, and your rebooking rate starts to fill: calls booked from long-term nurture, divided by the people on that list.

[SLIDE 5] Now the scale plan. Once a month, five lines. Funnel OS writes it from fetched numbers, never estimates.

[SCREEN: Funnel OS chat > type "Write my monthly scale plan" > the reply shows five lines: 1 cost per buyer per channel against lifetime value, with "not yet" where unknown, 2 the cheapest channel by cost per buyer and how much budget moves to it, 3 the worst step and this week's experiment, 4 the next offer up with its price, 5 the first-win date for buyers]

Line one: cost per buyer against lifetime value. Cost per buyer is spend divided by buyers, per channel and overall. Lifetime value comes from your payment provider. If lifetime value is about three times cost per buyer, you're healthy. If it's below one times, every customer costs you more than they bring back. If you can't fetch it yet, it says not yet.

Line two: your cheapest channel, by cost per buyer. Not by cost per click, not by cost per sign-up. Move budget toward it, and keep a small test budget on every other channel.

Line three: your worst step and this week's experiment, copied from your log so they agree.

Line four: your next offer up. What a happy buyer can buy from you next, and its price.

Line five: the first-win date. What your buyer's first result is, in their words, and by when. Customers leave in the gap between paying and their first result. Close that gap and they stay.

Post those five lines in the group once a month. Your coach reads them with you, and the plan gets sharper each month as more of the not yets become real numbers.

[SLIDE 6] So that's the course. Let me tell you what the next thirty days look like.

Every day, fifteen minutes in your reply inbox, answering every reply with your booking link. Your cold email is running now that your mailboxes are warm. Grow it by adding mailboxes, not by pushing one harder.

Every week, your content batch, your weekly review in Funnel OS, the worst step, and one experiment. You bring your dashboard and your worst step to the group coaching call. Sixty minutes, every week, and we start with your lowest number. And when you want the focus on your build, you book one of the four fifteen minute slots on that call in the Tantra CRM, and we go through your experiment log and next week's change. Post your numbers in the WhatsApp group any day and a coach answers the same day.

Every month, your five line scale plan. Every quarter, a fresh look at your offer and your buyer.

Here's how the month itself tends to run. In week one, your second run starts with a bigger list, a warmer audience and the lessons from your first experiments. In weeks two and three, cold email is fully up to speed, so that's when the channel check matters most. In week four, the scale plan, and the date for the event after that.

I can't promise you a number. Nobody honestly can. What I can promise is that if you run this loop every week, you'll know exactly where your funnel leaks and what to change next. That's how it gets better.

Do this now: put everyone who didn't buy on the long-term nurture tag, start the welcome lessons, set your next event, and post your scale plan in the group. Then bring it all to this week's coaching call. I'll see you there.

Screen recording shot list

  1. Tantra CRM > Contacts filtered to registered non-buyers; bulk add the long-term nurture tag; the two tag rules. Blur every name, email and phone number. About 40 seconds.
  2. Funnel OS chat: four welcome lessons written. About 30 seconds.
  3. Funnel OS chat: build the sequence, approval typed; Tantra CRM > Sequences > Welcome lessons Active. About 30 seconds.
  4. Content OS > Results > best post > Turn into weekly letter; the letter and WhatsApp twin. About 40 seconds.
  5. Tantra CRM > Sequences > Weekly letter on its fixed slot, twin one hour later filtered to WhatsApp consent, approval typed. About 30 seconds.
  6. Tantra CRM > Sequences > Reactivation with the 90 day trigger, nine word email, removal after 10 sends. About 25 seconds.
  7. Funnel OS chat: next event date, the re-invite plan across email, WhatsApp and the letter, approval. About 40 seconds.
  8. Funnel OS chat: monthly scale plan, five lines, one "not yet". Demo numbers only. About 50 seconds.
  9. Tantra CRM booking page for a 15 minute slot on the weekly group call. Blur host email. About 15 seconds.

Funnel Score checklist

  • [ ] Rebooking rate with its counts: calls booked from long-term nurture over the people on that list, or not yet
  • [ ] Long-term nurture tag applied, welcome lessons active and the weekly letter on its fixed slot with its WhatsApp twin
  • [ ] Reactivation email live and your next event invite approved
  • [ ] Your five line scale plan posted in the group before the call
